# Build log: decisions and progress

Running record of what's been built and decided, so any chat in this project can pick up where the last left off.

## Tech stack (decided Oct 18, 2026)
- Python 3.13 (not 3.15: some add-ons lack Windows builds for 3.15), NiceGUI 3.18 for screens, SQLite for saves.
- Desktop app, not a browser game (Blake). `python app.py` opens the game in its own window via NiceGUI native mode (needs the pywebview add-on); without pywebview it falls back to the browser. Later: package as a Windows .exe with a desktop shortcut so no terminal is needed.
- Project folder: C:\Users\bshec\OneDrive\Documents\Blakes New Front Office Football Project
- Claude writes all code; Blake runs, play-tests and makes design calls.
- Copying files to Blake's PC: wait a few seconds after staging, then verify sizes afterward. Copies have twice reported success but left old files in place.
- Claude's workspace can't reach PyPI; NiceGUI and its dependencies were cloned from GitHub for testing. The native window can't be tested there (no display), only browser mode.
- After any change to player generation or the roster template, re-check combine realism AND positional scarcity (a drift went unnoticed once).

## File layout
- `app.py` starts the game
- `engine/` game rules and data only, no screens
  - `positions.py` skills per position, overall weights, body sizes, combine data, positional scarcity
  - `player.py` Player record and generation; talent curve and size trade-off settings
  - `league.py` builds a 32-team league; balance settings at the top; your team; captain vote
  - `teams.py` 32 fictional teams, 53-man roster template
  - `depth.py` depth chart spots, personnel packages, fit at a spot (now/ceiling), AI auto-fill, who's on the field, captain boost
  - `roster_rules.py` 53-man roster limit, 46 game-day actives, minimums per position and group, auto game-day actives
  - `colleges.py` schools and draft rounds weighted by real draft history
  - `database.py` save/load a league file (writes to a temp file and swaps it in, so a crash mid-save can't break a save); quick save info for the Load list; rename; older save files load with defaults
  - `schedule.py` builds a season's whole schedule at once (NFL formula, weeks, byes); week dates
  - `season.py` the regular season: play a week, record results/box scores, injuries week to week, standings
  - `stats.py` season stats from stored box scores (regular season and playoffs apart; preseason never counts), team totals, league leaders with NFL minimums
  - `playoffs.py` NFL tiebreakers, 14-team seeding, the bracket (re-seeding), the Glory Bowl (name, numerals, rotating neutral site)
  - `saves.py` save slots (one .db file per game in `saves/`): list, new file names, copy, delete, recovery copies; game settings in `saves/settings.json` (autosave, last opened)
  - `formatting.py` fan-friendly display (6-2, 4.52 sec, "Very Good (87)")
  - `trades.py` player trades: pick ownership and values, what each team thinks a player is worth, deals, AI trades, your calls (Oct 23; `docs/trade-design.md`); `news_trades.py` their news and Bitter bits
  - `names.py` names, hometowns
  - `sim/` the game sim (see Game sim section): `game.py`, `plays.py`, `coach.py`, `gameteam.py`, `penalties.py`, `stats.py`, `text.py`, `weather.py`, `norms.py`, `data.py` (real NFL tables), `tuning.py` (all knobs)
- `screens/` the interface (reads from engine only)
  - `state.py` shared screen state: open/create/save games, unsaved-changes flag (`mark_changed()` after any change), recovery copy on close
  - `menu_page.py` main menu, Load game, Settings
  - `new_game_page.py` New game (league settings, then choose your team)
  - `schedule_page.py` Schedule tab (league week / team season, stored box scores)
  - `standings_page.py` Standings tab (seed column once the playoffs are set)
  - `stats_page.py` Stats tab (league leaders, player stats by category, team stats; regular season / playoffs) and the Season stats section of the player report
  - `playoffs_page.py` Playoffs tab (seeds and bracket by conference, Glory Bowl)
  - `main_page.py` game screen: header band (save status, Save, Save a copy, Main menu), team picker, choose-your-team prompt, tabs
  - `trade_page.py` Trades tab (Team section): make a trade, offers to you, shop a player, trade log (Oct 23)
  - `roster_page.py` Roster tab
  - `depth_page.py` Depth chart tab
  - `counts_page.py` Position counts tab
  - `formations.py` SVG field diagrams for each package
  - `game_page.py` Game tab: watch a game snap by snap (scoreboard, field strip, play-by-play, box scores)
  - `theme.py` colors (rating tiers, one per position), fonts, shared styles
- `stepN_*.py` temporary text test screens from early build steps (step3 predates LS and new colors; not maintained)
- `CLAUDE.md` instructions Claude Code reads automatically (project, Blake, how to work, sim care)
- `docs/build-log.md` this record (the copy to keep updated from Step 7 on)
- `tools/` realism tools: `realism_check.py`, `team_sport_test.py`, `consistency_test.py`, `recenter.py`, `build_benchmarks.py`, shared `measures.py`/`simlab.py`, real NFL data in `tools/data`, targets in `real_nfl_benchmarks.json` (see `tools/README.md`)
- `step6_season_test.py` plays a test season with the sim and compares stats to the real NFL: league averages, a "how common are big seasons" table (4,000-yard passers, 120-tackle players, 10-sack players... vs real 2021-25 average and range), leaderboards for every major stat, and injury games missed (changes nothing)

## Decisions
- Name (Blake, Oct 22): **Glory Bowl GM**, at least for now ("bizarre enough to get people to take a second look"). It ties to the league's championship, the Glory Bowl, and avoids the "Front Office Football" and "NFL" names in case of a Steam release (check Steam and trademarks before that). Shown in the window title, main menu (GLORY BOWL / GM), offseason and player card headers, and setup check. Packaged builds save in Documents\Glory Bowl GM\saves, but they keep using an existing Documents\Front Office Football\saves folder so earlier copies don't lose saves. The project folder and code comments keep the old name.
- Ratings 0-100 with current and potential per skill. Overall = weighted average of position skills.
- Hidden athletic traits (speed, strength, agility, explosion, intelligence) drive both skills and combine results, so the combine predicts ratings well but not perfectly. Traits are built from talent relative to the position (including star stretch), luck, and size, then rescaled to a spread of 15, so combine results stay within ~6% of real NFL averages and spreads (re-verified Oct 18 after a 10-17% drift from the scarcity tuning was found and fixed).
- Size trade-offs (Blake): players can be light or heavy for their own position, and it matters. `player.SIZE_TRADEOFF`: per spread of extra weight, +6 strength, -5 speed, -4 agility, -1 explosion. Heavy = better run defense/blocking/power; light = quicker pursuit/rush/coverage; overall roughly unchanged (a trade-off, not a penalty). Example DE: heavy run D 49 / pursuit 44 / 40 time 4.82; light run D 45 / pursuit 52 / 4.68. Shown quietly: gray italic "light for a DE" / "heavy for a DE" next to size, only for clearly light/heavy players (~16%).
- Hidden ratings scouts never see: interception avoidance (QB), fumble avoidance (ball carriers). Never shown on screens.
- Skill rating words and colors (Blake): Elite 90+ blue ("blue chip"), Very Good 80-89 green, Good 65-79 light green, Average 45-64 yellow, Bad 30-44 orange, Very Bad 15-29 brown, Atrocious <15 dark brown. Since Oct 21 these apply to the shown scale (median NFL player = 50; `engine/scale.py`), not the engine's internal numbers.
- Personality traits use neutral words (Extremely High ... Extremely Low), same cutoffs, because high isn't always good (volatility).
- Combine: 40, bench, vertical, broad, 3-cone, 20-yard shuttle, intelligence test, position drill. Averages, spreads and drill participation rates come from real NFL combine data 2010-2026 (nflverse dataset, drafted players); long snappers from 32 invitees 2006-2023 (rougher). Players skip drills at real rates ("Did not test").
- Positional scarcity (Blake: realistic at every position, QB the scarcest). `positions.TALENT_SHIFT` (overall talent level) and `positions.TALENT_SPREAD_MULT` (gap between best and worst; below 1.0 = replaceable) per position, tuned by search (re-run Oct 18 after the trait/size rework; talent star stretch raised 6 -> 9) to hit targets per league: QB 2 Elite / 6 rated 80+ / starters ~70 / best FA ~43; RB 1/6/72/55 (replaceable); FB 0/2/62/54; TE 1/4/68/47; WR 4/18/70/50; T 2/10/68/46; G 1/8/67/52; C 1/4/68/50; DE 2/10/70/46; DT 1-2/8/68/48; OLB 1-2/8/68/49; ILB 1/5/70/52 (replaceable); CB 2/10/70/47; S 1-2/9/68/50; K 4/14/76/65; P 3/12/74/65; LS 3/12/74/65. Targets are judgment calls (no clean public dataset of player quality by position); the table and reasoning live in positions.py. Result: 80+ counts, starter averages and best free agents on target (LS 80+ a bit under at ~10); elite (90+) tackles, edge rushers and corners a bit under (about 1 each per league), with Generational players adding more. League-wide mix: ~1.4% Elite, 7.8% Very Good, 21% Good, 64% Average, 5.5% Bad; average age 26.8. Applies to every player ever generated, including future draft classes.
- QB AI rule (future): treat QBs as precious (never release good QBs cheaply, price them high in free agency and trades) without making the "draft a QB over a good young starter" mistake.
- Generational players (Blake's idea; renamed from X-factor to avoid confusion with FOF8's X-factor): about 1 in 400 roster players, 3-7 per league. Hidden. Key skills top out at 90-99, high hidden consistency, steady development (low volatility). Max 1 Generational FB, K, P and LS each in the league at a time (Blake); `League.generational_allowed()` must be checked by future draft classes too. Planned: longer peak, fewer bad games, scouts/interviews may hint, and AI must value players by production so Generational players can't be exploited cheaply.
- Every player has a hidden consistency rating (how reliably he plays to his ratings).
- Draft history and colleges: every player has draft year and round (or undrafted). Rounds are assigned per position using real 2012-2026 draft data: each position's drafted share and round split (K, P, LS and FB never go in rounds 1-2; QBs go in round 1 most often; LS only rounds 5-7, ~15% drafted), weighted toward early rounds because early picks last longer. League totals land near real roster shares (Over The Cap 2023: Rd1 14.1%, Rd2 12.4%, Rd3 13.2%, Rd4 10.5%, Rd5 10.1%, Rd6 8.6%, Rd7 6.0%, UDFA 24.9%; game ~12/11/14/11/10/9/6/27). Colleges are picked by round from real draft counts per school: ~2.6% of first-rounders from small schools (real 2.5%), 70% from the top 25 first-round schools (real 70%). Drafted share per position and undrafted college weights are estimates.
- League: 2 conferences (Eastern, Western), 4 divisions each. Roster: QB3 RB3 FB1 TE3 WR6 T4 G3 C2 DE5 DT4 OLB4 ILB3 CB5 S4 K1 P1 LS1 = 53. Long snapper added Oct 18 (Blake); a cornerback was cut to make room. LS skills: Snap Accuracy, Snap Velocity, Coverage Tackling; body 6-2, 242 lbs; depth chart spot LS.
- Roster rules (Blake, modeled on FOF8's position distribution; numbers are this game's own): up to 53 on the roster, 46 active on game day. Game-day minimums per position and group: QB 2, RB 2, FB 0, backfield 3, TE 2, WR 4, skill positions 10, C 1, G 2, T 2, OL 7, DE 3, DT 2, DL 6, OLB 2, ILB 1, LB 4, defensive front 11, CB 4, S 2, secondary 7, K 1, P 1, LS 1; total 46. Actives are chosen automatically for now (least-needed backups sit, never breaking a minimum or benching a starter); later Blake picks his own inactives and injured players always sit. Every generated team meets all minimums. These rules must also govern AI signings/cuts and free agency. Shown in the Position counts tab (Active / Required / On roster, group rows bold, shortfalls flagged).
- Your team: `League.user_team_id`. You run one team; the other 31 are AI. Chosen in the New league dialog, or by a one-time prompt for a league without one. Only your team's depth chart can be edited; other teams are view-only. Header shows a "Your team" badge. (Preview of the planned main-menu team choice.)
- Depth charts (Step 5, replaces FOF8's per-grouping participation charts with something simpler): one ordered list per spot (QB, RB, FB, WR1-4, TE1-3, LT/LG/C/RG/RT; LE, LDT, RDT, RE, WLB, MLB, SLB, CB1, CB2, NB, DB, FS, SS; K, P, LS, KR, PR), starter + up to 3 backups. Packages pull from spots: 11/12/21/13/22/10 personnel on offense; Base 4-3, Nickel, Dime on defense. If a player is already on the field (or unavailable later, e.g. injured), the game walks down that spot's list. Depth chart tab is split by unit (Offense / Defense / Special teams), each with a field diagram of the chosen package (seen from behind your own unit) and only that unit's spots. AI auto-fill arranges each unit to maximize total weighted strength (spot importance: QB 3.0, LT 1.3, RE 1.3, CB1 1.2...) with swap search, picks returners from non-starters when possible, then fills backups from bench players. Warnings show for empty spots and a player listed as starter at two spots. Base defense is 4-3 for now; a 3-4 option can come with schemes. Per-package overrides possible later if Blake wants FOF8-style control. 3rd-down back (Blake, Oct 21: a depth chart spot AND AI teams pick one; "the main drivers for what makes a good 3rd down back is blocking and receiving"): spot "RB3" on the Offense list, rated on pass blocking 3, hands 3, route running 3, elusiveness 1 (`depth.SPOT_SKILLS`); in games he plays running back in passing situations (3rd/4th and 4+, 2nd and 8+, the two-minute drill, two scores down in the 4th), 85% of those snaps (`tuning.THIRD_DOWN_BACK_SHARE`). AI: the best backup at the job takes it if he's within 4 points of the starter (staffs keep the lead back fresh; `depth.THIRD_DOWN_BACKUP_WITHIN`), otherwise the starter keeps it; about 29% of AI teams use a different back. If the starter is his own 3rd-down back, nothing changes.
- Playing out of position (`depth.spot_fit`): rating "now" and "ceiling" at any spot. Uses his skills for the spot's position; untrained skills estimated from athletic traits plus little technique now, more at his ceiling. New position: -6 now (shrinks as he learns it), up to -4 ceiling depending on hidden adaptability. Offensive linemen have a natural side (45% L, 45% R, 10% both); switching sides (Blake: like switching hands) -5 now, up to -2 ceiling. At his own position, size is built into his ratings (see size trade-offs); at a new position, being too light/heavy for it costs extra (shown as a subtle gray label, no warning tag). `Player.learned` tracks reps at other spots/sides (0-100) and will grow with practice and games in the season loop. Screens explain penalties in words ("Switching sides of the line").
- Captains: one offense, one defense per team, voted on by the players (Blake: the GM can't pick them). Vote mostly leadership, plus experience and popularity, with some unpredictability; starters preferred. Held when the league is created; later each preseason, and again if a captain leaves. Captains keep the job for the whole season. Oct 20 (Blake): extra votes for the starting QB (real teams almost always make him a captain), last season's captain (usually re-elected) and Generational players (teammates' respect), so a Generational QB with good leadership is captain nearly every year (`league.CAPTAIN_QB_BONUS` 14, `CAPTAIN_RETURNING_BONUS` 5, `CAPTAIN_GENERATIONAL_BONUS` 6). Measured over 128 teams (saved,101,102,103; 200 votes each): starting QB is offensive captain on 27% of teams (was 11%); by QB leadership: under 40 1%, 40-54 12%, 55-69 44%, 70-79 70%, 80+ 90% (was 0/1/14/28/62%). If the QB had 70+ leadership and were Generational: 90% on average (lowest 42%, a young QB against a respected veteran). Re-vote the next season with the same roster: captains re-elected 87-89% (93% for a captain QB); real roster changes will lower that. The overall QB rate is limited because leadership is generated the same for every position, so 46 of 128 starting QBs have leadership under 40. Blake (Oct 20): keep leadership generated the same for every position; the vote bonuses are enough. `depth.captain_boost`: nothing for leadership 50 or below, up to +1 rating point to his unit for the best leaders (to use in the sim). Captains ★ (gold), starters ● (green), with a key on both tabs.
- Team parity (applies to the random generated rosters start option only): starting teams are fairly even. Blake agrees the variety should come over time from player development (breakouts and fall-offs), team building, drafting, free agency, injuries and seasons going sideways (Step 7 check: team strength spread should grow from ~4 toward the real ~6 points per game over several simmed seasons), not from the initial generation. Instant history (sim seasons before taking over) is an option later. (Oct 19: `league.ORG_STRENGTH` dial added, default 0.0 = this decision; 0.4 would give an NFL-like spread of team strength from day one. Kept off pending Blake.) (Oct 20 clarification, Blake: the main way to start a league is the startup draft, where good and bad drafts make uneven teams; historical rosters are uneven like the real NFL. Sim tests so far used even generated leagues; rerun the realism tools on drafted leagues once the draft exists.)
- Look: "field" theme. Deep turf green header band with faint yard lines, off-white page, penalty-flag yellow for the selected row and keyboard focus, Barlow / Barlow Condensed fonts. Rating tiers colored as above. Each position has its own color (Blake), grouped by family: QB orange, RB/FB reds, TE mauve, WR purple, OL blues, DL navies, LB greens, CB light yellow, S darker gold, K/P/LS/returners browns-grays; tag text color picked for readable contrast (all 4:1 or better). Used on the roster, depth chart, field diagrams and position counts. One main view with tabs and side panels, no stacked pop-ups. Blake: the look can be improved later; not a priority now.
- Team picker lists Free agents first (Blake).

## Player development (design agreed Oct 19, build in Step 7) - a core reason Blake loves FOF8
- NOT Madden-style steady climbs to potential. The drama is in POTENTIAL shifting; current-rating growth is fairly consistent.
- Each training camp, every player's potential is re-checked. No fixed percentages (Blake): each player's odds come from his own factors, and league-wide rates emerge from them. Factors: volatility (biggest), age (young swing more and lean up; veterans less and lean down), position coaching, serious injuries (can permanently cut potential, per the design doc), Generational status (steady, almost never collapses). Tune so a typical season has ~88-90% of players barely moving (±0-3) and big swings rare; check actual rates after simming several seasons.
- Two layers of potential (Blake: a player can fall off one season, then return to his old level the next): (1) a long-term ceiling that drifts by small continuous amounts and occasionally takes a lasting jump or drop; (2) a season-to-season swing on top that fades gradually (part carries into next year, part wears off, with randomness in how much). Both layers move continuously every season; there is NO yes/no check deciding "permanent" vs "comes back" (Blake: must feel natural, not like a coded switch). Outcomes span a spectrum: full bounce-back, partial recovery, stays down, keeps sliding, occasionally comes back better. Age, playing time and coaching nudge the direction without guaranteeing it. Before locking numbers, sim several seasons and show Blake real player histories to judge whether it reads like a player's story.
- Normal aging decline is separate (position peak windows from the design doc, e.g. QB seasons 5-13, RB 3-9). Some shifts hit specific skills, not just the whole player.
- Coaches matter (Blake): a coach who specializes in the player's position tilts shifts upward and makes collapses less likely. Hooks into the staff system (design still open; Blake wants an alternative to FOF8's staff draft).
- If potential drops below current, current drops with it, roughly scaled (not necessarily point for point) - a real fall off a cliff (Blake: option A).
- Current ratings: each season move a steady share of the way toward potential, with small noise.
- When you find out (Blake): the shift happens at camp, but what you SEE updates in stages as the staff learns: some after training camp, MOST after the preseason games, smaller shifts during the season that often settle back near where the player started.
- Rust (Blake, FOF8 had it): players who haven't played lose a few points, regained over a few games of reps. Covers preseason and returns from injury.
- Screens will show the staff's EVALUATION of ratings, not true ratings (Blake agreed), so reveals can happen in stages. Ties into interview-based draft scouting. Today's screens show true ratings; switch when development/scouting are built.

## Game sim (Step 6, built Oct 19, 2026) - Blake: "make or break"; must feel like the modern NFL and never pre-determined
- Files: `engine/sim/` (rules only, no screens): `game.py` (downs, clock, possessions, scoring, halftime, OT, late-game clock management), `plays.py` (one play from the matchups on the field), `coach.py` (play calls, personnel, defense, 4th downs, two-point tries, tempo), `gameteam.py` (who's active and on the field, rotation, fatigue, form, reading ratings), `penalties.py`, `stats.py` (box score), `text.py` (play-by-play), `weather.py`, `norms.py`, `data.py` (real NFL tables), `tuning.py` (every knob). Screen: `screens/game_page.py` (Game tab). Test: `step6_season_test.py` (plays a 17-game season, prints league averages vs the NFL, standings, leaders; changes nothing).
- How a play works: every play is built from individual matchups (each pass rusher vs the man blocking him; each receiver vs the defender covering him; blockers vs defenders at the hole; the runner vs the second level and safeties). Matchups tilt real NFL outcome distributions (2023-2025 play-by-play, nflverse); randomness comes from those real distributions, so a better team usually wins the play but not always. Nothing is decided ahead of time.
- Ratings are read as z-scores against the league's average starter at that spot, with a soft cap (Z_CAP 2.3) so one player can't take over every snap. Each matchup adjustment is "centered" on its league-wide average (tuning.CENTER, measured over 1,500 games in 3 leagues) so an average matchup reproduces the real NFL baseline exactly. If ratings or knobs change a lot, the centers need re-measuring.
- Tilt math fix found during calibration: the first version multiplied big-play odds exponentially (a modest edge tripled 30-yard runs). Now tilts are in log-odds: an edge of m multiplies big-play odds by about e^m.
- Play calling from real data: run/pass by down, distance, field zone and score/time (logistic model) plus each team's lean; 4th-down go/punt/kick table from real neutral situations with late-game logic; two-point table; 2025 kickoff rules (touchback to the 35); 2025 regular-season OT (10 minutes, both teams possess, ties possible).
- Clock: real runoff between snaps (37.5 s after an in-bounds run), out-of-bounds rules (restart on the ready signal except the last 2:00 of the half and the last 5:00 of the game), two-minute warning, timeouts (late-game, end of half, plus occasional ones), kneel-downs that know how much time they can burn, spikes, Hail Marys, and clock-aware field goals (teams kick or burn a timeout instead of letting time expire in range).
- Penalties at real per-play rates by type (20+ types), accepted or declined by expected points; DPI is a spot foul called on incompletions.
- Rotation: backups get planned snaps (RB committee, WR3, DL rotation...), more when they're close in talent; starters stay in on goal-line/short-yardage snaps. Fatigue subs on top of that.
- Red zone: the field "squeezes" near the goal line for runs, completions and yards after catch, matched to real TD rates from each yard line.
- In-game injuries (placeholder until Step 7): some players return the same game, others miss 1-17 weeks (about 1 in 10 is long-term, often the rest of the season). The season test carries them week to week. Since Oct 19 (stat realism pass): about 30 injuries per team cost at least a game and teams lose about 100-110 player-games a season (real NFL 2024: about 95-250 per team). Ball carriers get hurt less per touch than before, and one random player per side (often a lineman) is in each pile, so injuries are spread across positions instead of piling on running backs. Quarterbacks get hurt less often than other players because the rules protect them.
- Calibration result (2,000 sim games vs real 2023-25, per team per game): points 22.7 (22.6), points sd 9.8 (9.9), average margin 11.1 (11.2), home win 56% (54%), plays 61.8 (62.4), comp% 64.3 (64.4), YPA 7.07 (7.02), TD rate 4.5% (4.4%), INT rate 2.3% (2.2%), sack rate 6.4% (6.5%), YPC 4.41 (4.48), 3rd down 39.0% (39.3%), turnovers 1.26 (1.24), penalties 5.8 for 52 yds (6.2 for 50), FGA 2.2 (2.0), drive results within 1-2 points of real shares. Player-season spreads match (QB comp% sd 3.1 vs 3.2, YPA sd 0.72 vs 0.78, sack% sd 2.5 vs 2.1, RB YPC sd 0.66 vs 0.57, WR catch% sd 6.5 vs 6.9). Leaders match (passing yards #1 ~4,770 vs 4,750; rushing #1 ~1,640 vs 1,695; receiving #1 ~1,800 vs 1,767; receptions #1 ~123 vs 130; sacks #1 19 vs 19; INT #1 7 vs 8; tackles #1 ~170 vs 150).
- Known gaps: drives per team ~5% high (11.5 vs 11.0); ties slightly more common than real (~0.3% vs 0.1%); 4th-down conversion 48% vs 54%; RB carry shares measured without injuries are top-heavy (64/31/5 vs real season 54/24/9; the season test with injuries spreads them more). Team strength spread at league start is narrower than the NFL's (true sd ~4.5 vs ~6 points per game) because starting rosters are fairly even, per Blake's parity decision; `league.ORG_STRENGTH` (default 0.0) is a dial that would widen it from day one (0.4 gave ~6).
- Revision after Blake's first season test (Oct 19): he saw two 2,000-yard receivers and leaderboards that felt "scaled" rather than random. My first leader check had been flawed (it compared pooled sim seasons in a way that understated the top of the leaderboard), so a new test plays full seasons (schedule + injuries) in several leagues, including Blake's own, and compares every leaderboard rank and threshold count to real 2021-24 seasons. Fixes: coordinator styles now persist all season (they had been re-rolled every game, which made every team look alike); defenses roll coverage to the most dangerous receiver or worst mismatch, more on passing downs, near the goal line and against a receiver who's hot that game; stars are targeted by design, so their volume comes with contested throws instead of extra efficiency; the ball spreads around once one man has been heavily fed in a game; diminishing returns on stacked edges in a single play (completion, YAC, runs); chips on star pass rushers; clean-up sacks spread sack credit; tackles weighted by tackling skill; running back usage varies by team (workhorse vs committee); red-zone targets lean to tight ends; QBs can be hurt on sacks and hits; injuries about doubled (about 22 players per team miss a game per season); pass/run balance and team pass volume matched to real (565 attempts per team, sd 49). Result over 12 sim seasons: no 2,000-yard receivers (max 1,879; real max 1,947); receiving yards by rank match real from #3 to #32 within ~3%; receptions, rushing by rank, carries, RB1 share (0.59, sd 0.12 vs 0.59/0.14), tackles and sacks match. Remaining gaps: fewer 4,000-yard passers than 2021-24 (about 5 vs 9; 2021-22 were higher-passing years than the 2023-25 calibration era), top rushing TD totals a bit low (about 16 vs 17), a few too many 10-TD receivers (9 vs 7).
- Stat realism pass (Oct 19, Blake: "a lot of the stats seem inflated, tackles included... needs to be as realistic as possible"). Method: every real player-season 2015-2025 (nflverse player stats incl. defense, kicking, punting; plus 2023-25 snap counts) was turned into "how common is each stat line" targets: leaderboard values at ranks #1/#3/#5/#10/#20/#30/#50 and counts of players past thresholds (e.g. 120+ tackles, 10+ sacks, 100+ catches, TE 1,000 yards, RB 60 catches), by position group, plus each team's stat split by rank (its top tackler, 2nd, 3rd...). The same measures are computed from 12-16 sim seasons in 4 leagues including Blake's. Main findings and fixes:
  - Tackles: real combined tackles = solo + all assisted credits (my first comparison undercounted real assists). Special teams tackles count in totals (NFL convention) and go mostly to backup LBs and DBs, sometimes offensive backups. Who makes the tackle now depends on how far the runner got (`tuning.TACKLE_POOL`): linemen near the line, linebackers on short and medium gains, safeties and corners downfield; assisted tackles are common in traffic and rare in the open field (real DBs make more solo tackles). Result: tackle leaders ~186 (real 183), position shares DL 21% / LB 35% / DB 41% (real 21/35/42).
  - Sacks: DTs had 41% of all sacks (real ~20%). Inside rushers now win less (`RUSH_INTERIOR`), clean-up sacks and second-man QB hits go to edge rushers and blitzers more than tackles, defensive line rotation is deeper, and safeties/slot corners blitz more. Shared sacks give no tackle for loss (NFL rule).
  - Pass defense: pass breakups were too rare and only went to DBs; now real volume (4.1 vs 4.3 per team game) with ~12% batted at the line by rushers. Some interceptions go to a different defender (tipped balls, overthrows to a safety).
  - Forced fumbles: every fumble used to credit a forced fumble; real is about 2 in 3. Strip sacks slightly rarer.
  - Bug fixed: when tight ends or receivers were tired or hurt, the game subbed in linebackers and defensive ends from the depth chart's backup lists (~500 targets a season to defenders league-wide). Rotation and fatigue subs now stay on the same side of the ball, the emergency fill prefers a same-side player, and the AI depth chart (`depth.py`) fills offensive backups with offensive players and defensive backups with defensive players. A chart starter Blake places himself (even a two-way player) is still respected.
  - Receivers: tight end volume was too concentrated in TE1; TE2 now gets real 12-personnel targets. High-volume receivers were too efficient (9.1 yards per target vs real 8.2 for 140+ target players), because talent drove both targets and efficiency. Now targets are more by design (role), the pull from openness + talent has diminishing returns (`TARGET_EDGE_CAP`), a receiver fed heavily in a game draws tighter coverage (`FED_COVER_K`), deep-threat routes were trimmed for WR1s, and per-play completion/YAC edges are capped tighter. Extreme stacked offenses (max pass lean + fast pace + featured WR) are trimmed (`PASS_BIAS_MAX` 0.36). Result over 28 sim seasons in the last two checks: no 2,000-yard receivers in the final 12 (max 1,914; real all-time max 1,964); yards per target max 11.1 (real 11.0).
  - Team pace style added (`STYLE_PACE_SD`): fast and slow offenses run different numbers of plays. Receivers can run jet sweeps/end-arounds (`WR_RUN_SHARE`). Fewer designed QB runs, so backs get the real share of carries; a floor on the RB split so even workhorses rest.
  - Kicking and punting: FG% level and spread (85%, real 85.4%), punt gross 47.2 (47.1), punt returns now count 0-yard returns (average was inflated).
  - Red zone loosened slightly (TD rate was a bit low, FGs a bit high).
  - League-level calibration re-checked after all changes (800 games): points 22.6 (22.6), YPA 7.2 (7.0), comp% 65.4 (64.4), sack rate 6.4% (6.5%), YPC 4.5 (4.5), turnovers 1.20 (1.24), FG% 85.2 (85.2), punts 3.8 (3.8).
  - Team sport check (Blake: an average QB on a stacked roster should put up good numbers, like FOF8). Test: move the league's best (or worst) starter at every offensive spot onto one team, keep the defense, drop in the same QB or RB, 160 games vs the same opponents. Below-average QB (64; average starter 70): rating 97 and 9.7 wins with a stacked cast, 93 and 6.8 with an average cast, 71 and 1.6 with a weak cast. Best QB (93): 117 / 12.6 wins stacked, 93 / 7.0 wins weak. Below-average RB: 4.52 YPC stacked line vs 3.66 weak. The cast moves a player's numbers about as much as his own rating. Sacks behind the worst possible line were 89 per 17 games (record 76); now a terrible blocker's effect has diminishing returns (`RUSH_EDGE_CAP`, blocker side only so a rusher's own talent still counts fully): 74. Keep this test in the standard checks.
  - Randomness check (Blake: stats must not feel like a formula). Same rosters replayed with frozen ratings: the same player's seasons vary a lot (e.g. WR 93: 1,119 / 1,016 / 1,531; DE 86: 18 / 2 / 9 sacks) and the highest-rated player at a position rarely leads the league. Fair comparison of season-to-season change for established full-time players (median % change) matches the real NFL: WR targets 12% (real 12%), WR yards 16% (19%), RB carries 15% (14%), QB yards 7% (7%), edge sacks 31% (33%), tackles 13% (13%), RB YPC 0.45 (0.39), WR yards/target 0.99 (0.93); QB YPA moves less (0.42 vs 0.63) because rosters were frozen. (An earlier correlation-based check suggested volume was too noisy; that was mostly a measuring error from comparing a narrow group of sim starters with a wide group of real players.)
  - Injuries rebalanced with it: running backs were missing ~6 games a year (RB1s hogged snaps in games, then got hurt). Now backs share more snaps within games (`ROTATION['RB']` 0.25, split floor 0.25) and get hurt less per touch (`INJURY_CARRIER_MULT` RB 0.35, others 0.55); long-term injuries are 9% of injuries. About 95-100 player-games lost per team.
  - Durability, RB endurance, bell cows (Blake, Oct 19). Every player has a hidden Durability rating (`player.fill_new_ratings`, seeded by player id so older saves get the same values on load; saved in the `durability` column). Average 50, spread 14; size matters both ways (Blake): heavier than normal for his position is +3 per spread of weight on average, and the spread widens by 3 per spread of weight away from typical, so big men are more often iron men or fragile; Generational players lean durable (72, spread 12; not guaranteed). Effect: injury odds x e^(-0.35 per 15 points) (iron man ~half, fragile ~double) and durable players more often shake injuries off or miss less time; worn-down (over fatigue limit) players get hurt a bit more. Result over 16 seasons: starters with durability under 30 average ~545 snaps a season vs ~715 for 70+. Running backs have a visible Endurance rating (not in overall; stronger and thicker backs tend to have more): it sets how long he goes before tiring, and the staff builds the backfield around it: high endurance and clearly better than the backup = bell cow, similar backs or low endurance = committee (`RB_ENDURANCE_SPLIT_K`, `RB_GAP_SPLIT_K`, style still varies). In-game workload: after ~16 carries (later for high endurance) the backup gets more work (`RB_CARRIES_SOFT/STEP`). Starting RBs' carries by endurance: under 40 about 170, 70+ about 260 (median). Bell cows (300+ carries) 3.4 a season (real 3.0), max ~385 (real record 416). Goal-line backs and QB keepers at the 1-3 bring the QB share of rushing TDs to the real ~20%.
  - Generational players get more headroom at the top (`Z_CAP_GENERATIONAL` 2.7 vs 2.3; Blake chose "a little"). On a bad roster (26th-best starter at every spot), an average starting QB wins ~4 of 17, the best QB in the league (93) ~8, a generational QB (96) ~8.6: great QBs make bad teams competitive. The 93 in that test is already near-perfect in the skills the sim reads most, so the generational edge there is small; their bigger edges are steadiness, durability and (Step 7) longevity.
  - Tackle leaders (Blake saw a 211-tackle season): over 28 sim seasons the leader ranged 163-214 with 5 seasons of 200+, while real 2015-25 never topped 192 (range 173-192). Cause: tackling skill concentrated too many stops on the best tacklers. `TACKLE_SKILL_K` 0.30 -> 0.20: leader now 169-198 over 16 seasons, average 183 (real 183). Checked at the same time: RB receiving matches real by rank (catch leader avg ~85-89 vs 87, #10 ~50 vs 49, 60+ catch backs 4.5-5.6 vs 4.6, receiving yards #1 ~690 vs 699); 5,000-yard passing is not capped anywhere: 8 of 28 sim seasons had one (real 2021-25: 2 of 5 seasons).
  - Solo vs assisted tackles match real 2021-25: solo share 53.5% (real 54.0%); DL 47% (44%), LB 51% (50%), DB 59% (63%); 120+ tacklers 51% (50%). Injury totals: the league average per team stays near 100-105 every season by nature (an average over 32 teams); team to team it ranges about 50-160 (real 2024: 95-254). The real level is higher partly because real totals include training camp/preseason injuries and season-long IR stints from before week 1. To add in Step 7: camp and preseason injuries (then recheck stat calibration). The season report now shows the healthiest and most injured team.
  - Remaining gaps (Oct 19): fewer 4,500-yard passers (about 1.5 vs 3.8) and 100-catch receivers (about 6 vs 10), and receiving leaders vary more season to season than real (about 1,500-1,900 vs 1,700-1,950), since the generated leagues sometimes lack an elite QB-WR pairing. A few too many 10-12 sack edge rushers. The top workhorse back gets ~370 carries (real max ~350). Kicker and punter leaderboards below the top run high because sim teams keep one kicker all year, while real teams churn kickers; team-level FG attempts match. Real teams use ~46 defenders a season (roster churn) vs ~28 in the sim, so mid-pack tacklers run a bit high until Step 7 adds signings and cuts.
- Game screen (Game tab): pick away/home teams (exhibition, results don't count yet), scoreboard (score, quarter, clock, down & distance, timeouts, possession, weather), field strip (ball, line of scrimmage blue, first-down line yellow), scrolling play-by-play in NFL game-book style grouped by drive, with a gray detail line under each play saying what happened on the field (personnel, formation, coverage, blitz, who beat whom, open or covered), scoring plays green, turnovers red, big plays yellow; speeds Slow / Normal / Fast / Very fast, Pause, Next play, Sim to the end. Side tabs: team stats with line score, both box scores (passing, rushing, receiving, defense, kicking, punting, returns), scoring summary, drive chart.
- Coaches don't exist yet: team tendencies (pass lean, 4th-down aggression, man/zone, blitz rate, outside runs, heavy personnel) come from the roster plus a random coordinator style. Coaches will plug in here later.

## Planned game flow (Blake, Oct 18)
- Launch -> main menu: New game, Load game, Settings.
- New game: choose league settings, pick the team you'll be GM of (you control that one team; the other 31 are AI-run competitors), then how rosters are filled:
  1. Startup draft: all players pooled, every team drafts a full roster (53 rounds); Blake can draft himself or auto-draft.
  2. Random generated rosters (what exists now).
  3. Legacy rosters: real NFL rosters from a chosen season (going back to about 2000) with estimated ratings.
- Load game: multiple save slots in the saves folder.
- Season stages include training camp and preseason games as separate sim steps (needed for the development reveals above).
- Legacy rosters: nflverse has full season rosters back to 2000 (name, team, position, height, weight, birth date, college often blank in early years) and weekly player stats back to 1999. Ratings estimated from stats, draft position, starts and career data. Blake: where estimates are unsure (offensive linemen have no individual stats), keep them balanced realistically. Plan: rank players within each position by the best evidence available, then map ranks onto the same realistic per-position distribution as generated leagues (the scarcity table above), so uncertain ratings land at believable levels instead of extremes. Real names/teams are fine for personal, never-sold use (as FOF8's community roster files were). Build after the core season loop works.

## Later ideas Blake approved
- Arm length and hand size (need a solid data source first), pro days, combine injuries. Not interested in the 10-yard split.
- Choosing your own game-day inactives (with the season loop).
- Stadiums and facilities (Blake, Oct 23, "eventually"): build new stadiums and upgrade facilities inside the stadium. Not designed yet. To decide with him when it comes up: which upgrades exist (seating, luxury suites, concessions, field/roof, practice and training facilities), what they affect (revenue and the salary cap budget, attendance and fan mood, home-field edge, player development and injury recovery, free agents' interest), how they're paid for (team money, public financing, loans), and how long construction takes. Needs team finances first (revenue, attendance), which don't exist yet. FOF8 had stadium upgrades and new-stadium projects in its finances, so it's a natural fit.

- Oct 19 (Claude Code, first session on Blake's PC): `python tools/realism_check.py` (default, 8 seasons: saved,101,102,103 x 2) ran clean in 170 s on Python 3.13.16. This is the first `tools/realism_report.txt` on the PC (the baseline for future comparisons). League averages within ~3% of real except fumbles lost +12% and penalty yards +7%. 20 of 282 measures outside the real range (9 have known reasons; the rest match the known gaps: few 4,500-yard passers, top back's carries 363 vs ~326, receptions leader 121 vs 133, TE #5 yards low). Watch item: sack leader ranged 13-30 across 8 seasons (real 18-23, average right at 20.5); recheck on a 16-season run before changing anything.
- Oct 19 sack tuning (Blake asked to fine-tune sacks before Step 7). The 16-season baseline showed the 8-season "30-sack" worry was noise, and the real problem was the opposite: sacks spread too evenly. Leader 14-22, average 17.3 (real 18-23, 20.1); too many 5+ sack players (97 vs 87), DTs with 6+ (10.7 vs 7.2), 10+ TFL (60 vs 53), 15+ QB hits (68 vs 54). Considered and dropped: an in-game "hot rusher" protection adjustment (like `SHADE_HOT_K` for receivers), because it would push the top down further. Change: `tuning.SACK_CLEANUP` 0.55 -> 0.45 (more sacks credited to the rusher who won his matchup first). This changes only who gets credit, not how many sacks happen, so no recenter is needed. Two 16-season runs after: leader average 18.9 / 19.3, ranges 16-24 / 15-23; 5+ sack players 91 / 95; DT 6+ 8.1 / 8.2; QB hits 15+ 64 / 65; team sacks 2.26 / 2.33 (baseline 2.33, so the dip in run 1 was noise). Still a bit high: 10-12 sack seasons (sacks>=12 10.3 / 11.9 vs real 9.8, range 7-11), plus edge_sacks>=10/12 (partly the real-data labeling issue). 10+ TFL players 58 / 61 (real 50-56). Consistency test (saved league, 4 replays): edge-sack season-to-season change 27% (real 33%, earlier 31%), so slightly steadier; everything else unchanged. Team sport test not rerun (sack credit doesn't change team results).
- Note on the tools: the sim is not reproducible from a seed. Rerunning the same seeds gives different seasons (probably set or dict ordering somewhere in the sim). Leaderboard #1 values move noticeably between identical 16-season runs (e.g. receiving yards #1 1,748 -> 1,638 with no receiving change), so compare two runs before acting on a single #1 rank, and treat measures flagged right at the edge of the real range as noise. Making the sim reproducible would help tuning (a possible small task).
- Oct 19 sim made reproducible (fixes the note above). The same seed now gives exactly the same game and the same season, both twice in one run and across separate runs (checked: one game across 7 separate runs, a full 17-week `simlab.play_season(..., seed="saved-0")` twice in one run and in two separate runs, and a generated league). Two causes, both in `engine/sim/plays.py`: (1) the "was the QB hit hard enough to risk injury" roll (40%) used its own dice that kept rolling from game to game instead of the game's seeded dice, so replaying a game in the same run came out different; it now uses the game's dice. (2) The list of pass rushers was a Python "set" of spot names, whose order changes every time Python starts, so who got credit for clean-up sacks and second-man QB hits depended on that order; it's now a list in a fixed order (line first, then blitzers). No probabilities or tuning values changed; only the order dice are used in, so individual games come out different from before but the averages don't. 16-season realism check after (saved,101,102,103 x 4): league averages all within ~1% of the previous report (points 22.7 vs 22.6, sacks 2.29 vs 2.33, YPA 7.1 vs 7.1, comp% 65.0 vs 65.0), leaderboards within normal run-to-run noise (19 of 282 measures outside the real range vs 22). Third cause, fixed Oct 20 in `engine/database.py`: Blake's saved league has no captains stored in the file, so `load_league` re-voted captains on every load using Python's unseeded dice, and a different captain changes his unit's ratings slightly. Older saves without captains are now voted with dice seeded by season and team (`captains-{season}-{team}`), so the same save always gets the same captains. Checked with no workaround: one game on the saved league identical across 6 separate runs, a full saved-league season identical across 2 separate runs. The saved league's captains may differ from what it showed before this change (the vote itself is unchanged, just repeatable). Saves that already store captains are unaffected.
- Oct 20 rushing-leader check (investigation; the fix it led to is the next item). Watch item from the preseason work: 2,193 and 2,228-yard rushers in an 8-season run. 16-season run (saved,101,102,103 x 4): rushing leaders 1609, 1627, 1654, 1696, 1720, 1841, 1864, 1875, 1886, 1889, 1890, 1921, 2013, 2193, 2221, 2228 (average 1,883; real 2021-25 average 1,710, range 1,459-2,005, all-time record 2,105). Not new and not from the preseason code: the Oct 19 16-season run after the reproducibility fix had the same 2,193/2,221/2,228 seasons, and the run before that fix averaged 1,825 with a 2,124. The real problem is wider than 2,000-yard seasons: per 11 seasons the sim gives about 20 seasons of 1,600+ yards (real 2015-25: 7), 11 of 1,800+ (real 4), 2.75 of 2,000+ (real 2). Ranks #1-#5 run ~8-10% high, #20-#40 a bit low, so too much of the league's rushing goes to the top 5-8 backs. Two causes: (1) star backs are too efficient: 250+ carry backs average 4.77 yards per carry (real 4.44), and 5.5+ YPC on 200+ carries happens about 12 times per 11 seasons (real 5); (2) they get slightly too much work: carry leader 357 (real 326, max 349), because nearly every star's talent gap over his backup pins `rb_split` at the floor (`RB_SPLIT_MIN` 0.18), and teams with a great back also lean run (`STRENGTH_PASS_K`). Carries per game for 300+ carry backs look real (19.9 vs 20.3). Who does it: high-rated starters (overall 81-90, vision 81-99) on run-leaning teams, with average offensive lines (line strength barely matters for who leads). Root mechanism gap: the defense never reacts to a dangerous runner. Its front is picked only from the offense's personnel (`coach.call_defense`), while receivers face coverage rolled to them and tighter coverage when hot (`SHADE_RATE`, `SHADE_HOT_K`, `FED_COVER_K`, the fix that ended 2,000-yard receivers). Proposed fix (Blake approved, built the same day; see next item): "load the box" against a dangerous back, more often the better he is and the hotter he is that game, costing his runs some yards but leaving receivers less help in coverage (the real trade-off: stacking the box opens play action). Then recenter and rerun all checks; only if carries are still high, look at the bell-cow workload. Targets: leader average ~1,700, 1,600+ seasons ~7 per 11 seasons, 250+ carry YPC ~4.45. Scratch scripts (profile of every back's season with ratings, usage and line strength) were kept outside the project.
- Oct 20 load-the-box fix (Blake: "Go ahead"). Defenses now walk a safety down into an 8-man box against a dangerous running back. `coach.load_box` decides it each snap once the defense sees who's in the backfield, before it knows run or pass: only against a back above `LOAD_BOX_MIN` (talent z 0.3; about a quarter of carries), more often the better he is, the more rushing yards he has today (over 50) and the more his team runs; never on 3rd-and-7+, in the hurry-up, in prevent or in dime. Effects (`tuning.LOAD_BOX_*`): a run into a loaded box is tilted -0.35 (about 0.4 yards per carry less); every receiver gets a little more separation (+0.12, one fewer man in coverage), play action more (+0.15); rolling coverage to a star receiver is half as likely (the safety who'd do it is in the box). Play-by-play detail lines show "8 in the box". Measured: stars (talent z 1.5+) see it on ~44% of their carries, other above-average backs ~26%, average backs never; ~5% of all snaps. Recentered (`run` 0.126 -> 0.094, `qb_run` 0.206 -> 0.170, others within ~0.01). 16-season realism check after: league averages unchanged (points 22.6, YPC 4.5, YPA 7.1, rush yards 113.6 vs 114.0); rushing leaders 1388, 1462, 1537, 1541, 1554, 1569, 1615, 1647, 1694, 1736, 1739, 1779, 1789, 1807, 1851, 1999 (average 1,669; real 1,710, range 1,459-2,005; before 1,883 with three 2,190+); rush_yds #3 1,426 (real 1,383; before 1,515), #5 1,297 (1,248; before 1,352); carry leader 345 (real 326; before 357); 200+ carry backs' YPC 4.53 (real 4.45; before 4.62), best 6.21 (5.91; before 6.51). Per 11 seasons: 1,600+ yard seasons 9.6 (real 7; before 20), 1,800+ 2.1 (real 4; before 11), 2,000+ 0 (real 2; before 2.75). So the top is now a little tame rather than inflated (no 2,000-yard season in 16; Blake, Oct 20: records should occasionally fall slightly in 17-game seasons, since most were set in 16 games or fewer). Follow-up done the same day (Blake: soften the hot-back part; he also noted the last decade's 2,000-yard seasons came in 16 games played: Henry 2,027 in 2020, Barkley 2,005 in 2024 sitting week 18): `LOAD_BOX_HOT_K` 0.008 -> 0.003 (a back with 150 yards today now makes a loaded box ~1.35x as likely instead of ~2.2x). Recentered (no center moved more than 0.004). 16-season check: rushing leaders 1534, 1534, 1564, 1579, 1585, 1605, 1616, 1637, 1653, 1676, 1776, 1782, 1857, 1909, 2032, 2191 (average 1,721; real 1,710); per 11 seasons 1,600+ 12.4 (real 7), 1,800+ 2.75 (4), 2,000+ 1.4 (2). The 2,191 is 129 a game, under the per-game record pace (Dickerson 2,105 in 16 = 132). rush_yds #3 1,462 / #5 1,323 (real 1,383 / 1,248, inside the real ranges); carry leader 354 (real 326, max 349); 200+ carry backs 17.2 a season (no longer flagged low), YPC 4.56 (real 4.45), best 5.92 (5.91). Receiving leaders 1443-1949 (no 2,000-yard receiver this run). League averages unchanged (points 22.5, YPC 4.5, YPA 7.1). Other measures moved only by normal run-to-run noise (the change reshuffles the dice). Team sport and consistency tests were run for the first version, not rerun for this small change. Still a bit high: 1,600-1,800 yard seasons and the carry leader (workload, the second cause above). (Correction, later Oct 20: the "real" per-11-season counts above came from 2015-25, mostly 16-game seasons. The 17-game era 2021-25 has 1,600+ about once a season (5 in 5: Taylor 1,811, Jacobs 1,653, Barkley 2,005, Henry 1,921, Cook 1,621), 1,800+ 3 in 5, 2,000+ 1 in 5. Against that, 1,600+ is right, not high.) Side effects checked (first version): 200+ carry backs per season 16.6 (real 18-23, now just under the range; before 17.9) and ranks #20-#40 still a bit low (older gap). One 2,079-yard receiver appeared (league 102, season 3; real record 1,964, about 2,080 at the same per-game pace over 17 games), but his team never faced a loaded box (0% of snaps): the extra dice roll reshuffles every game, so that season simply came out differently. Receiving leader average 1,758 (before 1,724, real 1,811). Team sport test (league 101, 160 games): below-average QB 64 with a stacked cast rating 101.6 / 10.9 wins (before 98.9 / 9.1; the stacked cast includes a 90 RB, so the defense now loads up and the passing game benefits, as in the real NFL), average cast 93.5 / 6.7 (92.6 / 7.2), weak 74.2 / 3.5 (70.9 / 3.0); best QB on a bad roster 8.3 wins (8.8), generational 8.0 (8.7); below-average RB 4.86 / 4.16 / 3.90 YPC (4.79 / 4.26 / 3.80). Consistency test (saved league, 4 replays): RB carries 14% (real 14%), RB YPC change 0.47 (0.39; before 0.49), edge sacks 33% (33%; before 29%), WR yards per target 1.13 (real 0.93; before 0.78, calibration 0.99; this measure has swung run to run, so watch it rather than act on one run). Before/after reports and scratch scripts are in this session's scratchpad.
- Oct 20 blowout rest for running backs (Blake: "tune further" on workload). Found: star backs' games played already match real (97.5% of games for 250+ carry backs vs real 96.7%) and carries per game match (19.7 vs 20.3), but sim teams never rested their lead back once a regular-season game was decided: 250+ carry backs got ~10 carries a season in the 4th quarter while up 17+ (some 22-37). Now (`tuning.RB_BLOWOUT_REST`, `gameteam._fill`, game tells each team the quarter and its lead before every snap via `game_lead`): from the 4th quarter with a 24+ lead the backup takes 85% of RB snaps, 17+ 60%, from the 3rd quarter with 28+ 50% (talent gap doesn't matter then). Regular season only (the preseason has its own playing-time plan). No recenter (it changes who's on the field, not how ratings become results). Measured: those carries fell from ~10 to ~3 a season. 16-season check: carry leader 343 (real 326, range 280-349; before 354), rush_att >=300 2.6 (3.0), >=250 7.6 (8.2); rushing leaders 1465-1901, average 1,685 (real 1,710); 1,600+ 12.4 per 11 seasons (real 17-game era ~11), 1,800+ 2.1 (real ~6.6), 2,000+ 0 (real ~2.2). So the very top is now a bit rare (the previous run had 2,032 and 2,191; these are rare events, so 16 seasons swing). Still low (older gap): 200+ carry backs 16.3 a season (real 18-23) and carry ranks #20-#30 (187/153 vs 206/164): mid-tier and committee backs get too few carries. League averages unchanged (points 22.6, YPC 4.5, YPA 7.1, rush yards 114.8). Changed (3): (1) bug fix: receivers no longer play running back while a healthy back is available (about 15 carries a team-season had gone to WRs or QBs); (2) backups get slightly fewer planned series at RB, so lead backs reach real carry totals; (3) a QB's accuracy matters more, so completion % now rises with QB quality like the real NFL and good QBs separate from bad ones. Also fixed a crash in tools/realism_check.py (a 200-carry back with 0 rushing TDs). Looked into, nothing changed (with reasons below): receiving backs, pass rush, team pass volume, top tight end, plays per game. Needs your call: (a) a passing-down / 3rd-down back (AI choice, a depth chart spot, or both; item 3); (b) whether to loosen `PASS_BIAS_MAX` (item 6). Still outside the real range: mostly the roster-churn gap (tackles 80+, sacks 12+, QB hits 20+, TFL 10+; recheck after in-season signings), 4,500-yard passers and the #1 tight end (pass-heavy-team volume), kicker/punter ranks (known). Your step6_season_test.py runs fine on the new code. Nothing committed.
  - 1. Bug fix: receivers playing running back (`gameteam._fill`, new `_BACKS`). Why 200-carry backs were short: per team, healthy lead backs got exactly the real share of their team's RB carries (deciles 0.48/0.52/0.62/0.71/0.78 vs real 0.48/0.52/0.61/0.70/0.78), but RBs got 354 carries a team-season vs real 370 (real 2021-25 by team: 457 carries, RB 370, QB 70, WR/TE 17; sim 449 / 354 / 74 / 21). About 15 carries a team-season went to the wrong player: when a team's other backs were hurt, the planned RB rotation, blowout rest and fatigue swaps brought in the receiver listed last on the RB depth chart (8.7 carries a team-season, with a healthy starter on the bench) or left no back so the QB kept the handoff (6.0). Now swaps at running back only bring in another RB, or a FB if he's all that's left; a receiver plays there only when no back is left. Result: RB carries 370 per team (real 370), WR/TE 7-8, QB 71. Left over: a team with all 3 backs out at once still uses a receiver there (about 50-70 snaps a season in one test league); in-season signings (piece 6) will fix that. Told the main session. 16 seasons: league averages unchanged; the extra carries went to backups (RB2 107 / RB3 43 vs real 99 / 35), so 200-carry backs still 16.4. Team sport and consistency unchanged (RB carries change 16%, real 14%).

Per team now RB1 / RB2 / RB3 median 212 / 104 / 34 (real 216 / 99 / 34). 16 seasons: 200+ carry backs 17.8 (real 18-23; no longer flagged), rush_att #20 193 (206), rush_yds #20 895 (889), #30 708 (739); carry leader 348 (real 326, max 349; just inside); rushing leaders 1416-1966, average 1,673 (real 1,710); #3 1,451 / #5 1,332 (real 1,383 / 1,248, inside the ranges); 300+ carry backs 3.3 (3.0). What's missing is a role: on 33% of real teams the best receiving back isn't the lead rusher (a third-down or passing-down back); in the sim only 14%, so catches pile onto lead backs a bit (top-decile receiving back 72 vs 68). A passing-down back would bring in a better receiving back on 3rd-and-long and in the two-minute drill. That touches the depth chart (FOF8 lets you set it), so it's Blake's call: AI staffs choosing one automatically, a "3rd down back" spot on the depth chart, or both?

Too many 12+ sack (12.2 vs 9.8), 20+ QB hit (35 vs 27) and 10+ TFL (61 vs 53) seasons, while team totals match (per team game sacks 2.30 vs 2.39, QB hits 5.71 vs 5.61, TFL 4.77 vs 4.83) and the leaders are right or a bit low (sacks #1 19.3 vs 20.1; TFL #1 22 vs 27.5). Checked against real 2023-25 snap counts: productive rushers play the real share of snaps (8-11 sacks: 72% vs 69%; 12+: 74% vs 74%), and defensive regulars miss the real number of games (40+ tackle players: 14.75 games vs 14.9). The difference: real teams spread defense over more players (31.6 defenders with a tackle per team-season vs 23.9), because injured players are replaced by signings and call-ups; a sim team has the same ~25 defenders all year, so its regulars stack up good-not-great seasons (12.1 per team with 40+ tackles vs 9.9). This is the known roster-churn gap behind tackles>=80 and db_tackles>=80 too. Fixing it with a sim knob would be the wrong cause; recheck after in-season signings (piece 6). Regular edge rushers do get sacks a little more often per snap (1.22 vs 1.08 per 100 snaps; real labels mix in some 4-3 off-ball OLBs, so the real number is a bit low).

Steps taken: the first version (only 3rd/4th and 5+ and the hurry-up, about 1 snap in 5) barely moved receiving (top receiving back = lead rusher 86% -> 83%, real 67%), so passing situations were widened (3rd/4th and 4+, 2nd and 8+, two-minute drill, down 9+ in the 4th); that showed a side effect (a starter who's his own 3rd-down back lost his usual rotation on those snaps and caught more), fixed by using the 3rd-down list only when it names a different back. Result (8 seasons): RB targets per team 103 (real 101); lead rusher / 2nd / 3rd back catches 41.3 / 22.4 / 9.8 (real 38.7 / 22.1 / 8.9); top receiving back 44.7 (43.4), top-decile 69 (68); top receiving back is the lead rusher on 76% of teams (real 67%; was 86%). 16 seasons: 60+ catch backs 6.3 (real 4.6, range 3-6; no longer flagged); league averages unchanged; rushing leaders 1472-1970, average 1,747 (real 1,710); carry leader 361 (real 326, max 349; flagged, but it has ranged 339-361 across tonight's runs). Cost: some lead-back carries go to the 3rd-down back (often the 3rd-string back): lead back median 201 (real 216; was 212), 200+ carry backs 16.8 (real 18-23). Consistency: RB carries change 14% (real 14%), RB YPC 0.41 (0.39), WR yards per target 1.08 (0.93; this measure has ranged 0.78-1.13 run to run).

Plays per game (looked into, nothing changed): 61.3 vs real 62.4 per team game, counted the same way in both (runs, passes, sacks, scrambles, kneels, spikes); the gap is in every part (passes -0.3, runs -0.7, sacks -0.2) and sim teams get ~5% more drives with fewer plays each. A 1.8% gap, inside the 6% tolerance; it touches the clock and drive logic behind every stat, so not tuned overnight.
  - How to read the 16-season leaders (worth knowing when judging records): the "real range" is from only 5 seasons, so even a perfect sim would put about 1 in 6 seasons above the real 5-season max by chance (2-3 of 16). Over the runs tonight, receiving leaders above 1,947 came out at 1-2 per 16 seasons and rushing leaders above 2,005 at 0-2, which is consistent with real. Both 2,000-yard receivers in the last run (2,001 and 2,065) faced a loaded box on 0-1% of snaps, so they're not from the box change. Added to tools/README.md.

## Step 7 plan (agreed with Blake Oct 19)
Pieces, in order, each testable on its own:
1. Save slots + main menu (New game, Load game; several saves in `saves/`; save file stores season, week, games, stats). Blake: build this first, since everything after needs to save season progress.
2. Real schedule + week-by-week play: NFL schedule formula (Blake): 6 division games, 4 vs a rotating division in the conference, 4 vs a rotating division in the other conference, 2 vs same-place finishers in the conference, a 17th cross-conference game; bye weeks; "Regular season games" setting (16/17, default 17). Sim week button, standings, injuries carried week to week.
3. Season stats + playoffs: today's 14-team format (Blake): 7 per conference, 4 division winners + 3 wild cards, only the #1 seed gets a bye; real NFL tiebreakers.
4. Offseason basics: aging, retirements, player development (design above). FIRST (Blake, Oct 20: "every player's entire stats from their entire career should be saved and accessible"): before the next season starts, archive every player's complete season line (every stat; regular season and playoffs apart; split by team if he played for more than one) permanently in the save; keep retired players in the save (marked retired) so their careers stay viewable; move past seasons' box scores to a separate history file beside the save (nothing deleted, main save stays fast). Player card: FOF8-style stats with a row per season (year, team, games, stats) and a career totals row at the bottom, per category (Blake wants this; fine to build with piece 4). Note: `season.start_season` clears `league.boxes`, so it must never run for a new season before the archive step exists. Until the real draft exists (Step 8), a placeholder auto-draft (Blake): a generated rookie class each offseason handed out worst-to-first, so multi-season tests have roster turnover. Show Blake player histories before locking development numbers.
5. Training camp + preseason as separate sim steps: camp/preseason injuries, rust, staged development reveals. (Preseason games, schedule and playing time were built early, Oct 20; see Piece 2. Still to do here: training camp before the Hall of Fame Game, camp injuries, rust, development reveals.)
6. In-season roster moves: injured reserve, AI signing free agents to cover injuries. (From the rushing session, Oct 21: when all of a team's RBs are hurt at once, a WR still has to play RB, about 50-70 snaps a season in one test league; signing a back fixes it.)
7. Multi-season check: team strength spread should grow from ~4 toward ~6 points per game; rerun all realism tools.

### Piece 1: save slots and main menu (built Oct 19)
- Decisions (Blake): saving is MANUAL by default; autosave is an option in Settings. New game asks for save name, first season, regular season games (16/17) and roster method; Blake invited extra quality-of-life touches.
- Flow: `python app.py` opens the main menu: Continue (last save played, with team/season/saved time), New game, Load game, Settings, Quit game. Pages: `/` menu, `/new`, `/load`, `/settings`, `/game`.
- New game: step 1 league settings (first season 2000-2099, default 2026; 17 or 16 games, default 17; "Random generated rosters", with Startup draft and Legacy rosters listed as coming later). Step 2 builds the league and shows a sortable table of all 32 teams (division, starters/offense/defense averages, starting QB, best player) so you can pick knowing what you're getting. Extras added (Claude): "Pick a team for me", "Build a different league" (re-roll rosters), save name auto-filled as "<Team> <year>" until you type your own, settings summary. Nothing is saved until Start game. Settings are stored per save (`League.settings`: season_games, roster_method; `League.name`).
- Saving: header shows "Unsaved changes" (yellow) or "All changes saved"; Save button and Ctrl+S. Main menu with unsaved changes asks: Save and leave / Leave without saving (undoes them) / Cancel. "Save a copy" writes the game as it is now to a new save and you keep playing the original. Load game: Load, Rename, Make a copy, Delete (confirm; can't be undone). Duplicate names are refused (or numbered for new games).
- Recovery copy (Claude, technical): the game window can't be stopped from closing (NiceGUI doesn't pass the window's "closing" event through), so closing with unsaved changes writes `<save>.recovery.db` on shutdown. The main menu then offers "Restore and continue" or "Throw them away"; the real save isn't touched unless you restore. Saving normally deletes any recovery copy.
- Screens now call `state.mark_changed()` after changing the league instead of writing to the file directly (depth chart edits, choosing your team). Every future screen that changes the league must do the same.
- Blake's existing `saves/league.db` is untouched and shows up as a save named "League". The realism tools' `saved` league is still `saves/league.db` (it could later be pointed at a chosen save).
- Testing options: `python app.py --browser` opens in the web browser instead of the window; `--no-show` doesn't open a tab; `--saves-dir=<folder>` uses another saves folder so tests never touch real saves. `.claude/launch.json` has a "game-test" setup using all three.
- Test results (Claude, browser mode, test saves folder with a copy of league.db): menu, New game both steps, team table, Start game, unsaved-changes status, Save, Ctrl+S, leave dialog (both choices), Continue, Load/Make a copy/Rename/Delete, autosave on (file written on each change) and off, recovery copy written and restored, old league.db loads. Save 0.05 s, load 0.04 s, new league 0.8 s. Not tested here: the real desktop window (closing it really writes the recovery copy; NiceGUI runs shutdown handlers on window close, so it should work). Blake to check.

### Piece 2: schedule, week-by-week play, standings (built Oct 20)
- Decisions (Blake): real NFL schedule formula. The schedule is made all at once before the season so every team knows all its games in advance (Blake: "teams know the entire schedule before the season starts"); for now that's at league creation (or when an older save is first opened); once the offseason exists it goes before training camp (Blake suggested the camp-to-preseason transition; the real NFL releases it in mid-May, so it could be visible during camp too). Your weekly game: watch it or sim it, your choice (Blake picked "Watch or sim").
- `engine/schedule.py`: who plays whom follows the formula (6 division, 4 vs a rotating division in the conference on a 3-year cycle, 4 vs a rotating division in the other conference on a 4-year cycle, 2 same-place games in the conference, 17th game vs a same-place team from another non-conference division, host conference alternating by year). Every team gets 8 or 9 home games. First season: "last season's" division order is random (`League.division_ranks` None); piece 3/4 must store real final division ranks for the next season. Weeks: byes in weeks 5-14 (5-13 for 16 games), at most 6 teams off a week, final week all division games, no 3 straight road or 3 straight home games (a bye breaks a run). Method (Claude, technical): week-by-week greedy filling failed every time, so it uses the standard sports-scheduling approach: place games with "Kempe chain" swaps so nobody is double-booked, then simulated annealing with chain swaps (including trading weeks with the division rematch to flip home/road) until byes and streaks are legal. ~1-2 s per schedule; 24 test seasons (12 x 17 games, 12 x 16) passed every rule. Week 1 is the Sunday after Labor Day (2026: Sun, Sep 13).
- Rotation over the years (Blake asked to confirm every team eventually plays every other, as in the NFL): a new schedule is built each season from the year and last season's division finish (the season rollover itself comes in piece 4). Fixed a bug found while checking: home/away in the 4-game division-vs-division sets didn't flip between cycles (it was tied to finishing place, which changes yearly), so a team could keep hosting the same opponents. Now hosting uses a fixed team order and flips each time the rotation comes back. Checked over 2026-2037: every conference pair meets within every 3-year window and every cross-conference pair within every 4-year window (0 misses), and every team hosts every non-division opponent at least once within 6 years (conference) / 8 years (other conference) (0 misses). Schedules made before the fix (test saves only) are still valid first-season schedules.
- `engine/season.py`: play a week (every unplayed game), record score, overtime, compact box score (players, team totals, quarter points, scoring, drives, weather) and injuries; heal injuries by calendar week (a bye counts as a healing week, as in the real NFL; the realism tools don't have byes); standings (W-L-T, pct, PF/PA, home/away, division, conference, streak, head-to-head). Division order ties: head-to-head, division record, conference record, point differential, points scored (full NFL tiebreakers come with the playoffs in piece 3). Each game's seed is season + game number, so a watched game is exactly the game Sim week would play (checked: 16 of 16 identical; relies on the reproducibility fix from the separate Oct 19 session).
- Saved in the save file: `schedule` table (with each played game's box score as JSON), `injuries` table, week/stage/division ranks in league_info. A full season's save is ~5 MB; save/load still ~0.05 s. Older saves get a schedule when opened (shows "Unsaved changes").
- Screens: a season bar under the header (week of 18 and date, your record, this week's opponent or result, Watch my game, Sim week, Sim ahead: through week N or to the end of the regular season) with a progress window ("Simming week 8...", Stop after this week). New tabs: Schedule (League week: every game of a week with scores and records, byes listed; Team season: the selected team's 18 weeks with date, vs/at opponent, result, running record, bye; click any played game for its box score: line score, team stats, both player boxes, scoring summary, drive chart) and Standings (both conferences, every division). Game tab: "This week" line with Watch this game (counts; recorded when it ends) above the exhibition picker; Sim week is blocked while you're watching your game. Injured players get a red OUT tag (hover: "Out: knee, 3 more weeks") on the Roster, the player report, and the Depth chart.
- Test results (Claude): full UI pass in browser mode with test saves: old save gets a schedule, watch my game -> recorded (TAM 0-1), Sim week blocked while watching, Sim ahead through week 4, Schedule both views and box scores, Standings, OUT tags, save -> menu shows "week 5" -> Continue restores week 5, sim to end -> "regular season complete". Stats check, 8 seasons (saved,101,102,103 x 2) through the season engine: points 22.5 per team game (realism check 22.6), pass att 32.7 (32.7), pass yds 230 (230), YPA 7.04 (7.1), rush yds 114 (113), sacks 2.29 (2.33); player-games missed per team ~96 (realism ~100, slightly lower from bye-week healing); ties 0.6 per season (known gap: slightly more than real). The realism tools still use their own round-robin schedule without byes; switching `tools/simlab.py` to the real schedule is a possible later change (it would slightly lower injury totals).
- Preseason (built Oct 20, ahead of piece 5 at Blake's request). Blake: 5 preseason weeks; week 1 is only the Hall of Fame Game; the two Hall of Fame teams get a preseason bye in another week, so every team plays 4; "Exhibition" renamed to preseason and the preseason is not part of the regular season; the preseason schedule is set in advance like the NFL's, not "pick any two teams". (This is the NFL's pre-2021 format; the NFL has played 3 + the Hall of Fame Game since 2021.) Playing time (Blake chose the classic NFL pattern): starters play 1-2 series in their 1st preseason game, about a quarter in the 2nd, the first half in the 3rd ("dress rehearsal"), and sit the 4th; the second string plays until the 4th quarter, then the third string; everyone healthy dresses (no 46-man limit).
  - `schedule.build_preseason`: Hall of Fame Game = one Eastern vs one Western team at random, neutral site (Canton, Ohio: no home edge, Cleveland's weather); their bye is the same later week (so that week has 15 games). Weeks 2-5: 2 home / 2 away for everyone, opponents weighted to nearby cities (`CITY_COORDS`; median trip ~600 miles), never a division rival, never the week-1 regular-season opponent, nobody twice. Dates: Hall of Fame Game the Thursday 4 weeks before the last preseason week, which is the Thursday before Labor Day (2026: Aug 6, 13, 20, 27, Sep 3). 20 test preseasons passed every rule, ~0.15 s each.
  - Sim (`engine/sim`, preseason games only; regular-season games run exactly the same code as before): `GameTeam(preseason_game=n, neutral=...)` and `Game(preseason=..., neutral=...)`. Before each snap the game tells each team the elapsed time; resting players are moved behind fresh players from the same position family (OL, DL, LB, DB, WR, TE, RB, QB), including unlisted backups; when no fresh player is left the second string comes back before the starters. Weather: August uses September's climate +4 F (cities without August data). Measured over a full preseason: starting QB plays 12% / 20% / 47% / 0% of snaps in games 1-4 (planned ~12% / 25% / 50% / 0%); offensive starters as a group 29% / 38% / 58% / 29%, because a 53-man roster has only 4 backup linemen, so one starting lineman plays all game. A 90-man camp roster (with the draft and undrafted signings) would fix that. ~5 players per team are still injured from the preseason going into week 1 (they carry over; preseason injuries were already planned for Step 7).
  - Season flow: stage `preseason` (`League.pre_week` 1-5) -> `regular_season` (week 1-18) -> `regular_season_done`. One calendar for injuries (preseason weeks 1-5, then regular-season weeks), so healing carries across. Preseason games are stored in their own table (ids 1001+) with box scores; they never count in the standings (and won't count in season stats). New leagues start at the Hall of Fame Game; a save at regular-season week 1 with nothing played gets this year's preseason added when opened.
  - Screens: season bar shows "2026 preseason · Hall of Fame Game week · Thu, Aug 6" (or Preseason week 1-4), your preseason opponent or bye; Sim ahead offers preseason weeks, end of preseason, regular-season week 1, end of regular season. Schedule: League week runs Hall of Fame Game -> Preseason weeks 1-4 -> Weeks 1-18 (preseason labeled "don't count in the standings", preseason byes listed); Team season shows the preseason first (HOF, P1-P4) then the regular season. Game tab: the exhibition picker is gone; it shows your game this week (Hall of Fame/preseason/regular) with Watch this game; watched preseason games are recorded like regular ones (15 of 15 identical to simmed).
  - Also (Oct 20): the regular-season schedule now never puts a team's two games against a division rival in back-to-back weeks (Blake's test league had Denver vs San Francisco in weeks 4 and 5); the 24-season schedule check and the rotation check still pass; schedules take ~3 s now.
  - Tests: engine (preseason sim + playing time, mid-preseason save round trip identical, watched = simmed, full season after the preseason: 17 games per team in the standings); browser UI: Hall of Fame week bar and Schedule, Sim week, HOF box score header ("Hall of Fame Game (preseason) · Thu, Aug 6 · Canton, Ohio"), Team season view with preseason, watching a preseason game -> recorded. Not checked in the browser: the "Sim ahead" menu from preseason into the regular season (the browser pane was hidden at that point; the engine path is covered by the script tests). Realism check (8 seasons) after the sim change: league averages unchanged (points 22.5, YPA 7.1, YPC 4.5); sack leaders 17-22 (real 18-23). Watch item: rushing leaders 2,193 and 2,228 in 2 of 8 seasons (real 2021-25 max 2,005; all-time record 2,105); not caused by the preseason code (it doesn't run in regular-season games); look at it separately with 16-season runs. (Done Oct 20: an older, wider gap; see "Oct 20 rushing-leader check" under Progress.)
- Blake's first play-test of piece 2 + preseason (Oct 20): "seems pretty good"; UI look to be reworked later (foundation first). Decisions: preseason games never count toward season or career stats, and no preseason stats section (Blake: "seems pointless"); preseason box scores stay viewable in the Schedule tab. Fixes from his test: the Game tab now clears the live game view (scoreboard, field, play-by-play) as soon as a watched game ends, and after simming; the "This week" line then shows the final score with a Game summary button, and the season bar shows "Last game: W 26-20 (OT) vs TAM" + Game summary (your most recent played game, preseason or regular). Game summary opens the Schedule tab on that week with the game selected and its box score (line score, team stats, both player boxes, scoring, drives) on the right. Tested in the browser. His "Continue" error on the main menu came from opening the game while another session was mid-edit on engine files; it couldn't be reproduced afterwards (both Continue and Restore and continue work on a copy of his saves). Lesson: test the game only when side sessions working in this folder are idle.
- Known gaps (piece 2): injured players aren't replaced on the roster (piece 6: injured reserve and signings), the game walks down the depth chart instead. (Season stats and playoffs: done in piece 3. The Game tab clearing: fixed Oct 20.)

### Piece 3: season stats, league leaders, playoffs (built Oct 20)
- Decisions (Blake): the title game is "the Glory Bowl" (Claude added Roman numerals counting from the league's first season, stored as `settings["first_season"]`: the 2026 game is Glory Bowl I; easy to drop). Preseason games never count in stats. Playoff format as decided earlier: 14 teams.
