- Playoffs (`engine/playoffs.py`, `season.start_playoffs`): after the last regular-season week, 7 seeds per conference (division winners 1-4 by record, then 3 wild cards); Wild Card 2v7 3v6 4v5, #1 bye; Divisional and Conference rounds re-seed (best seed left hosts the lowest); an off week; the Glory Bowl at a neutral site rotating among warm/dome cities (seeded by season), "home" side alternating by conference each year. Stages: preseason -> regular_season -> playoffs (rounds 1-5, 4 = off week) -> season_complete. Each team's division finish is stored (`division_ranks`) for next season's same-place games. Saves whose regular season had finished before the playoffs existed go straight to the Wild Card round when opened.
- NFL tiebreakers (division and wild card procedures, three-team rules restart at step 1 once a team is decided, wild-card ties compare only the best team per division, sweep rule for 3+ team head-to-head, common games minimum 4 for wild cards, strength of victory/schedule, net points in conference/all games, seeded coin toss). Left out: combined ranking and net touchdowns (almost never decisive). The Standings tab uses the same tiebreakers.
- Playoff overtime (`engine/sim/game.py`, playoff games only): 15-minute periods, 3 timeouts, both teams possess (as before), and another period if still undecided, until someone wins. Regular-season and preseason games unchanged. Check: 400 playoff games between two teams: no ties, 12 went to OT, 1 needed a second period.
- Stats (`engine/stats.py`): every player's and team's totals from the stored box scores, regular season and playoffs apart (as the NFL keeps them). Leaders use NFL minimums for rate stats (passing 14 att/team game, rushing 6.25 carries, receiving 1.875 catches, FG 1 att, punting 2.5 punts, returns 1/0.5). Career totals will sum archived season lines once piece 4 archives seasons.
- Screens: season bar shows the playoff round and date, your seed/opponent/bye/elimination ("Missed the playoffs", "Season over: lost in the divisional round"), and at the end "2026 season complete · Glory Bowl I champion: X" with how your team finished; Sim ahead offers each playoff round and "Through the Glory Bowl I". New Stats tab (League leaders grid, top 5 per stat with your players highlighted; Player stats table per category, team filter, sortable; Team stats per game, sortable; Regular season / Playoffs switch). New Playoffs tab (seeds with record and how they got in, eliminated teams struck through, each round's games, Glory Bowl; click a game for its summary). Schedule tab: playoff rounds after week 18 with seeds; team view adds a Playoffs section. Standings: Seed column once seeded. Player report: Season stats section (each category the player has, regular season and playoffs).
- Tests (Claude): engine season through the Glory Bowl (seeding sanity: division winners 1-4, wild cards at least as good as every non-playoff team; re-seeding; mid-playoff save round trip; champion; next season's division ranks stored); browser: Tampa test save simmed to the end of the regular season -> Wild Card round, "Missed the playoffs", Playoffs tab bracket, sim through the Glory Bowl -> champion shown, Stats tab leaders and player stats, player report Season stats. Realism check not rerun for this piece: the only sim change only affects playoff games. The rushing session was editing engine/sim at the same time; its own realism runs cover its change.
- Watch items (single seasons, may be noise; passed to the rushing session to check over 16 seasons): tackles leader 203 in one league (real 173-192); yards-per-carry leaders mostly QBs in one league (4 of top 5, 5.9-6.1). Rushing session's answer: tackles unaffected by its change (leader 168-179 across three 16-season runs, one 201 in 16), so the 203 was noise; QB rushing totals unchanged and near real; the QB-in-top-5-YPC count was still being measured.
- Rushing check (Blake, Oct 20): two 2,000-yard seasons in 16 sim seasons "seems accurate": the NFL had 3 in the last 15 seasons (Peterson 2012, Henry 2020, Barkley 2024; 4 counting C. Johnson 2009). One sim season was 2,191 (above Dickerson's 2,105 record, but under his per-game pace over 17 games); keep an eye on it as more seasons are simmed.

### Piece 4a: career stats for good, player card, season rollover (built Oct 20)
- Blake: every player's entire career stats must be saved and accessible; FOF8-style player card with season rows and a career total at the bottom.
- `season.start_next_season` (season bar button "Start the 2027 season" once the Glory Bowl is played): 1) archives every player's season lines (`stats.season_lines`: every stat, regular season and playoffs apart, one line per team he played for) into `League.career`, each team's season (`team_season_summary`: W-L-T, points, division finish, seed, playoff result) into `League.team_history`, and the champion into `League.champions`; 2) returns the finished games with box scores, which the screens write to the save's history file (`saves/<save>.history.db`, `database.write_history`; INSERT OR REPLACE so writing twice is harmless; `read_history` reads them back); 3) next year: experience +1 for rostered players (ages move by themselves since age is computed from the season year), captains re-voted (seeded), injuries healed over the offseason, new schedules from this season's division finish (`start_season`). Retirements, rookies and development: 4b/4c.
- Save file: new tables `player_seasons` and `team_seasons`, champions in league_info. Copying a save (Load game > Make a copy, or Save a copy) copies its history file; deleting a save deletes it too. Sizes after one archived season + 2 weeks: main save 2.4 MB, history file 3.5 MB.
- Player card (`/player/<id>`, `screens/player_card_page.py`): "Open player card" button in the Roster tab's player report, or click a player in Stats > Player stats. Left: bio, ratings, combine, personality. Right: career stats, Regular season and Playoffs apart; one table per category he has stats in; a row per season (per team if traded), "*" for the season in progress; a bold Career row (counts added, longest plays kept, rates recomputed). "Back to the game" returns to the tab you were on.
- Tests: engine (season 1 -> rollover -> 2027: archived lines add up exactly to the season's totals, experience +1, boxes cleared, history file 349 games and no duplicates when written twice, save round trip identical, career rows 2026 + 2027 in progress); browser (Tampa test save: sim through Glory Bowl I -> Start the 2027 season -> HOF week Thu Aug 5, 2027 -> sim into week 2 -> player card shows 2026, 2027* and Career rows; back returns to the game; save writes the history file).
- Not yet: viewing past seasons' standings/leaders/box scores in the screens (the data is all kept: team_history, career lines, history file); league history page (champions by year).

### Piece 4b: retirements, placeholder draft, cuts, Offseason report (built overnight Oct 20-21)
Blake asked Claude to keep going while he slept; these are Claude's judgment calls, for Blake to review:
- `engine/offseason.py`, run by `season.start_next_season` after the season is archived and the year advances: 1) retirements, 2) rookie class + 7-round draft, 3) undrafted rookies sign, 4) cut down to 53, 5) short positions filled from free agents, 6) free-agent pool refreshed, 7) depth charts. Then training camp (development, 4c), captains re-voted, new schedule.
- Retirements: each rostered player's chance climbs past his position's typical retirement age (`RETIRE_AGE`: QB 37, RB 30, WR/TE/DE/DT/S 32, OL 33-34, LB/CB 31, K 39, P 38, LS 37), more gradually or sharply via `RETIRE_AGE_SPREAD`; better players at their position last longer (`RETIRE_TALENT_K`); Generational players +2.5 years. Retired players stay in the save (`Player.retired` = last season) so career stats stay viewable; the player card says "Retired after the 20XX season". Measured: ~165-185 retirements a year after the first (254 the first year, the generated league starts a bit old), average age ~30.5; league average age holds ~26.2-26.4 (real NFL ~26).
- Rookie class (placeholder until Step 8's real draft and scouting): per position, sized by that position's openings this year x1.3 plus 5% of its roster spots (~320 a year), each the best of a pool of 1.3x as many generated prospects (since Oct 21: 2.5x, set 8 points lower with less for top prospects; see the aged-league entry in 4c) (`ROOKIE_POOL`, see 4c: this is what holds the league's talent level steady). Rookies: experience 0, age 21-23, ~70% developed.
- Draft order: teams that missed the playoffs by record (worst first), then playoff teams by round of elimination, champion last (coin flip ties). Draft board (`_set_draft_board`): ratings can't be compared across positions (kickers' numbers run higher), so each position is ranked on its own (70% ceiling + 30% now, with scouting misjudgment `DRAFT_SCOUTING_MISS` 6) and given the rounds that position really goes in, from real 2012-2026 drafts (`colleges.DRAFT_BY_POSITION`); the 224 picks are shared among positions by how often each is drafted. Teams pick from the board nudged by need (reach `NEED_BONUS` 0.6 round early per player short, up to 2) and wait on positions where they're over the roster count. AI rule (Blake's design rules): a team with a starting QB aged 27 or younger rated 70+ doesn't take a QB in rounds 1-2. Measured over 6 drafts: QBs ~2 per first round (real ~2.7), K/P/LS only in rounds 4-7 (first attempt let kickers go in round 1 because their raw ratings are higher; fixed with the board), FBs rounds 6-7.
- Undrafted: each team signs up to 3 undrafted rookies who beat its weakest player at that position; cut to 53 (lowest-value players at positions over the roster count go; this year's round 1-3 picks are protected a little); positions still short are filled with the best free agent (or a generated one if none). Every team ends at exactly 53 with every position at its count (checked every offseason). Free agents: old ones and ~30% of unsigned veterans leave each year; the pool keeps at most 260.
- Your team: drafted automatically for now (Step 8 brings the real draft). Your depth chart keeps your order; departed players come off and only empty spots are filled. AI depth charts are redone after camp.
- Offseason report (`/offseason`, button in the season bar during the preseason that follows): your team's retirements, draft picks (round, pick, college, overall, potential), undrafted signings, releases, positions filled; around the league: the best players to retire and the whole draft round by round (click a player for his card). Draft history is kept for good (`drafts` table).
- Save file: `players.retired`, `players.dev` (hidden development state), tables `drafts`, `rating_history`; league_info `offseason_report`, `season_injuries`.

### Piece 4c: player development (built overnight Oct 20-21; numbers NOT locked)
- `engine/development.py`, run once a year at training camp (in the offseason, after the draft). Implements the Oct 19 design: two layers of potential (long-term ceiling that drifts every year with heavy-tailed randomness, so it now and then takes a bigger lasting step; and a season swing that partly carries over and partly fades, the carry share random 25-75%), no yes/no switch anywhere. Odds come from each player's volatility (biggest), age (young swing more), playing time (half the snaps or more leans up a little; under 10% leans down), serious injuries (8+ weeks can lower the ceiling for good; since Oct 21: 12+ weeks, 60% of the time), Generational (half the movement, no big drops). Each skill also wanders a little on its own. Aging: past his position's peak years (`PEAK_SEASONS`; QB 5-13 and RB 3-9 from the design doc as quoted in this log; RB set to 3-8; the rest are Claude's estimates and should be checked against the design doc's table, which Claude couldn't read here: no PDF reader installed. Corrected Oct 21: the doc has only three examples, and the table was off by one season; see the aged-league entry below) his potential declines 1.2 points the first year and 0.45 more each further year, physical skills faster. Current ratings close ~20-38% of the gap to potential each year (more when young), with a little noise; if potential falls below current, current drops 75% of the gap. Rookies' first camp just sets them up. Every player's overall/potential at each camp is kept (`rating_history`) and shown on the player card ("Ratings by season").
- Tuning (fast harness: fake game scores, real offseason + development, 16-20 years; two leagues): 
  - First version: potential barely moved (93-95% moved 0-3 points a year vs Blake's 88-90%) and the league's talent crept up (starter average 68.6 -> 76 over 12 seasons; QBs rated 80+ 4 -> 26). Causes: leans by age averaged upward, and repeated yearly selection (keep the best of veterans + rookies + free agents; worse players retire earlier) with rookie classes as strong as the starting league.
  - Fixes: leans balanced (`LEAN_BY_AGE` +0.30 to age 23, +0.10 to 25, -0.15 to 28, -0.45 after; playing time +/-0.15); movement up (`LONG_SD` 1.4, `SWING_SD` 1.8); rookie pool 2.5 -> 1.3; rookie class per position sized by openings (a flat share let long-career positions pile up talent: QB 80+ 4 -> 20 while corners thinned).
  - Result over 16 years in leagues 101 and 102: league starter average stays within ~1.5 of the start (68.6 -> 69.1; 68.9 -> 67.8); ~89% of players move 0-3 points of potential a year (target 88-90%); QBs rated 80+ 6-11 (target ~6); corners steady. Still drifting a bit: WRs rated 80+ 16 -> mid-20s (target 18) and kickers 8 -> 12-18 (target 14); worth a closer look.
  - Average overall by age among rostered players keeps rising into the early 30s (survivors are the good ones, as in the NFL) with rookies (21-23) averaging mid-40s and 27-year-olds low 60s.
- Real-game check (first long run, old settings, 13 seasons): game stats stay at real levels even as league talent drifts (points per team game 22.6-24.4), because the sim reads players against the league's own average starter.
- Later overnight changes: leans by career stage at his position instead of raw age (`LEAN_BY_STAGE`: before peak +0.30, early peak +0.10, peak -0.10, past peak -0.30; raw-age leans made long-career QBs slide for years, starters 71 -> 66); movement eased to `LONG_SD` 1.25 / `SWING_SD` 1.6 (real games add movement from injuries and playing time). Real-game validation, league 102, 15 seasons: 87.7-89.7% of players move 0-3 points of potential a year, 0.5-1.5% move 8+; QB starters 68-70 with 5-8 QBs rated 80+ (target ~6); WRs 80+ 14-25 (target 18); corners steady; kickers 80+ 13-20 (target 14, a bit high); points per team game 22.1-23.4.
- Reading the most dramatic careers from that run (`drama.py` in Claude's scratchpad: biggest jumps, drops, comebacks, random careers, Generational players): bounce-backs (first-round ILB 87 -> 66 -> 82 over four years), lasting drops (2nd-round T 83 -> 64, stays down), early busts (2nd-round CB 82 -> 69 in year two), steady stars (1st-round T 62 -> 86) and Generational players with long ~93-95 peaks fading in their 30s all read naturally. But the biggest jumps were flukes: very volatile backups jumping 30+ points of potential for one year and falling back (WR 55 -> 91 -> 71), and one 26-year-old C whose current rating fell 19 points in a year. Fix: lighter tails (`TAIL_DF` 3 -> 5) and the most extreme single-year draw limited to 3.5 spreads (`TAIL_LIMIT`; shapes the development dice only, no game stat is capped). Rare 8-15 point moves remain.
- Save size after 15 seasons: 11 MB (31,849 career lines, 6,814 players incl. retired); load 0.3 s, save 0.3 s, career lookups instant. Fine for autosave.
- Final settings (validated with real games, league 104, 15 seasons; `SWING_SD` later 1.6, see the aged-league entry): `LONG_SD` 1.15, `SWING_SD` 1.45, `TAIL_DF` 4, `TAIL_LIMIT` 4.0. 87-91% of players move 0-3 points of potential a year (average ~89%); 0.3-0.9% move 8+; biggest one-year moves +11/-12; QBs rated 80+ 6-10; WRs 80+ 14-25; corners steady; kickers 80+ 13-18; points per team game 22.2-24.1 (avg ~22.9). With 5 tails and limit 3.5 the biggest jump in 15 seasons was +14 (felt tame), with 3 and no limit +36 (flukes).
- **For Blake's review: `docs/development-review.md`** has the final sample careers (breakouts, drops, late bloomers, busts, Generational players) and which knob does what. Numbers stay unlocked until he's read them. If he wants more drama, raise `TAIL_LIMIT` / lower `TAIL_DF` (bigger rare moves) or raise `LONG_SD`/`SWING_SD` (more movement overall).
- AGED-LEAGUE REALISM (Oct 21, after the rushing session finished): the realism tools only test fresh leagues, but Blake's leagues will age through many offseasons. Realism check (8 seasons, frozen rosters) on two leagues aged 15 seasons vs the same leagues fresh: 48 vs 32 and 53 vs 33 measures outside the real range. League averages still real (points 23.2), but RB receiving ran high and TE receiving low, and too many 5-12 sack / 80+ tackle / 15+ QB-hit seasons. Cause found: talent within each position had spread out (starters' spread +~30%: RB 7 -> 10.5, DT 9 -> 12.6, K 9 -> 16, P 10.5 -> 17), because the looser rookie filter chosen to stop inflation (pool 1.3) let more weak players in. Position levels also drifted a few points each way (top WRs 83 -> 79, TEs 71 -> 68, RBs 71 -> 74 in league 104).
  - Tried and rejected: steering each position's rookie class toward its first-season level (`POOL_STEER_K`, left in the code at 0: overshot because rookies take years to develop, and inflated the league ~3 points since a class can't be thinner than a random set); a slow fade on the long-term layer (`LONG_PULL` 0.10, kept: harmless, but not the cause).
  - Fix (scored across 3 leagues x 16 seasons, later 8 years vs start): strict rookie filter again (`ROOKIE_POOL` 2.5, the starting league's) with every rookie class 8 points lower (`ROOKIE_LEVEL_SHIFT` -8), and calmer K/P/LS development (`SPECIALIST_SCALE` 0.6). Result: league level +0.4 (was +0.9), starter spread -0.6 (was +1.6), roster spread +1.0 (was +2.2), K/P spread +2.3 (was +4.2), ~92.7% moving 0-3 in the fast harness (real games run ~3% lower). Note: the shift lowers rookies' skill ratings but not their athletic traits/combine numbers, so for rookies the combine slightly overstates their ratings; a cleaner version would shift talent inside generate_player. Real-game validation of setting E: see the next entry.
  - Real-game validation of E (league 102, 15 seasons) failed on stars: level and movement were right (~89% moving 0-3), but players rated 90+ went 13 -> 0 and 80+ 104 -> 73. Aged-league realism 46 measures out (fresh 33): too few big receiving seasons, TE leaders low.
  - Late-night fixes (Oct 21), each found by checking real-game leagues player by player:
    1. **Generational odds bug.** Each prospect in the 2.5x rookie pool rolled the Generational odds, and a Generational prospect always makes the class: ~5 new Generationals a year, ~28 active after 15 seasons, and in one league every single player rated 90+ was Generational. Now `offseason.GENERATIONAL_CAREER` (10): ~0.4 new a year (`GENERATIONAL_RATE` x roster spots / 10), ~4 active like a new league's 3-7. (My first read of these runs wrongly credited fix 3 for the stars coming back; it was these extra Generationals.)
    2. **Rookie shift tapers for top prospects** (`SHIFT_TAPER_FULL` 85, `SHIFT_TAPER_TOP` 93): the full -8 up to potential 85, fading to none at 93; K/P/LS always get the full shift (their ratings run higher, so the taper spared most of them: 5-8 punters rated 90+). Why: the shift offsets the slow climb from teams keeping players who improve and cutting ones who don't, which acts on the middle of the league, not on rare top prospects. A flat -8 left ~1 non-Generational player rated 90+ per league; a gentle taper (75 -> 92) brought back stars but ~150 players rated 80+ (target ~108). Fast harness, 4 leagues x 16 seasons, later half: 11-15 non-Generational 90+ (target ~17), 2-5 Generational, 107-125 rated 80+, level within 0.7, starter spread +0.6.
    3. **Serious-injury rule** (`development.SERIOUS_INJURY_WEEKS` 8 -> 12, new `SERIOUS_INJURY_CHANCE` 0.6): a real season in the sim has ~210 injuries of 8+ weeks (13% of rostered players; 20% of stars), and each one cut the ceiling for good: a steady wear on the whole league. Now 12+ weeks, 60% of the time: ~80 lasting hits a season, about the real NFL's yearly ACL + Achilles count. Judgment call for Blake.
    4. **Peak years off by one** (`PEAK_SEASONS`): the design doc says QB 5th-13th season, RB 3rd-9th, K 1st-16th (Claude read the PDF tonight with a homemade text reader; it has only these three examples, no full table). The code compared the table against completed seasons, so decline started a season late everywhere except RB (hand-set to 3-8). Now `development.season_number` (experience + 1) and the table is in "Nth pro season": RB 3-9, K/P/LS 1-16 (P and LS follow the kicker example); other positions unchanged (Claude's estimates).
    5. **Kickers/punters/snappers leaned down every year** in real-game leagues: playing time is measured in scrimmage snaps, which they never get (share 0, "seldom used"). Specialists now skip the playing-time lean.
    6. **Same dice in every league.** The offseason and training-camp randomness was seeded by the year alone, so every league got the same development draws in, say, 2031 (and my three test leagues shared them). Each league now gets its own hidden salt (`settings["salt"]`, set at its first season; older saves get one at their next season).
    7. `SWING_SD` 1.45 -> 1.6: with fewer lasting injury hits, ~91% of players moved 0-3 points; back to Blake's 88-90%.
  - Real-game validation (leagues 101/102/104, 15 seasons each, settings as above before the K/P detail): 89.2-89.5% of players move 0-3 points of potential a year; league level steady (67.8-69.1 -> 68.1-68.4); 90+ (incl. Generational) 13-17 a season in the later years, 9-13 non-Generational at the end; 80+ 113-128 (K/P inflation then fixed); QBs 80+ 8-9; WRs 80+ 17-25; points per team game 22.9. Aged-league realism (8 seasons, frozen rosters): **41 and 40 measures out of 282** (same leagues fresh: 33, 32; earlier aged versions 46-59). The remaining misses go both ways (TE receiving a little low and QB stars fewer in one league; more 500-yard rushers), i.e., which stars a league happens to grow, not a drift.
  - After the K/P detail (fast harness, 4 leagues): K/P level -1.6 on average (was rising ~+3 in real games), K/P spread +2.1; other numbers unchanged. Official `realism_check.py --seasons 4` (fresh leagues): 23 of 282 out of range, same as the Oct 21 baseline (none of tonight's changes touch the game sim).
  - Final settings confirmed in real games (leagues 101/102/104, 15 seasons): K/P levels 72.9-76.9 -> 72.7-75.2 (no more inflation; one league -3.4, watch it); 90+ (not K/P, incl. ~4 Generational) 13-20 a season in the later years; 80+ 113-124; level steady (68.3-68.5); 89.4-89.7% moving 0-3; points per team game 22.7-22.9.
  - Fast harness note (Claude's scratchpad `devscore.py`): it now fakes playing time (by depth) and long injuries (real rates) so its results track real-game leagues; before that it missed the injury wear entirely.
- Bug fixed (Oct 21): over unmanaged offseasons the user team's depth chart could start emergency backups (a QB at TE1 in test league 104, a long snapper at FB), because the offseason moved the next listed player up when a starter left. Now when your starter at a spot leaves, the AI's best fit there becomes the starter, your other choices keep their order behind him, then the AI's backups fill the list (`offseason.update_depth_charts`).
- Team strength spread in the real-game runs: point differential per game 3.4-7.3, average ~4.8 (real NFL ~6-7); wins 1.5-3.5, average ~2.7 (real ~3.1). As the finding above says, this needs whole-team factors (coaches, front-office skill, free agency) to widen; it's Blake's design call.
- FINDING for Blake (Step 7 check "team strength should spread toward the NFL's over seasons"): it doesn't yet. Spread of point differential per game stays ~4.1-5.5 (real ~6-7) and of wins ~2.2-2.9 (real ~3.1) over 13 seasons. Individual breakouts and busts average out over 53 players. In the real NFL, team gaps come from things that hit a whole roster together: coaching (development and scheme), front-office skill (drafting, free agency, trades), cap management. Recommendation: build those as planned (coaches tilting development at their specialty; AI front offices with different scouting/drafting skill in Step 8; free agency), then recheck. Not done overnight: it's Blake's design call.

### Ratings scale, real peak years, specific injuries (Oct 21, day; Blake's requests after reading the development review)
- **Rating scale (Blake: "too many players consistently rated high... make it look like there's too many good players").** Claude measured: starters averaged 68 ("Good"), 59% of starters showed green, ~110 players 80+. Blake chose "median NFL player = 50" from a side-by-side preview. Built as a display scale (`engine/scale.py`, `shown()`): the engine keeps its internal ratings (the sim compares players to the league's average starter, and development, the draft and every tuning number were calibrated on them), and every screen shows ratings through one curve built from the real spread of rostered players' overall ratings in 8 generated leagues (`tools/build_rating_scale.py`). Same curve for every position, so positional scarcity is kept (QBs scarcest, most kickers still read "Good"). Result on screen: median player ~50, average starter 61-63, backups 38-39, ~40-70 players 80+ and ~8-14 90+ per league; a player's overall is within ~1 point of the average of his shown skills. Screens converted: roster table and report (skills, overall, potential, bars), depth chart chips and field picture, new-game team list, season bar, offseason report, player card ratings history. Game results are untouched (display only).
  - Notes for Blake: kickers, punters and snappers hold about a third of the 80+ spots (his earlier "most kickers are good" design carried over); about a quarter of backups now read "Very Bad"; out-of-position emergency fill-ins show 8-18.
  - Internal numbers still appear in Claude's tools and older build-log entries; anything for Blake's eyes should go through `scale.shown`.
- **Peak years from real players (Blake: "make it extremely accurate").** `tools/aging_curves.py` with nflverse data (rosters 1999-2025, snap counts 2013-2025, season stats 1999-2025; ~16 MB in tools/data, downloaded with Blake's OK). Delta method (each player against himself from one season to the next). Measures: share of snaps in games played (6+ games; per team game made every-down starters look like they declined ~2 seasons early because they can only lose share), EPA per play for QB/RB/WR/TE, FG% vs league by distance for K, gross yards for P. New `PEAK_SEASONS` (Nth pro season): QB 2-9, RB 2-6, FB 2-7 (est.), WR 2-6, TE 3-8, T 3-10, G 3-8, C 3-10, DE 3-9, DT 3-8, OLB/ILB 2-8, CB 2-7, S 3-8, K 1-9, P 1-14, LS 1-14 (est., like P). The FOF8 doc's examples (QB 5-13, RB 3-9, K 1-16) peak later than real players. New `DECLINE_SCALE`: kickers decline at half speed, punters/snappers at 0.35 (real kickers lose ~0.5 FG% a year at first; punters barely lose distance into season 14). Table and evidence in the comments beside `PEAK_SEASONS`.
- **Specific injuries (Blake: "specific injuries should have a higher chance to cause permanent / sometimes temporary step backs").** New `engine/injuries.py`: ~45 injury types grouped by how long they keep a player out (shaken up; 1-2 weeks: sprains, strains, concussions; 3-7: high ankle sprain, MCL sprain, broken hand...; 8-11: torn MCL, fractured ankle, Lisfranc...; 12+: torn ACL, torn Achilles, major knee, broken leg, torn labrum, torn pectoral, neck/back surgery). The sim still decides how long a player is out (calibrated), with the same random draws in the same order, so game results and games missed are unchanged; the type is chosen to fit the length. Each type has odds and sizes for: a temporary step back (he comes to camp below where he'd be and gets 70-100% of it back at the next camp), a permanent loss to his ceiling (physical skills hit hardest), and extra retirement odds. Biggest: torn Achilles (always a step back ~5, 75% a lasting ~4.5, +15% retirement; real: ~66% return, production often halved), torn ACL (85% step back ~4, 35% lasting ~2.5, +5%), neck/back surgery (+20% retirement). One real-game season: 53 torn ACLs (real ~47-62 incl. preseason/practice), 13 torn Achilles (real ~10-25). `league.season_injuries` now holds [[kind, weeks], ...]; older saves convert (12+ week injuries keep the old rule). Screens and play-by-play show the injury name ("Out: torn ACL, 9 more weeks").
- Re-tuned for both changes, checked on screen counts (`scale.shown`) and in real-game leagues: `GROWTH_SHARE` raised (0.46 at 21 down to 0.29 at 27; was 0.38-0.24: real players reach their prime by season 2-4, and with slow growth aged leagues ran short of stars, players topping out just under 90 and then aging), `ROOKIE_LEVEL_SHIFT` -8 with taper 84-92, `LONG_SD` 1.15 -> 1.05, `SWING_SD` 1.6 -> 1.3 (more veterans now decline each year, which is movement too). Tried and dropped: more randomness (1.15/1.45) and sparing more top prospects (taper 80-88): no more stars in real games, worse realism (41/58 out of range). Torn Achilles made harsher (85% chance of a lasting ~6.5) after a 5-season follow-up showed returners ending up above where they started.
- Final validation (real games, leagues 101/102/104, 15 seasons; sim as of the rushing session's 3rd-down back): ~90% of players move 0-3 points of potential a year; level steady; on screen 39-57 players 80+ and 3-15 90+ (fresh leagues 49-71 and 10-15); 22.9-23.1 points per team game. Aged-league realism (8 seasons, frozen rosters): 45 and 40 of 282 out of range (same leagues fresh, today's code: 33 and 37). Injury follow-up (5 real seasons): ~41 torn ACLs a season (18% gone right after, -3.0 overall at the next camp, +3.9 two camps later), ~12 torn Achilles (22% gone, -10.6, then -0.6). `docs/development-review.md` rewritten on the new scale for Blake.
- Not done / for Blake: whether kickers/punters/snappers should keep a third of the 80+ spots on the new scale; whether rookies showing 15-25 is fine; FB and LS peak years are estimates; starting-league veterans are generated at full ability regardless of age (a 10-year RB in a new league isn't pre-aged; the first camp ages him), worth fixing with the new, earlier peaks.
- Coordination: the rushing session (2,000-yard seasons) was editing the sim at the same time (tuning.py, game.py, gameteam.py, depth.py). Real-game validation of today's changes waits until it's idle.

### Blake's test notes, Oct 21 (evening)
- Bug fixed: "NameError: user_season_text" when the game page opened after the season ended (e.g. back from a player card): the season bar was drawn before two helper functions it uses were defined (same could happen in the playoffs). Moved them above the bar. Tested on a finished-season save: loads, and back-from-player-card works.
- **Team summary** (Blake: needs FOF8's Team Summary job, doesn't have to look the same). New first view in the Stats tab, any team (yours by default), regular season or playoffs: record, points, playoff seed/result, franchise history once seasons are archived (all-time record, playoff trips, titles), week-by-week game log (W/L, score, OT, upcoming games); team and opponent stats with league ranks (points, yards, rushes, rushing yards, YPC, pass attempts, completions, completion %, passing yards, Y/A, yards per catch, sacks, 3rd-down %, turnovers/takeaways, turnover margin; ties marked (T)); every player's stats for this team by category (passing, rushing, receiving, defense, kicking, punting, returns), names link to player cards. Engine: `stats.team_summary`, `stats.team_player_totals`, `stats.TEAM_SUMMARY_STATS`.
- **Injuries (Blake: less likely to cause lasting damage overall, more for specific injuries at specific positions, e.g. a running back's Achilles).** Base step-back and lasting-loss odds lowered (torn ACL step back 85% -> 60%, lasting 35% -> 20%; Achilles 100%/85% -> 90%/60%; others similar). Each injury has a body area (lower, upper, spine, head) and `injuries.POSITION_RISK` multiplies its odds (capped 95%), retirement push and, more gently, size by position: lower body RB 1.6, WR/CB 1.3, S/DE/OLB 1.15 ... linemen 0.8, K 0.6, P/LS 0.5; upper body QB 1.5 (throwing shoulder), OL/DT 1.3, DE 1.2 ...; spine: linemen 1.15. Judgment calls to tune by feel. 5-season follow-up: torn ACL ~-1 at next camp overall (RBs: 27% gone right after); torn Achilles ~-7 overall, RBs 3 of 4 gone (survivor -25), WR/CB -9, linemen -5.
- **Kickers, punters, snappers on the rating scale (Blake: a third of the 80+ spots realistic, or calibrate separately?).** Not realistic (the Pro Bowl takes about as many specialists per team as other positions), so they now have their own curve: a starting specialist at a given rank among starting specialists shows what a starter at that rank among all other starters shows. The main curve was rebuilt from every other position. Fresh leagues now: median 50, starters 62-63, backups 39-40, 48-60 players 80+ (4-7 of them specialists, was 16-26), 6-14 90+. `scale.shown(value, position)`; every screen passes the player's (or the depth chart spot's) position.
- Decision (Blake): rookies' development % will vary by player once the real draft exists (Step 8); the shown number will be the staff's estimate, not exact.
- Open question (Blake): FOF8-style windows (player card, team summary open as boxes over the main screen) instead of separate pages. The design rule so far was "one main view with tabs and side panels, no stacks of floating windows"; Blake now leans toward FOF8's windows "later down the line". **Decided Oct 21 (night): pop-ups, see "Pop-ups like FOF8" below.**

### Injuries rebuilt from real NFL data (Oct 21, night; Blake: "make sure the time they're out is realistic and data backed"; a Questionable / Doubtful / Out system; "download as much data as you can... any NFL data that will help the game")
- **Data downloaded (Blake's OK):** every nflverse dataset as CSV into `tools/data/nflverse/<dataset>/` (662 MB): play-by-play 1999-2025, on-field personnel (participation), Next Gen Stats, PFR advanced stats, weekly rosters (incl. injured reserve), weekly injury reports, depth charts, draft picks (career value), contracts, combine, schedules, trades, officials, player and team stats (weekly and season), snap counts, FTN charting. `tools/download_nflverse.py` re-runs safely (skips what's there). Earlier files stay in `tools/data/`.
- **What real injuries look like** (`tools/injury_data.py`; weekly injury reports joined to snap counts and injured-reserve status; 2022-25 are the most complete years, earlier years link fewer players and undercount):
  - ~820 injuries a regular season cost at least one game; ~90 player-games lost per team (players who had been playing).
  - Length (injuries in weeks 1-6, not cut short by the season's end): 1 game 32%, 2-3 games 28-30%, 4-7 games 17-19%, 8-11 games 4%, 12+ / rest of season 17%.
  - Which injuries make up each length (body parts from the reports): 1 game led by hamstrings and concussions; 2-3 hamstrings, ankles, knees; 4-7 high ankle sprains, hamstrings, knees; season-enders knees (torn ACL ~21-33 a regular season), feet, ankles, shoulders, Achilles. ~200 players a season go on injured reserve after having been playing; 60% never appeared on a report first (hurt in a game, straight to IR).
  - Game-day status: real reports list ~1,300-1,750 Questionable a season (about two-thirds play, at ~97% of their usual snaps), ~150-220 Doubtful (1% play), ~1,000 Out. Last missed game before a return: Out 48%, Questionable 39%, Doubtful 13%; first game back still Questionable ~35%.
  - Playing while Questionable: missed the next game with the same injury 5.7% of the time (healthy players ~0%; any new injury 2.4%). Play-by-play 2016-25 (`tools/playing_hurt.py`), each player vs his own other games: QBs -4% yards per dropback (-0.06 EPA), receivers -4.5% per target (-0.08 EPA), backs -3% per carry.
- **Built:**
  - `engine/injuries.py` rewritten: the sim picks a length from the real shares (`LENGTHS`), then the specific injury from the real mix for that length (`LENGTH_MIX`, ~60 injury types by body part: "hamstring strain", "high ankle sprain", "MCL sprain", "torn MCL", "torn ACL", "Lisfranc injury"...). Season-ending injuries last the rest of the season and the playoffs. `tuning.INJURY_RATE` 0.0138 -> 0.0118 so a regular season has ~820 game-costing injuries (sim: 817-827; ACL ~28 after weighting).
  - Game-day statuses (`season.set_status`, `injury_status`): Out / Doubtful / Questionable from the real patterns, Injured reserve for injuries of 8+ weeks (real IR players aren't on the report). Knocks that cost no games are listed Questionable for the next game (he plays); a healed player is still Questionable in his first game back 35% of the time. Questionable players who play do so 4 rating points below themselves (`tuning.PLAYING_HURT`, matched to the real -4% in the sim) and have a 5.7% chance of aggravating it (1-3 weeks out). Statuses are seeded per player and week (same on reload) and saved (`league_info.injury_details`; older saves still load).
  - Screens: roster and depth chart tags Q (yellow), D (orange), OUT (red), IR (dark red); text like "Questionable: hamstring strain", "Out: high ankle sprain (about 3 more weeks)", "Injured reserve: torn ACL (out for the season)".
- **Checks:** sim per regular season 817-827 game-costing injuries, shares by length match, Doubtful ~147, Out ~840, Questionable ~1,100 (real 1,300-1,750: practice and nagging listings aren't modeled yet), Questionable players play ~61% (real 67%). Realism check (16 seasons): 30 of 282 out of range (was 24 with the old injury model); ~89 player-games lost per team. Team-sport and consistency tests: unchanged character (consistency close to real).
- **First pass, corrected:** I first targeted 557 injuries a season, averaging in early years whose data links fewer players; that made 66 measures out of range (too few injuries, starters played too much). Found it by measuring games lost per team by year (17 in 2013 rising to 95 in 2025 is the data, not real injuries changing).
- **Next (in progress):** career effects of injuries recalibrated to the studies (ACL: ~51% play the next season, 28.5% still in the league after 3; QBs ~71-92% back with little loss; backs, defensive linemen and linebackers lose the most; Achilles: 57-81% return, only 27% recover their old level, 68% higher chance the career ends, backs worst; real normal turnover for comparison: `tools/nfl_retention.py`); player-by-player aging variation measured from real careers.
- **Per-injury recovery (Blake: a flat Questionable penalty and re-aggravation rate "is dumb"; hamstrings and high ankle sprains re-aggravate, bruises are pain tolerance; the tags only say where a player is in recovery; a concussion protocol).** `tools/injury_recovery.py` (2018-25, regular players), by body part: re-aggravation after playing listed (hamstring 12.0%, groin 7.9%, foot/calf 7.7%, knee 6.3%, ankle 6.0%, ribs 5.8%, hand 5.0%, shoulder/hip 4.4%, quad 2.3%, back 1.9%, concussion 0%) vs a healthy player's 0.1-0.6% chance of a new injury there; flare-ups per game in the first 4 games back (hamstring 3.9%, calf 3.5%, foot 3.1%, ankle 2.8% ... concussion 1.1%); per-play results while listed (EPA vs his own other games), blended toward the -0.06 all-injury average by sample size. `injuries.RECOVERY` turns these into, per body part: rating points off while playing listed (hamstring 5.6, concussion 5.7, back 4.8, ankle 4.3 ... ribs 2.6, groin 2.1, hip 1.8, hand 0.8), aggravation odds, and flare-up odds for 4 games after a return (`league.recovering`, saved). Replaces the flat 4 points / 5.7%.
- **Concussion protocol:** a concussed player shows "Concussion protocol" (purple CONC tag: can't play until cleared) until he's cleared; once cleared he isn't listed Questionable. How long it takes is the real distribution (40% miss exactly one game).
- **One set of injury rules everywhere:** `engine/injury_report.py` (InjuryReport) holds the week-to-week rules (who can't play, who plays hurt and how much, aggravation, flare-ups, statuses, healing); the season loop, `tools/simlab.py` (all realism tools) and `step6_season_test.py` all use it. (Before, the realism tools had their own simple injury bookkeeping and couldn't see flare-ups or playing hurt; a run that relied on it read 42 out of range.)
- `tuning.INJURY_RATE` 0.0112: flare-ups add ~90 game-costing injuries a season, about half of which real reports count as new injuries. Results: step6 season 789 game-costing injuries, ~93 player-games lost per team; realism check (16 seasons) 31 of 282 out of range (13 of those have known reasons: kicker/punter churn, edge-rusher labels, mid-pack tackles until in-season roster moves), ~89 player-games lost per team.

### Careers: aging player by player, and what serious injuries do to careers (Oct 21-22)
- **Real yardsticks** (new tools): `tools/retention_check.py` (of players who played 25%+ of snaps in 6+ games, how many play again next season, by position group and pro season; real vs sim), `tools/aging_check.py` (regulars staying regulars), `tools/injury_careers_real.py` / `tools/injury_careers_sim.py` (season-ending injuries vs matched uninjured players at the same position, career stage and role: playing again, change in snap share the next two seasons; real 2013-24 vs simulated leagues measured the same way).
- **Finding: sim careers ended in a cliff.** Real NFL: 84-91% play again in seasons 1-6, then a gradual slide (seasons 9-10: RB 65%, LB 73%, DB 65%; 11-13: 58-78%). Sim (old): 93-96% early, then a cliff (seasons 11-13: RB 19%, LB 29%, DB 33%, WR/TE 37%). Every player at a position aged on the same schedule and retirement odds climbed steeply at a fixed age. (Early careers: the sim keeps young players too reliably; real teams churn them through cuts and signings, coming with in-season roster moves.)
- **Each player now ages his own way** (`development.aging_profile`, fixed per player, seeded by id): his prime ends up to 4 seasons earlier or later than his position's (`AGING_PEAK_SD` 2.5 seasons), he declines faster or slower (`AGING_RATE_SD` 0.35), and each year's decline varies (`AGING_YEAR_NOISE` +/-50%). His typical retirement age follows his own prime (`offseason.retire_age`). Retirement eased: `RETIRE_AGE` +1 at every position, `RETIRE_AGE_SPREAD` 1.6 -> 2.2. Fast harness, 3 leagues x 16 seasons: late-career (seasons 7+) "plays again" vs real, average gap -11 -> -0.1 points (typical gap 14 -> 7); players showing 90+ ~12 (fresh leagues 10-15).
- **Bug fixed:** with few retirements (long careers), the rookie class could be smaller than the 224 draft picks and the draft crashed. A class now always has at least 1.3 prospects per pick (`offseason.MIN_CLASS_PER_PICK`).
- **Serious injuries, real** (`tools/injury_careers_real.py`, ~490 season-ending injuries): plays again next season vs matched uninjured: RB 77% vs 94%, WR/TE 73% vs 86%, LB 78% vs 89%, DL 75% vs 82%, DB 79% vs 85%, QB 79% vs 95% (24 QBs); knees (mostly ACLs) 62% vs 87% and -8 points of snap share the next season, -5 two seasons later; shoulders -11 / -7; feet barely. Sim (old effects): injured players came back as often as healthy ones and played as much (effects far too weak).
- **Calibration in progress:** `injuries.CATALOG` retire (extra career-end chance) now from the real gaps (torn ACL / major knee 0.18, torn Achilles 0.25, Lisfranc 0.08, labrum 0.10, others 0.05-0.06); lower-body position multipliers from the real gaps by position (RB 1.55, WR/TE 1.2, LB 1.0, QB/OL 0.8, DL 0.65, DB 0.55). `injuries.CAREER_SIZE_SCALE` (all step-backs and lasting losses) being tuned against the real snap-share drops.
- **Final (this round):** `injuries.CAREER_SIZE_SCALE` 3.0 (all injury step-backs and lasting losses x3), torn ACL / major knee retire 0.22. In simulated leagues (6 x 8 seasons, real games) injured players' return rates match the real ones: season-ending QB 78% vs 79%, WR/TE 73 vs 73, DL 75 vs 75, DB 76 vs 79 (knees 69 vs 62 before the knee bump). Broken down by career stage, knee-injured players come back 13-18 points less often than healthy ones in the sim. The "matched" comparison reads smaller in the sim (-6 vs real -25) mostly because the sim's comparable healthy players leave more often than real ones (75% stay vs 87%): the same early-career roster-churn difference, which in-season roster moves should address. Real-game sim careers by pro season now slide like the real ones (seasons 9-10 "plays again": RB 62-66% vs real 65, WR/TE 73-76 vs 70, DL 72 vs 76, DB 68-69 vs 65, OL 78-80 vs 69, LB 66-67 vs 73).
- Checks: realism (16 seasons) 31 of 282 out of range (unchanged: a single season's stats don't depend on careers); fast development harness (4 leagues x 16 seasons): league level within ~1 point, ~88.7% of players move 0-3 points a year, star counts steady.
- Known gaps: real teams churn young players more (sim keeps 93-96% of young contributors vs real 84-91%), which needs in-season roster moves (Step 7 piece 6); per-position effects of upper-body and spine injuries have no usable real split yet (kept small).

### Injury history (Oct 22; Blake: like FOF8's "Past injuries: View record", to help assess injuries)
- Every injury is kept for good (`league.injury_history`, saved in the `injury_history` table): season, game ("Week 7", "Preseason week 2", "Hall of Fame Game", "Wild Card round"...), team, injury, games it cost, and how ("played through" for knocks that cost no games, "aggravated", "re-injured"). Recorded by the shared injury rules (`InjuryReport.on_injury`), so new injuries, knocks, aggravations and comeback flare-ups all appear. Older saves start with an empty history.
- Player report (roster panel) and player card: "Health" (healthy, or his status and injury) and "Past injuries: View record (N)"; hovering shows a box (Year, Game, Team, Injury, Missed: "1 game", "Played through", "Rest of season") with "<name> is currently healthy." at the bottom, closing when the mouse leaves.
- **Bug fixed (since the playoffs were added):** regular-season games against conference opponents have kind "conference", the same as the conference-championship round, so `season.is_playoff_game` counted ~4 regular-season games per team as playoff games: playoff overtime rules, January weather, and their injuries were dated after the regular season, so the injured player kept playing and the injury didn't heal on schedule. Now playoff games are identified by id (2001+). Saves from before the fix: such injuries are re-dated to last week on load (`bring_up_to_date`). The realism tools play games their own way, so the calibrated stats weren't affected; season mode was.

### New leagues: veterans start out aged, and rosters match real NFL experience (Oct 22; from the to-do: "starting-league veterans are generated at full ability regardless of age")
- **What was wrong.** A new league made every player at his peak ability, however long he'd played (a 12-year running back was as good as he'd ever been), and used one made-up experience table for every position. Measured against real NFL week-1 active rosters (2021-26, nflverse weekly rosters; `scratchpad real_exp.py`): new leagues had 16% of rostered players with 9+ seasons (real 9%), running backs 20% (real 5%), and too few young players (1-2 seasons 21% vs real 27%); QBs and kickers had too few long careers.
- **Built.** `development.pre_age(p, rng)`: a made-up veteran loses the aging he'd have had at every camp already past his own prime, by the same rules as `aging()` (his own aging profile, bumpy years, physical skills faster); called at the end of `player.generate_player`. `player.EXPERIENCE_WEIGHTS` (by position group) replaces the old table, fitted over 30 leagues (`scratchpad exp_calib.py`) so the players who make a new league's rosters match real rosters by experience within about a point at every level.
- **Result (new leagues vs real).** Roster share by experience, all positions: rookies 11.8% (real 12.3), 1-2 seasons 26.0 (27.0), 3-4 23.8 (23.0), 5-6 17.5 (17.4), 7-8 11.0 (11.1), 9-10 5.6 (5.2), 11+ 4.3 (4.0); QB 11+ 14.8% (15.6); RB 9+ 6.1% (4.7; was 20.5). Starters by experience (real: each team's top players by snaps in 2021-25, matched to rookie seasons): 1-2 seasons 22.1% (24.7; was 17.0), 9+ 12.2% (8.8; was 17.6); rookies start 6.9% of spots (real 9.6%).
- **Rating scale rebuilt** (`tools/build_rating_scale.py`, both curves), since aged veterans pulled the league's middle down (median player showed 47 on the old curve): median rostered player 50-51 again, starters 62-63, backups 40-41, 51-74 players 80+ (5-9 of them K/P/LS), 10-18 90+. Existing saves will show most ratings about 3 points higher than before (display only; no game results change).
- **Checks.** Realism (16 seasons; the generated leagues 101-103 now start aged): 26 of 282 out of range (was 31); league averages unchanged (points 22.6, YPA 7.1, YPC 4.6). Now inside: top tight end's catches, FG/punt volume, DB tackles #30. Newly just outside (all sat at the edge before): pass_yds#3 4,364 (real 4,500-4,886), rec>=80 24.8 (17-23), sacks>=10 25.8 (17-24), qb_hits#10, qb_hits>=15. Team sport test (league 101): same character (below-average QB 58 stacked/average/weak 9.8/6.2/3.2 wins; best QB on a bad roster 8.2, Generational 9.2). Consistency test not rerun (it uses the saved league, which this doesn't change). Retirements in the first offseasons steady (~190-215 a year from rosters). Game window: new game builds, roster shows aged veterans (current ~ potential past their prime).
- **Found, not fixed (pre-existing, same with the old code):** over the seasons the development and offseason systems pull every league to the same settled state, which is less realistic than the new leagues: veterans pile up (9+ seasons 15-17% of rosters by year 8-12 vs real 9%) and rookies rarely start (about 4% of starting spots vs real 10%; rookies average ~44 internal vs ~49 in a new league). On screen the median player drifts from ~51 to ~56 over the first 4-6 seasons (the old code drifted too, about half as much, because its new leagues started closer to that state). Same root as the known young-player churn gap (real teams cut and replace young players more; Step 7 piece 6, in-season roster moves) plus rookie strength (Step 8, the real draft).

### In-season roster moves: practice squads, injured reserve, signings, waivers, camp injuries (Oct 22, Step 7 piece 6; Blake: real NFL rules, "you decide, the game warns you", AI moves matched to real rates; camp injuries included)
- **Real NFL targets** (nflverse weekly rosters, snap counts and injury reports, 2022-25 unless noted; scratchpad `real_moves.py`, `real_camp.py`, `real_ir_len.py`), per team per regular season: 72 different players on the 53 (53 at a time); 24 added after week 1 (11.6 from their own practice squad, 5.6 back from reserve lists, 6.6 from outside); 11.8 moved to reserve lists; 17.7 released or sent to the practice squad; 16.4 practice squad signings from outside; ~6 game-day elevations; 63 players take an offensive or defensive snap (71 counting special teams); ~1.4 direct moves from one team's 53 to another's (claims and trades). Week 1: 7.5 on reserve lists (4.0 new since last season: IR 3.2, IR-designated 0.3, PUP 0.3, NFI 0.2; 0.8 still hurt from last season) and 1.0 newly Out/Doubtful. IR placed in weeks 1-6 (2018-25): 57% back the same season (mostly after 4-8 weeks), 29% next season, 15% never. Players who end a season on IR and play again: ~23-26% not ready for week 1, half of those miss 6+ games.
- **Rules (real NFL, `engine/transactions.py`):** 53-man roster + 16-man practice squad (2 or fewer seasons, up to 6 veterans); injured reserve frees a spot, at least 4 games, 8 returns a season (playoffs included); PUP for last season's injuries not healed at final cuts (at least 4 games); practice squad injured list; up to 2 elevations a game, 3 per player a season; released players with fewer than 4 seasons go on waivers (claims by worst record from week 4, last season's order before); injured free agents can't be signed. Checked against the CBA by the research session (rules check in its scratchpad): matches, except the items under Open below.
- **AI teams each week** (after their games; tuned to the real counts, knobs at the top of transactions.py): long injuries (4+ weeks) to IR; healed IR players back (cutting the least valuable spare player); open spots filled from their practice squad (preferred) or free agency; short-term cover when injuries leave a position below game-day needs (sign/promote now, cut a spare elsewhere); now and then a clear upgrade from free agency; waiver claims (one a week, only a clear upgrade); practice squad kept at 16 with now-and-then upgrades. Final cuts after the preseason (IR/PUP placements). A team never cuts a player it just added.
- **Your team** (Blake's choices): nothing happens to your 53 on its own; the Roster tab shows a to-do (open spots, players who could go on IR, positions short for game day, players who can come off IR). If you sim ahead and your team can't legally take the field, your staff makes the smallest moves that fix it (IR for long injuries, then the best player at each short position) and the Roster tab lists them. Blake (via the research session): "practice squad management should be able to be hands off... if they don't want to", then "deciding that staff manages it. and if you only partially do it, the staff will finish it automatically. default should be you managing it but there should be an easy to use/access way to set it to staff mode". Built as a switch on your Roster tab, "I run my practice squad" (default) / "Staff runs my practice squad" (`league.settings["ps_staff"]`). Your mode: your choices are never overridden, but what you leave undone the staff finishes when the week is simmed (empty spots filled, injured players to the practice squad injured list, call-ups when you're short on game day). Staff mode: also swaps in better young players now and then and elevates for depth like an AI team. Your 53 stays yours either way. Tested both (8 weeks each): your mode refilled 3 spots you'd emptied, no swaps; staff mode also elevated 5 times. Blake then: "in staff mode let the staff also make short-term injury signings on my 53, like AI teams do": staff mode now also runs the AI's injury moves on your 53 (long injuries to IR and back, open spots filled, short-term cover with a spare player let go; `injured_reserve_week`, `injury_cover`), but never the AI's performance upgrades. The IR part was needed: with IR left to you, the staff's cover signings churned twice the AI rate (17 cuts in 10 weeks); with it, 6 (AI teams 6.4). Toggle now reads "Staff runs practice squad + injury cover".
- **Staff check-in (Blake: FOF8's staff sometimes cut a really good player mid-season, e.g. for cap space; "a staff check in... before they make a decision that seems larger than normal").** Your staff never cuts an important player on its own: a starter or first backup at his position, one of the team's 25 most valuable players, a captain, or a round 1-3 pick in his first 3 seasons (`transactions.important_reason`; judgment calls). If the only way to make a move would cut one, it asks instead: a "Your staff wants to check with you first" box on the Roster tab ("Release CB Frank Watson (one of the team's best players) to sign a S while injuries leave the position thin?") with Approve / Decline; nothing happens until you answer; unanswered requests drop when the week is played. Tested: 10 normal weeks, 4 staff cuts, 0 important; forced test (everyone important), 5 requests, approve worked. The same check-in is the place for cap-driven moves once contracts exist.
- **Practice squad mix** matched to real squads (research session, `docs/research/real_movement_summary.md`): WR 3 (was 2), DE 1 (was 2); real squads also carry a QB only ~59% of the time and a C ~50% (the template keeps one of each; a squad may run one under).
- **Roster tab:** views 53-man roster / Practice squad / Reserve lists / Moves (every move this season; your team's, or every team's from the Free agents view); counts line (53, practice squad, IR returns used); player panel buttons: Place on injured reserve, Activate, Release (waived or released by the rules), Sign to the 53, Sign to practice squad, Promote to the 53, Elevate for this week's game / Cancel elevation; each move explains itself and says plainly why one isn't allowed. Status tags IR and PUP; player card and report show "(practice squad)", "(injured reserve)". Saved: practice squads, reserve lists, waivers, IR returns, elevations and every move (new `transactions` table). Older saves get practice squads from their free-agent pool when loaded (their pools are thin, so some squads are weak; new leagues keep the best young leftovers for them).
- **Training camp injuries (Blake: include camp injuries):** practice injuries every week of camp and the preseason (`injuries.CAMP_INJURIES_PER_TEAM` 4.0 per team over 6 weeks; same length/injury mix as games; durability matters), on top of preseason-game injuries (~6.2 a team, 19% to starters, who barely play); one more week of healing between the last preseason game and week 1 (the real calendar has ~2 weeks). Week 1 in real-game seasons: 2.4-3.9 on reserve lists (real 4.0 new + 0.8 carryover; real camps have 90 players, ours 69), 1.0-1.6 newly Out/Doubtful (real 1.0). The injury history shows them as "Training camp".
- **Long injuries carry into next season:** about 1 in 11 "season" injuries lasts 12-16 weeks (back late in the year if there's time); the rest end the season, and next year a player hurt that way misses the start with the real odds (by when he was hurt; knees and Achilles slower, concussions rarely), on PUP if 4+ games.
- **Results (real-game seasons, scratchpad `moves_test.py`, leagues 101-105, 1-3 seasons each), per team per season, real in brackets:** on the 53 at some point 66-72 (72); added after week 1 20-25 (24): own practice squad 12-15 (11.6), back from reserve 4-6 (5.6), outside 3-8 (6.6); to IR 9-10 (11.8; real counts also suspensions and other lists); released/waived 12-17 (17.7); practice squad signings 17-22 (16.4); elevations 4.4-5.9 (6); waiver claims 1.2-2.8 (~1.4 with trades); defenders with a tackle 26-28 (31.6; was 23.9 with frozen rosters); players with an offensive or defensive snap 52-57 (63: sim teams still rotate fewer players, a game-sim usage matter).
- **Realism tools now play by the same rules** (`tools/simlab.py`, `step6_season_test.py`: camp, preseason injuries, final cuts, weekly AI moves, elevations; rosters restored after each season). 16-season realism check: 22 and 26 of 282 out of range in two runs (26 before; run-to-run noise is ±3-4). League averages unchanged (points 22.5-22.6, YPA 7.1, YPC 4.6). Fixed by the churn, as predicted: tackles #75/#100/100+, DB tackles 80+/100+ (tackles 80+ 125 -> 114-118, real 91-110, still high). Player-games missed per team ~122 (was ~85: camp injuries and IR players now count; real 2024 95-254, also counting camp and IR). Consistency test: season-to-season changes still match real (RB carries 10-15% vs 14%, edge sacks 29-33% vs 33%, tackles 11-13% vs 13%). Team sport test not affected (single games).
- **Open (for Blake):** (1) Game-day actives: real teams dress 48 (with 8 offensive linemen; else 47) plus an emergency third QB, the game dresses 46: changing it affects stats, needs your OK and a realism rerun. (2) Real IR returns may be 8 in the regular season plus 2 for playoff teams (sources conflict; kept 8). (3) Not built yet: other teams signing your practice squad players (real ~1 a team-season), trades and the trade deadline (vested veterans go through waivers after it), contracts and money (Free agency step). Research for the coming steps, from a helper session the same day, kept in `docs/research/`: the CBA rules check of transactions.py, real contracts (OverTheCap via nflverse, May 2022 snapshot: pay ladders by position as % of the cap, contract lengths, guarantees, rookie scale, cap history), real draft value (AV-based pick value chart, outcomes by pick band and by position and round, TEs by round, combine distributions), real draft AI behavior for Step 8 (Blake: "i want the draft to just feel absolutely accurate and real"; `real_draft_ai_summary.md`: board rank vs actual pick by range, how a recent high pick at a position lowers the next one, QB-need rules (no team with a young good starter took a QB in rounds 1-2), draft-day trades priced at the Jimmy Johnson chart while movers-up overpay 30-50% by outcomes, older prospects underperform their slot, which combine results matter beyond draft slot, team drafting skill persists), and offseason movement (`real_movement_summary.md`: 66% same team / 24% new team / 10% out of the league from one season to the next, by experience and position; real practice squad mixes). (4) The veteran pile-up over many seasons (see the entry above) still needs a long real-game run with roster moves to see how much this closes.

### Game day: 48 actives and the emergency third QB (Oct 22; Blake: "switch game day to the real NFL's 48 actives (47 without 8 offensive linemen) plus the emergency third QB, and rerun the realism checks")
- `engine/roster_rules.py`: teams dress 48 when 8+ offensive linemen are among them (real teams do nearly every week: nflverse 2022-25, 48.0 active, 6.3 inactive a team-week), else 47 (was 46). The AI keeps 8 linemen active when it has 8 healthy. Elevated practice squad players count toward it (teams with fewer than 48 healthy now elevate to reach it).
- Emergency third QB (real rule since 2023): the best healthy inactive QB; he can come in only once both active quarterbacks are out for the game (`GameTeam.available`). Tested: not available before, available and on the field after both active QBs went out.
- Position counts tab explains both. No recenter: who dresses changed, not how ratings turn into matchup edges.
- Checks: realism (16 seasons) 19 of 282 out of range (the previous two runs 22 and 26); league averages unchanged (points 22.6, YPA 7.1, YPC 4.6); dt_sacks>=6, sacks>=10, qb_hits#10/#20/>=15 and pd#1 back inside. Watch: fumbles lost +14.5% (before roster moves +7.6%; backups playing more may fumble more; one run). Team sport test (league 101): same character (below-average QB stacked/average/weak 10.3/6.5/3.4 wins; best QB on a bad roster 8.6, Generational 9.0). Consistency test: season-to-season changes still match real (tackles 13% vs 13%, WR targets 12% vs 12%, RB YPC 0.39 vs 0.39, edge sacks 36% vs 33%).

### Overnight Oct 22-23 (Blake asleep: "grind out whatever you have planned... keep tuning this game to be absolutely perfect"; side sessions: draft engine, free agency/contracts, news hub, each in its own new files)
- **Multi-season check (Step 7's last item; 3 leagues x 8 seasons, real games, full season loop):** team strength spread (point differential per game, 1 SD) 4.4-5.4 by season, rising slowly (real 2016-25: 4.8-6.6, average ~6.0; wins SD real ~3.1, game ~2.7-2.9). Veterans pile up (9+ seasons 9.6% -> 15% of rosters by season 8; real 9.2%); rookies start ~2% of games (real ~9.6%). See the two items below.
- **Fumbles fixed (Blake asked to look).** The league total was right but the split wasn't (nflverse weekly player stats 2021-25, per team game): QBs fumbled 0.22 (real 0.53: 0.30 on sacks, 51% lost; 0.24 on runs, scrambles and fumbled snaps/handoffs, ~30% lost); backs/receivers 0.86 (real 0.39; 0.74% of carries, 1.03% of catches), i.e. ball carriers ~3x too often, all with one 49% recovery share. Now (`tuning.FUMBLE_RUN` 0.0205 -> 0.0056, new `FUMBLE_QB_RUN` 0.015, new `FUMBLE_EXCHANGE` 0.0072 per designed run = a fumbled snap/handoff credited to the QB, `FUMBLE_CATCH` 0.0115 -> 0.0090, `FUMBLE_SACK` 0.08 -> 0.115, `FUMBLE_LOST_BY_KIND` sack 0.51 / QB 0.30 / run 0.60 / catch 0.53): QB 0.53-0.57, backs/receivers 9.4-10.2 per 1000 touches (real ~9.2), fumbles lost 0.49 (real 0.51). Return fumbles were never counted in stats: now the returner's fumbles and his team's fumbles lost include them, as the NFL counts them; return fumble rates set from play-by-play 2023-25 (`KR_FUMBLE` 1.7% of kick returns, 52% lost; `PR_MUFF` 3.5% of handled punts, 37% lost). Realism check after: fumbles lost -0.7% vs real (was +14.5%), 25 of 282 out of range (normal run-to-run range 19-26), league averages unchanged.
- **Kick returns are not a bug:** the game has ~4.0 kick returns a team game; real 2023 1.25, 2024 1.69, 2025 3.82 (touchbacks 73% -> 64% -> 21% with the 2024 and 2025 kickoff rules). The game plays 2025 rules, so it matches; only the 2023-25 average benchmark misleads.
- **Why veterans pile up (measured; nflverse weekly rosters 2018-25, `scratchpad real_stay.py`):** of players on a 53/IR in the last regular-season week, the share on a 53/IR the next week 1 is about 74% at 0-5 seasons, 69-71% at 6-7, 58-64% at 8+; the game keeps ~91% of young players, ~82% at 5-7, ~70% at 8+ (`scratchpad movement_fast.py`, stand-in game results). So nobody churns, and the league ages. Main cause: rookie classes are made ~8 points weaker on purpose (`offseason.ROOKIE_LEVEL_SHIFT`, added Oct 21 to stop development lifting the league's level), so rookies rarely beat incumbents (rookies 7.4% of week-1 rosters vs real 12.3%). Proper fix: realistic rookie classes (the draft session got these targets) plus holding the league level another way. Interim changes in `offseason.cut_to_53`: this year's picks are kept more by round (`DRAFT_KEEP` R1 16 ... R7 4, `UDFA_KEEP` 2; was +5 for R1-3 only) and veterans past 6 seasons cost a little (`VET_COST_K` 1.0 per season, a stand-in for their higher pay until contracts exist; replace with real cap cost). Fast harness, 8 offseasons: rookies 10.8% of rosters (was 8.4), 9+ year veterans end at 15.3% (was 18.2). Tried and rejected: a keep bonus for young players who ended last season on the 53 (real young players churn more than the game's, not less).
- **Corners play sides (`tuning.CB_SIDE_SWAP` 0.35, `plays._assign_coverage`).** The top receiver was always covered by the top corner (man and zone), like a permanent shadow corner: each team's #1 in passes defensed ran 14.2 (real 12.7) and 15+ PD seasons 15.8 a season (real 9.8). Now 35% of pass plays put the top receiver across from the #2 corner. 16 seasons: #1 13.7, 15+ PD 13.9 (still high, better), pd#1 22.3 (real 20.8); receiving leaders up to about real (rec#1 135 vs 133, rec_yds#1 1,843 vs 1,811; rec>=80 and wr_rec_yds>=1300 now just above the range); league averages unchanged; 25 of 282 out of range.
- **Rust (Blake wanted it; FOF8 had it): real data shows none measurable.** Per-play EPA vs the player's own other games (nflverse weekly stats 2018-25, `scratchpad real_rust.py`): first game back after missing 3+ games QB +0.04, RB +0.04, WR +0.04, TE +0.23 (players return when ready; no rust); later games mixed and noisy; week 1 vs the rest of the season -0.02 to +0.02. Not built. **For Blake:** skip rust (data), or add a small one for feel?
- **Snap shares (`scratchpad snapshare.py`, real snap counts 2023-25):** the top WR / TE play more of the snaps in games they play than real (WR1 sim 0.91+ of the season's snaps vs real 0.81 per game played; TE1 0.82+ vs 0.71; CB1 0.94 vs 0.91); availability itself is real (real top players play 92-95% of games). Not changed: targets by rank within a team already match real (0.24/0.18/0.14...), so stats are right; more receiver rotation would mean re-tuning the targeting. Defensive line/linebacker snap shares can't be compared directly (real 3-4 teams list edge rushers as OLB).
- **News and social media hub (Version 1 scope; built by a side session tonight, wired in here).** New News tab (between Playoffs and Game): written news stories (game stories, big performances, milestones and records, injuries and weekly injury reports, roster moves, playoff race, draft, retirements, season review, captains) and a social feed (insiders, beat writers, a stat desk, fans, players), all built from what the league already records (nothing new saved; old saves get news right away; same league = same words every time). Never invents facts, never mentions ratings (hidden or shown). Filters: league / your team / any team / one player, by story type and season. `engine/news.py`, `screens/news_page.py`, `tools/news_check.py`. Tested: full season + offseason, 4,253 stories and 2,903 posts built in ~0.5 s, passes its automatic checks; the tab opens in the game window with no errors. **Blake's choices are listed in `docs/news-design.md` (all PROVISIONAL)**: weekly standouts vs official awards (should the league have Player of the Week / MVP / All-Pro?), the invented social personas, fan tone, player posts, how much news league-wide, what counts as a big game.
- **Long field goals (news session's tip) checked, fine:** per team season, 60+ yard attempts sim 0.53 vs real 0.48 (pbp 2023-25; make rates within small-sample noise). The real difference runs the other way: real kickers try more 50-59 yarders (7.6 vs the game's 5.6, while total attempts match), a mild shape difference logged for later.
- **Contracts, salary cap and free agency (built by a side session tonight; wired in here; all of Blake's choices PROVISIONAL, see `docs/free-agency-design.md`).** Real CBA accounting (`engine/contracts.py`: cap by year from real history, $301.2M in 2026; minimum salaries by experience; signing bonus proration; dead money; June 1 cuts; rookie scale; franchise tag; RFA tenders; fifth-year options), prices from the real pay ladders (`engine/cap_market.py`: a good player never comes cheap), and AI teams' league year and 7-wave market (`engine/free_agency.py`). Wiring: new League fields and saves (`contracts`, `contract_log` tables; cap history/carryover/charges/June 1/fa in league_info); new leagues get realistic contracts (`initial_contracts`); older saves get them on load; the offseason now runs in the real order (retirements, new league year with re-signings/extensions/tags/cap cuts, free agency before the draft, draft + rookie deals, undrafted, free agency after the draft, cut to 53 with money, practice squads, getting under the cap); `cut_to_53` uses the real cap cost of cutting a player (`free_agency.cut_money_value`) instead of last night's `VET_COST_K` stand-in; every week after the moves `season.money_week` gives every signing its contract and every release its cap charge and gets AI teams under the cap; AI signings, claims and promotions check the cap (`transactions.can_afford`); your Sign/Promote buttons refuse moves that would put you over; elevated players are paid. **Your team, until the contract screens exist (PROVISIONAL):** your staff re-signs your expiring players before the market opens: important players (starter/first backup, top-25, captain, recent high pick) at their asking price whenever it fits under the cap, others the way AI teams decide; logged as staff moves. Anything bigger (restructures, cap cuts) comes to you as an Approve/Decline request on the Roster tab ("Restructure WR X's contract ... to save $18.3M of cap room this season?"); cap requests stay until you answer. Player report and card now show each contract ("$12.5M a year through 2029 (3 seasons left of a 4-year deal signed in 2026, $20.0M guaranteed); cap hit this season $10.1M").
  - Checks: end to end (new league, a real season, the offseason, the next preseason): 0 AI team-weeks over the cap, every team at 53, no player without a contract, save/load identical; offseason ~9 s; 704 signings with new teams, 262 re-signings, 62 extensions, 15 tags. The side session's own check (12 offseasons): AI teams never over the cap; minimum-level deals 81% (real 76-87%), one-year deals 76% (real 75-80%), pay ladder within ~10%, positional spending and rookie scale on target. Movement (fast harness, 8 offseasons): kept on a 53 from one season to the next 71% (real ~72%); rookies 13.2-13.5% of rosters (real 12.3); 9+ year veterans ~9% for 6 seasons, 12.8-13.6% by season 8 (before free agency 15-18%; real ~9). Realism (16 seasons): 22 of 282 out of range; league averages unchanged; tackles>=80 now inside.
  - Not done: the contract and free agency screens (your negotiations, a free agent board); in-season the game doesn't stop you from starting the season over the cap (it asks you to fix it).
- **Fixed (found by the draft session):** (1) after free agency was wired in, the offseason could end with a team at 52 (getting under the cap for week 1 cut players after rosters were filled); now it refills to 53 with minimum deals and re-checks (`run_offseason`, up to 4 passes); 4 leagues x 6 offseasons clean. (2) A talent leak: when no eligible free agent was left, made-up "street free agents" were generated at full starting-league level (~60 a year; after 15 seasons 16.5% of starters were these players, part of why rookie classes needed the -8 shift). They now come from `draft.street_free_agent` at undrafted-rookie level (practice squad fills, emergency signings, `fill_short_positions`). Pending with the draft: undrafted signings by scouting boards instead of true ratings (`draft.sign_undrafted`; with true ratings 19% of starters were undrafted vs real 12.9%).
- **The real draft (Step 8; built by a side session tonight, wired in at the very end; Blake's choices PROVISIONAL in `docs/draft-design.md`).** `run_offseason` now uses `draft.make_class` (classes built so pick-band outcomes match real), `draft.run_draft` (AI boards with real scouting error, needs, QB rules, draft-day trades) and `draft.sign_undrafted` (by the teams' boards, not true ratings); AI depth charts give recent high picks a real chance (`draft.draft_capital` via `depth.auto_depth_chart(..., pick_of)`; not for your chart). The side session's results (6 leagues x 22 seasons, with free agency): picks 1-5 become 3+ year starters 90% (real 93%), rookies 8% of starters (real 8%), ~19.6 trades a draft (real ~18.4), league level steady; gaps: rounds 4-7 and undrafted players start a bit too rarely; 9+ year veterans 13% (real 9%). Smoke test here (an older finished-season save): offseason with the real draft runs in ~10 s, 224 picks, every team 53, no missing contracts, saves fine; that one draft had 41 trades (watch: the side session's average is ~20). **Not yet done:** the full realism check, team sport and consistency tests and `tools/draft_check.py` after this wiring; deleting the old placeholder draft code; your team drafting (the engine supports it: `DraftRoom` with your picks, trade offers; screens not built); the startup draft option on the New game screen (`draft.startup_draft`).

### Awards and the Pro Bowl (Oct 23; side session. Blake: "absolutely there should be a player of the week, mvp, all pro team, and a fan voted pro bowl (which maybe that one can have a slight impact on players with stronger personalities who play well... it should be realistic and shouldnt be high personality players who barely play or suck getting voted into it). It would even be fun to add a pro bowl game afc vs nfc ... and have there be substitutes for the players that are playing in the glory bowl the next week. I really want this news page to feel really real and fleshed out and amazing.")
- **What's handed out, on the real NFL's calendar** (`engine/awards.py`, rules only): Players of the Week after every regular-season week, Offensive / Defensive / Special Teams for each conference (the NFL's set); Players of the Month (same three per conference, plus league-wide Offensive and Defensive Rookie of the Month; December includes the January games, as the NFL does); the media's MVP watch (top five every other week from Week 6); Pro Bowl rosters announced after the next-to-last week; All-Pro first and second teams at the start of the playoffs; Pro Bowl replacements once the Glory Bowl teams are set; the Pro Bowl game in the week off before the Glory Bowl (`playoffs.ROUND_NAMES[4]` is now "Pro Bowl week"); the season awards (MVP, Offensive and Defensive Player of the Year, Offensive and Defensive Rookie of the Year, Comeback Player of the Year) the week of the Glory Bowl, like the NFL Honors, voted on the regular season only; the Glory Bowl MVP right after the game. **No Coach of the Year**: coaches don't exist yet (add it with the staff system).
- **Voting (technical calls, all knobs at the top of awards.py):** a 50-member media panel. Season awards: each voter ranks five, 10-5-3-2-1 points (the AP's ballot since 2022). Voters see the season's stats turned into one number (offense in yards: 20 a touchdown, minus 45 a turnover; defense: sacks, takeaways, tackles for loss, passes defensed, QB hits, tackles), compared with what the league's best season at that position usually is (`REF`, measured in the game's own seasons), weighted by how voters value each position for each award (`WEIGHT`), plus team success (MVP leans hard on winning: win%, playoffs, No. 1 seed), games missed, and voter fatigue for last year's MVP. Every player's season gets its own media "story" and every voter sees it a little differently, so close races go either way and unanimous votes are rare. A linebacker who mostly rushes the passer is judged as an edge rusher (like real 3-4 outside linebackers). Voters count a quarterback's rushing yards at 35% and add a bit for touchdown passes (otherwise running quarterbacks won almost every MVP). All-Pro: each voter picks a first and a second team at every AP position (LT/LG/C/RG/RT by the player's depth chart side, 3 edge rushers, 2 interior linemen, 2 linebackers, 2 corners, a slot corner from the NB spot, 2 safeties, all-purpose, K, P, KR, PR, special teamer, long snapper); linemen and long snappers are judged mostly on "film" (their current overall rating, which is shown on screens, standing in for what watching them shows); at quarterback the panel votes the way it votes for MVP. Earlier All-Pro/Pro Bowl nods help a little (voters remember). Hidden ratings are never used.
- **Pro Bowl (Blake's fan vote):** fans, players and coaches each count for a third (the real NFL's method), 44 per conference at the real Pro Bowl's counts (QB 3, RB 3, FB 1, WR 4, TE 2, T 3, G 3, C 2, DE 3, DT 3, OLB 3, ILB 2, CB 4, S 3, K, P, LS, return specialist, special teamer); starters are the top vote-getters.
