Fans go more by the numbers and by fame (popularity) and team success; coaches more by film; players in between. **To get in at all** a player must have played at least 45% of his team's snaps (fullbacks 20%) in at least half its games AND be above the typical regular at his position: nobody who barely plays or plays badly can be voted in, however popular. **Blake's personality boost (PROVISIONAL):** in the fan vote, players who play well get a boost that grows with how well they play and with how big a personality they are (popularity 70%, leadership 30%); it changes about 1.4 of the 88 picks a season (measured by re-voting without it), so it's slight, as asked. Alternates are kept in order (4 per spot). Replacements, once the Glory Bowl teams are known: Glory Bowl players, injured players, and players who choose not to play (PROVISIONAL: 15% chance, more for veterans past 5 seasons and QBs, up to 40%; news only says "will not play", no invented reasons); alternates step in, then the next in line if alternates run out. In test seasons about 28 of 88 were replaced (8.5 Glory Bowl, 7.7 injured, 11.4 not playing); the real NFL often replaces 40+, so this may be a bit low.
- **The Pro Bowl game (Blake, Oct 23: keep it a tackle game, and Pro Bowl injuries never carry over).** Built as the classic tackle Pro Bowl (Eastern vs Western Conference; the real NFL played tackle through 2022 and switched to the flag-football "Pro Bowl Games" in 2023). Recommended tackle because the game sim plays real football and a flag game would need a new sim. Played with the real game sim on a small stand-in league of the two rosters, at a rotating warm/dome site (never the Glory Bowl's), with the "dress rehearsal" playing-time plan (starters the first half, then the backups). It never counts in season or career stats (kept apart in `league.awards`, not in the box scores the stats read), and **its injuries don't carry over** (Blake decided). Offensive and Defensive MVP of the game. Scores came out normal (about 40-70 combined); real Pro Bowls were higher-scoring because nobody tackled hard.
- **Saved** as one JSON entry `awards` in the save's league_info (`database.py`); older saves start with no awards, and the season catches up on the next sim (Players of the Week for weeks already played, etc.).
- **Screens:** new **Awards** tab (after News): Season awards (winner, his season, the top five in the vote with first-place votes and points; before they're announced, the latest MVP watch), All-Pro (first and second teams with first-team votes), Pro Bowl (game result, MVPs and top performers; each conference's roster with starters, who's out and why, who was added, alternates), Players of the Week and Month, History (every season's winners, Glory Bowl MVP, champion). Player card: "Awards and honors" (every honor by season); player report: an Honors line ("MVP (2026) · First-team All-Pro · 2x Pro Bowl · 3x Player of the Week"). Playoffs tab: the Pro Bowl score. Season bar in the week off: "Pro Bowl week (no game), then the Glory Bowl".
- **News** (`engine/news_awards.py`, story type "Awards and honors"; `news.py` only calls it, so the news-hub session's file stays its own): Players of the Week per conference (each player's game in words), Players of the Month, MVP watch with an insider's "ballot if the votes were due today", Pro Bowl rosters per conference, each team's Pro Bowlers, Pro Bowl snubs (first alternates with the strongest cases, with their stats), Pro Bowl changes ("X replaces Y, who will play in Glory Bowl I / is injured / will not play"), the All-Pro teams with unanimous choices, each All-Pro, the Pro Bowl game, each season award with the vote ("He received 38 of 50 first-place votes and 432 points... Next: ...", rookies with their draft slot, comebacks with what they came back from), an awards roundup, the Glory Bowl MVP; social posts from the stat desk, insiders, the players (thanks, a snubbed player's "Noted.") and fans. The outlet's own "Week N standouts" now only appear for weeks without official Players of the Week (older saves). One season gives ~150 award stories; no template gaps, no hidden-rating words, no repeated headlines.
- **Checked against real history** (`tools/awards_check.py`, real winners in `tools/real_awards.py`: every AP winner 2000-2025 with stats and team records from the nflverse files; 2025 winners confirmed: MVP Stafford, OPOY Smith-Njigba, DPOY Garrett, OROY McMillan, DROY Schwesinger, CPOY McCaffrey). 17 test seasons (8 leagues x 1 season, 3 leagues x 3 seasons through the real season loop and offseason), game vs real:
  - MVP: QB 94% / RB 6% (real 85 / 15); team win% .792 (.799); made playoffs 100% (100%); QB MVPs 4,707 yards, 34.6 TD, 9.2 INT (4,547 / 40.0 / 8.6) but 513 rushing yards (250: the game's best quarterbacks run more); the MVP is the first-team All-Pro QB 82% (real: almost always); winner's first-place votes avg 39 of 50, 2 unanimous in 17 (real: 2 since 2000).
  - Offensive Player of the Year: QB 41% / RB 35% / WR 24% (real 35 / 50 / 15); team win% .661 (.737); also MVP 18% (real 42% overall, 20% since 2016).
  - Defensive Player of the Year: edge 59%, S 18%, CB 12%, LB 12% (real edge 46, LB 19, S 12, CB 12, DT 12); edge winners 19.4 sacks, 5.0 forced fumbles (19.1 / 5.1); team win% .644 (.652).
  - Offensive Rookie: QB 41 / RB 29 / WR 29% (42 / 35 / 23). Defensive Rookie: edge 41 / LB 29 / CB 24 / S 6% (LB 42 / edge 35 / DT 12 / CB 12).
  - Comeback (only from a league's second season: it needs last season on record): 6 winners, QB 50% (real 63%), all injury comebacks (real 67% injury / 33% revival). A revival needs an earlier good season on record (otherwise second-year breakouts were winning it), so revivals only become possible from a league's third season.
  - Players of the Week: offense QB 56% / RB 30% / WR 14%, defense DE 57%, special teams K 68% / P 17% / returners; ~85-92% from the winning team (no real breakdown on file; judgment).
  - All-Pro first teams come from about 20 different teams a season, at most 3-4 from one team. Pro Bowl: 44 per conference; picks average 1.4 standard deviations above the typical regular at their position; 52% from playoff teams.
- **Gaps / for later:** small samples (17 seasons) at the top of the leaderboards; RB MVPs a bit rare and QB OPOYs a bit common; no Coach of the Year; no Walter Payton-style or "Protector of the Year" awards; Pro Bowl fan votes are shown as ranks, not vote counts; rookie quarterbacks in the game post bigger seasons than real rookie OROY winners (4,315 yards vs 3,661: a draft/development matter, not voting). One finding for the sim: a few tight end seasons of 1,500-1,650 yards (real record 1,416). `app.py` accepts `--port=<n>` so two test copies can run at once (launch config "awards-test").
- **Decided (Blake, Oct 23):** the Pro Bowl stays a tackle game, and its injuries never carry over. **Still PROVISIONAL for Blake:** (1) The personality boost's size (about 1-2 of 88 picks a season) and what counts as a "strong personality" (popularity + leadership). (2) How often Pro Bowlers skip it (15%+). (3) Names: "Pro Bowl" and "All-Pro" are used as-is (like "Glory Bowl", they could get the game's own names).

### Training camp, 90-man rosters and one cut to 53 (Oct 23; Blake: "shouldnt it be late free agency, then training camp, then pre season, then the cuts happen around/during the pre season every week right?" Claude checked: since 2023 the NFL has ONE cut-down, 90 to 53, after the last preseason game, with small moves every week before it; Blake: "Build it the modern way")
- **Before:** the offseason cut every team to 53 and formed practice squads right after the draft, before training camp, so camp and the preseason were played with 53 + practice squads.
- **Now (real NFL order):** the offseason ends with **90-man camp rosters** (`transactions.fill_camp_rosters`: over 90 after the draft, a team lets its least valuable players go, never this year's picks or, for your team, an important player; under 90, it signs cheap free agents at its thinnest positions, `roster_rules.CAMP_TEMPLATE`, judgment from typical real camps). Camp and the preseason are played with the full camp roster (everyone healthy dresses in preseason games, so the 3rd string really plays the 4th quarter; the old note "one starting lineman plays all game" is gone). Every week (`transactions.camp_week`): a player out for the season goes on injured reserve (real rule: placed on IR before the final cut he can't return that year; other injured players are carried to final cuts), open spots are filled, and now and then a camp body is swapped for a better free agent. Waiver claims in camp are judged against the team's 53-man level at the position.
- **Final cuts** (`transactions.final_cuts`, after the last preseason game): players out 4+ weeks go on injured reserve (back after 4 games) or PUP (last season's injuries); every team cuts to 53 (AI: each position to its roster count, `offseason.template_cuts`/`keep_value`, the rules the old cut used); AI teams claim cut players who'd clearly help (one each, same position); practice squads are signed (each team's own cuts first); short positions filled; everyone gets under the cap with every contract counting; the free-agent pool keeps its best 260. A test league: 1,149 cuts, 26 claims (real cut-down days: a few dozen), every team at 53 with a 16-man squad, no missing contracts, nobody over the cap. Two full seasons with offseasons ran clean.
- **Your team:** the Roster tab shows "Training camp roster: N of 90", a to-do line with how many cuts are left, and a **CUT** tag on each player your staff would cut; **Keep at final cuts** (and Remove keep mark) on any player protects him (your staff cuts someone else). Whatever you haven't cut by final cuts, your staff cuts (never an important player while anyone else can go), and it signs your practice squad from your cuts (you can change it in week 1). In camp you can sign up to 90, release anyone (injured: an injury settlement), and place a player on injured reserve (out for the season); practice squad moves wait for final cuts. In staff mode the staff also fills open camp spots and moves season-ending injuries to IR. Tested in the app: CUT/KEEP tags, keep marks changing the list, final cuts keeping the kept player, 53 + 16 after cuts, Moves view, save/load in camp.
- **New leagues start at camp too** (`create_league(camp=True)`: the practice squads and camp bodies join each 53). Tools that play games without a preseason use `camp=False` (`recenter.py`, `team_sport_test.py`, `build_rating_scale.py`, `draft_lab.py`'s first season); `simlab.play_season` makes the final cuts in its tool preseason and now also restores money, captains and the free-agent pool after each season.
- **Fixed on the way (a real bug):** the offseason ran while the league still said "season complete", so the money rules counted all 18 weeks of the new season as paid: every offseason cut charged a vested veteran's whole salary as dead money, and the top-51 rule was off. The offseason now runs as its own stage. `free_agency.final_compliance` uses the top-51 rule until the regular season (a 90-man camp isn't counted in full).
- **Talent leak found and fixed:** at the plain street-free-agent level, the ~390 made-up camp bodies a year became 25% of all starters after 12 seasons and the league's talent crept up ~4 points (on-screen median 52 -> 60). Real starters who went undrafted: 8-11% (nflverse 2023-25, 50%+ snaps, not K/P/LS). Made-up camp bodies are now long shots (`transactions.CAMP_BODY_SHIFT` 20; real free agents and the draft class's undrafted tail come first): undrafted share of starters 10.4%, level steady (22-season draft check: starters 68-69, median 52-54, as before camp rosters). Changing the order of development and the cut made no difference.
- **22-season draft check after the fix:** R1/R2 start 3+ seasons 83/57% (real 78/58), rookies 8% of starters (8%), 9+ year veterans 12% (real 9; was 13). Still off and handed to the draft session: teams draft too few WRs (19.7 a draft vs real 28.6; it was 23.9 before camp rosters) and too many DE/LB, and over 22 seasons WR/CB starters drift down ~3 points and QB/DE up ~3-4; R3-R5 start a bit too rarely; draft trades 17.1 a draft (real scaled 18.4).
- Realism (16 seasons) with camp rosters: 27 of 282 out of range (13 with known reasons), league averages unchanged.
- Also: the old placeholder draft code is deleted from `offseason.py` (the real draft replaced it); `tools/free_agency_check.py` uses `engine.draft`. The News tab is now **Bitter** (the news session's rename, Blake's name).
- **Open:** camp churn beyond injury replacements is rare (~1 swap a week league-wide: once camps are full, the free agents left aren't better); no real camp transaction data on file to tune against.

### Draft position mix re-tuned for 90-man camps (Oct 23; draft session, at the main session's request)
- **Why the mix was off:** each position's draft class was sized by its "openings" on the rosters at draft time (roster count minus the position's spots). With 90-man camps the rosters then hold last year's practice squads and pre-draft free agents, so a WR room looked full: WR classes had 12-17 players (one league's 2030-31 drafts) and teams drafted 19.7 WRs a draft (real 28.6), while DE, S and OLB classes ballooned (DE 26.8 drafted vs 18.1, LB 35 vs 26). Openings at draft time no longer measure real need. (Script: draft session scratchpad `plan_diag.py`.)
- **Fix (`engine/draft.py` only):** classes now follow the real NFL's mix of new players by position: real picks per draft 2014-26 (nflverse; generic OL/DB/LB/DL split by the real position mix and the roster template; `REAL_PICKS`) divided by each position's drafted share (`colleges.DRAFT_BY_POSITION`), so undrafted entrants count too (most K/LS/FB). A gentle nudge remains toward real roster shortages (`OPENINGS_K` 1.0, limited to x0.75-1.35). Class size `CLASS_PER_PICK` 1.4 per pick plus the 40% undrafted fringe, as before. With the real mix, QBs piled up (17 a class; QB starters +8 points over 22 seasons, since QBs last long in this game), so the QB class is x0.65 (`POS_CLASS`). The per-position class levels (`POS_LEVEL`) were re-set so every position holds its level, and position values nudged (RB a bit up, DE/OLB/CB/S a bit down; WR/DE recent-pick need slightly weaker; K/P a little earlier).
- **Result (`tools/draft_check.py`, 6 leagues x 22 seasons, sim | real):** picks per draft WR 26.8 | 28.6, DE 20.5 | 18.1, LB 29.4 | 26.0, DB 47.2 | 45.4, OL 38.3 | 38.5, DT 20.5 | 21.4, TE 12.4 | 12.9, QB 10.4 | 10.1, RB 16.7 | 18.7. Every position's starters stay within ~2 points over 22 seasons (was WR 66->63, CB 66->63, G 64->62, QB 69->73, DE 71->74); league level steady (starters 68, median 52-55). Draft trades 19.3 a draft (real 18.4 scaled; was 17.1). Unchanged and still real: picks 1-5 start 3+ seasons 90% (93), R1/R2 83/59% (78/58), rookies 8% of starters (8), the QB rules (0% with a young good starter), need effects (WR 32% normally, 15% after a recent 1st-rounder; real 32/16).
- **Still off:** R3-R5 start 3+ seasons 37/24/13% (real 38/25/20: R5+ and undrafted players start a bit too rarely, the churn gap); 9+ year veterans 12% (9); kickers/punters drafted 1.9 a draft (3.9); older prospects (24+) still underperform their slot more than real (0.70 vs 0.88); front-office skill a bit too lasting (0.68 vs 0.53); players shown 80+ end around 35-53 per league (fresh leagues 62-78), 90+ 7-24 (10-20).

### A packaged copy to send to a friend (Oct 21; Blake: "would i be able to send a version of the game in its current state for my friend to look at?")
- **What:** `tools/build_exe.ps1` packages the game with PyInstaller (the NiceGUI way, like `nicegui-pack`) into a folder with `FrontOfficeFootball.exe` and zips it to `dist/FrontOfficeFootball-<date>.zip` (about 33 MB). The friend needs no Python or add-ons: unzip, double-click the .exe. Needs Windows and WebView2 (built into Windows 11 and up-to-date Windows 10). Unsigned, so Windows SmartScreen may warn ("More info" -> "Run anyway").
- **Run it again for a newer copy:** `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File tools\build_exe.ps1`. Temporary build files go to `C:\Users\<you>\FOF-build` (outside OneDrive). Blake's saves are never included.
- **Code changes for it:** `engine/saves.py` puts saves in a `saves` folder next to the .exe when packaged (unchanged when run with `python app.py`); `app.py` calls `multiprocessing.freeze_support()`, gives the console-less .exe somewhere to send log output, and the game window now uses any free port (`native.find_open_port()`) instead of always 8080.
- **Tested (Oct 21):** unzipped to a fresh folder and launched: the game window opened. In browser mode (same .exe, `--browser`): main menu with no saves, built a new league, picked a team, simmed the Hall of Fame week and preseason week 1 (scores shown), saved; the save appeared in the `saves` folder next to the .exe. Not tested on a PC without Python (only Blake's).
- **Oct 21, first report from the friend:** double-clicking showed "a little loading bar and then nothing"; his AI said the game ran but the window failed; it worked when started from PowerShell (exact command not known yet). Likely cause (unconfirmed): double-clicking the .exe inside the zip without extracting it (Windows unpacks just that one file, without its `_internal` folder, so it can't start). Added anyway: the packaged game checks for WebView2 (`app.webview2_installed`, Microsoft's registry key) and opens in the web browser if it's missing; browser mode in the packaged game uses any free port; each zip has "Play in browser (if the window won't open).bat"; `build_exe.ps1 -ErrorWindow` makes `dist/FrontOfficeFootball-ErrorWindow-<date>.zip`, a copy with a text window and "START HERE - shows errors.bat" that keeps any error message on screen. Retested on Blake's PC: window opens, browser mode serves the menu.

### Receivers and tight ends rotate like real ones; personnel, depth chart backups, hot pass rushers (Oct 23; Blake on the morning summary's item 5: "im down for retuning if it makes it more realistic and doesnt hurt the fun aspect of the game")
- **Measured first** (real: nflverse snap counts and participation 2023-25; sim: 2 leagues, `scratchpad snapshare_pg.py`, `sim_personnel.py`, `real_personnel2.py`). Share of snaps in the games he played, real vs before: WR1 0.81 vs 0.92, WR2 0.71 vs 0.83, WR5 0.33 vs 0.21, TE1 0.71 vs 0.85, TE3 0.28 vs 0.11. Three causes, fixed in turn:
  1. **Personnel** (`coach.choose_personnel`): every situation's grouping mix set to the real one, measured in exactly the game's own situations (its hurry-up rules cover 17.1% of real plays vs the game's 18.5%). Before, short yardage went 2 backs / 3 TEs about half the time (real 11 personnel 42-61%) and hurry-up offenses went 3-4 wide 91% (real 85%). Now 11/12/21/13/22/10 = 64.6/20.8/7.7/3.8/1.9/1.3% overall.
  2. **Depth chart backups** (`depth.auto_depth_chart`): situational starters (2nd/3rd TE, 4th WR, fullback, nickel/dime backs) now back up the base spots. Before, a team's other TEs were never listed behind its starting TE, so when he needed a breather a running back or receiver came in (TE spots were 15% non-TEs). Tight-end rotation brings in another TE first (`gameteam._fill`).
  3. **Rotation** (`tuning.ROTATION`): WR1 0.11 (+0.23 to the second backup), WR2 0.10 (+0.20), TE1 0.18 (+0.12), TE2 0.10; receivers' rotation shrinks faster with the talent gap (`ROTATION_GAP_K_WR` 0.5: stars play 90%+, an ordinary #1 about 80%). Now WR 0.82/0.75/0.58/0.40/0.27 and TE 0.73/0.38/0.19 vs real 0.81/0.71/0.59/0.45/0.33 and 0.71/0.45/0.28.
- **Throws follow the player, not the spot** (`plays.py` target pick, `gameteam.target_role`): a backup filling the top receiver's spot while he rests no longer inherits his share of throws (rotation alone cut the league's top receiver from ~1,810 to ~1,560 yards). Starters' pull rebalanced (`TARGET_ROLE_BIAS` WR1 0.48, WR2 0.34, WR3 0.16, TE1 -0.08, a receiver with no starting role -0.15), and the in-game brakes on feeding one receiver start later (`FED_COVER_FROM` 6 -> 8, `TARGET_SATURATION` 10 -> 13: 12-catch games were 3.5 a season vs real 15.8).
- **Hot pass rushers** (`HOT_RUSHER_K` 0.6, `HOT_RUSHER_CLEANUP` 0.1): a rusher who already has sacks or hits today draws the slide and the chip, and more of the next sacks are clean-ups by someone else. 3-sack games 32.5 -> 21 a season (real 16.4, range 10-23), 4-sack games 6.5 -> 3 (real 2, 0-4); season sack leaders back in range.
- **New tool** `tools/game_extremes_check.py`: one-game spikes per season vs real (weekly stats 2021-25). Final: 400-yard passing games 6.3 (real 8.8), 5-TD games 6.0 (4.8), 150-yard receiving 33.5 (26), 200-yard 4.5 (2.6), 12+ catches 12.8 (15.8), 3+ sacks 21 (16.4), 2+ INTs 24 (21). **Still high: 12+ tackle games 187 a season (real 99), 15+ 33 (17).** Solo tackles match real (36.0 a team game vs 36.2) and assists run 30 vs 25, but trimming assists pushed the season leaders below real while mid-pack totals stayed high, so it was put back: the gap is game-to-game spread, open for later (the news session flagged it too).
- **Checks:** realism (16 seasons) 23 of 282 out of range (camp rosters before this: 27; worst point during the work: 45), league averages unchanged (points 22.7, YPA 7.0, completion 64.8%), top receiver 1,754 yards (real 1,811), targets#1 185 (183). Team-sport test: supporting cast still matters as before (a 58-rated QB 10.3 wins with a stacked cast, 3.5 with a weak one). Consistency test unchanged (season-to-season repeat: WR yards per target 0.96 vs real 0.93). No recenter needed: who's on the field and who's targeted changed, not how ratings turn into matchup edges.
- Rust: dropped (Blake, Oct 23: "it makes the game too overcomplicated and doesnt make it more fun just more annoying"; real data showed none either).
- **Decided (Blake, Oct 23, on the morning summary):** (1) rust is dropped; (2) "user should control contracts unless the user is missing a significant amount of players and needs to start signing": your staff should stop re-signing your players on its own once the contract screens exist (for now it still re-signs your important expiring players, because there's no screen to do it yourself yet, so without it you'd lose them all); (3) yes to official awards, a fan-voted Pro Bowl and a Pro Bowl game (built by the awards session); (5) retune receiver rotation if it's more realistic (done above). The Testing rule (Blake): Claude tests the game itself in the app instead of asking Blake to; Blake fine-tunes how it feels once the base is there.

### Bitter, storylines and the draft's position mix (Oct 23, side sessions; details and every PROVISIONAL choice in `docs/news-design.md` and `docs/draft-design.md`)
- **Bitter** (news session, Blake's idea: "organized as if it's a social media page. Let's call it Bitter instead of Twitter"): the News tab is now a social media site where invented outlets, insiders, team accounts, a hot-take host, a receipts account, fantasy and stat accounts, celebrities, players (some with bold, cryptic or weird personalities, random, never from ratings) and fans post "bits"; news stories are bits with an article card. Tone PG-13 (Blake). Storylines (`engine/storylines.py`: streaks, turnarounds, collapses, breakouts, slumps) and a phrase library (`engine/news_words.py`) keep wording fresh across a season. Final cuts, camp and offseason moves become one league-wide roll-up each. Tool: `tools/big_games_real.py`.
- **Draft position mix re-tuned for 90-man camps** (draft session, `engine/draft.py`): classes had been sized by roster openings at draft time, and with camp rosters the rooms looked full (WR classes shrank). Classes now follow the real mix of new players by position. Drafted per draft: WR 26.8 (real 28.6), DE 20.5 (18.1), LB 29.4 (26.0), DB 47.2 (45.4); every position's starters stay within ~2 points over 22 seasons; trades 19.3 a draft. Still a bit off: round 5 picks start 3+ seasons 13% (real 20), 9+ year veterans 12% (real 9), K/P drafted 1.9 (3.9).
- Sim findings passed on by the news session, for the realism work: 15+ tackle games run ~2x real (confirmed by `tools/game_extremes_check.py`, open); long field goals (55+) and 4+ FG games look more common than real weekly rates (to measure).

### Playtest fixes: non-quarterbacks at QB, and sim speed (Oct 23; Blake's friend playtested: "noted slow sim times" and "he put a dt in at qb and he threw for 300 yards and played extremely unrealistic"; Blake: "please make sure all non position qbs playing qb are not doing stuff like that")
- **Out of position at QB (and anywhere):** each player's game-day rating went through a soft floor (`tuning.Z_CAP`, the same tanh that keeps stars from taking over every snap), so a defensive tackle at quarterback (passing skills ~25-35) counted as merely a bad QB, and a bad QB can throw for 300. Out of position the floor no longer applies (`gameteam.z`, down to `Z_FLOOR_OUT_OF_POSITION` -6), and the completion chance's own cap no longer hides it either (`UNTRAINED_PASSER_K` 0.30: only a passer below every real QB's level, i.e. a non-QB, is affected). 40 games each, forced in at QB for the whole game: DT 37% completions, 3.3 YPA, 67 yards, 1.45 INT (on 20 throws), 5 sacks, team scores 5 a game; guard 39%, 3.7 YPA; kicker 44%; receiver and running back ~48% (some arm and athleticism). The real starter's and backup's lines are exactly the same as before (true QBs are untouched).
- **Sim speed** (measured: `scratchpad week_timing.py`, `pre_profile.py`, `cuts_profile.py`):
  - Preseason weeks were 6-10 s (games with 90-man rosters asked for each player's rating at a spot ~1 million times a week when looking for a fresh player): ratings are now remembered for the game (`GameTeam.rating_at`). Preseason weeks ~3 s, same results.
  - Final cuts were ~15 s: waiver claims rescanned the whole league for every cut player and team, and ran a full salary-cap check ~40,000 times; practice squads rebuilt the free-agent pool for every pick. Now team lists are built once and cap figures worked out once (`free_agency.AffordCheck`, same answers as `affordable`: 1,440 of 1,440 test pairs agree). Final cuts ~4 s.
  - Bitter rebuilds the whole season's news each time (~1 s by week 4, several seconds by the playoffs); it now rebuilds only while its tab is open and catches up when you open it (`screens/main_page.py`).
  - Regular-season weeks were already ~2.5 s (16 full games). In the app: Sim week 2.9 s on the Roster tab, 3.2 s with Bitter open (both include the screen refresh).
- Realism after these fixes (16 seasons): 26 of 282 out of range (the run before: 23; the usual band is 19-26, and the measures that differ come and go between runs), league averages unchanged (points 22.8, YPA 7.1, completion 64.9%).

### Step-by-step offseason and the Contracts tab (Oct 23; Blake: you control your own contracts; "Phases, Next button", "Offer, counter, patience", "Rough interest", "Routine only")
- **The offseason is now phases with a Next button** (`offseason.offseason_start` / `offseason_next`, `season.begin_offseason` / `advance_offseason`; saved in the new `league.offseason`, a league_info key, so you can save and load mid-offseason): (1) start of the league year: AI teams decide their expiring players, yours wait for you; (2) free agency waves 1-4 (you place offers before each; Next plays the wave out); (3) the draft (your staff picks for now); (4) waves 5-7; (5) training camp opens (90-man rosters, development, captains, the preseason schedule). "Start the 2027 offseason" replaces "Start the 2027 season" at the end of a season; during the offseason the season bar shows the phase and its Next button, and the game opens on the Contracts tab. Pressing Next at the start of the league year with important players unsigned asks first (it lists them). Tools still run the whole offseason in one call (`run_offseason`, `season.start_next_season`).
- **Your staff no longer re-signs your players** (`free_agency.staff_resign_pending` is no longer called): routine only is automatic (rookie deals, practice squad reserve/future deals, camp bodies).
- **Contracts tab** (`screens/contracts_page.py`): **Payroll** (every contract: deal type, through, per year, cap hit now and next year, dead money and savings if cut; click for the year-by-year table), the cap line (cap, used, room, standing offers, dead money, next year's commitments); **Re-sign** (your expiring players: status, asking price and years, your staff's advice in words; click to negotiate, tag or tender: lowball = rejected, close = a counter with his ask, three lowballs and he stops talking); **Free agency** (the board: the best 250 or one position, asking price this wave, "Several / A few / No other teams" interested, your offer; make, change or withdraw an offer; he picks among all offers when the wave plays out).
- **Market changes for your offers** (`free_agency.market`): your offer is weighed with your years and guarantees and signs on your terms, and only if it still fits under your cap. Offers can't take your offseason roster over 90 or past your cap room (counting your other standing offers).
- **Fixed on the way:** (1) signing a free agent from the Roster tab before week 1 gave him the minimum whoever he was (an exploit): in camp and the preseason he now gets his in-season asking price, and in the offseason free agents sign only through the board; (2) an offer at exactly a player's ask could count as below it (rounding of the guarantee to whole $K).
- **Tested:** engine (re-sign lowball/counter/accept, tag, save/load in the re-sign phase, offers in a wave: 5 of 10 at-ask offers signed with you, 5 elsewhere; all phases through camp, 224 picks, 90-man rosters); in the app (Start offseason, re-sign at the ask, the unsigned-players warning, the free agency board, an offer standing, every phase's Next at ~0.3 s, camp opening into the 2027 preseason, the offseason report with its new free agency sections, no server errors). The one-click path tools use still runs clean (a full season, offseason, and the next final cuts).
- **Not built yet:** extensions during the season, your fifth-year option decisions, June 1 designations and restructures from the screen, your own draft picks (draft session's screens).
- 22-season draft check after the offseason split (draft_lab runs every phase in one call): level steady (starters 68.0-68.5 in the late seasons, on-screen median 54), every position's starters within ~2 points of the first season.

### Your contract controls finished: extensions, fifth-year options, restructures and June 1 cuts (Oct 23; side session; Blake: "user should control contracts unless the user is missing a significant amount of players and needs to start signing")
- **Extensions (Payroll view, any time: season, end of season, offseason).** Click one of your players: if he can be extended you see what he's asking (per year, new years, guaranteed), type an offer, and a confirmation shows the deal year by year with this season's cap hit before and after and your room after. Same offer / counter / patience as re-signing. This season's salary stays; the new years start next season (they replace whatever is left of his current deal; old bonus money keeps counting where it was), and the new signing bonus is spread from this season on, so part of it counts now (real rule). Real rules: a drafted rookie's deal can't be redone until after his 3rd season; a tagged player can sign long-term only until camp opens. Engine: `free_agency.extension_check`, `extension_ask`, `extension_preview`, `extension_offer` (built on the AI's `_extend`, which now takes your exact terms).
- **Fifth-year options (Re-sign view).** Your first-rounders entering year 4 appear there with the option year, the salary, the level (starter or basic) and your staff's advice; Exercise or Decline, with a confirmation. A reminder sits at the top of the Contracts tab until you decide. **Deadline: the draft** (real: early May, right after the draft): an option you haven't exercised by then is declined and logged. Your options used to be skipped completely. Engine: `options_for_you`, `decide_option`, `lapse_your_options`.
- **Restructure and cut (Payroll view).** Restructure: pick how much of this season's salary turns into bonus (up to his salary above the minimum); the panel shows, live, the cap room it frees this season, every later season's cap hit before/after, and the dead money if he were cut afterwards; a confirmation repeats it. Release: dead money by season and room saved, both as a normal cut and as a **post-June 1 designation** when one is left (2 a spring) and it changes something; your staff says which is better (`contracts.best_release`). Once camp opens every cut splits its dead money on its own (it's after June 1). The cut goes through the same roster move as the Roster tab's Release (waivers for players under 4 seasons). Engine: `contracts.restructure_preview`, `free_agency.restructure_check`, `your_restructure`, `release_choices`, `release_block`, `your_release`.
- **Real-rule fixes in `contracts.py`:** (1) **fifth-year option salaries were about twice the real level** (a starting QB's option was ~20% of the cap): now the 2020 CBA levels, the average of the 3rd-20th biggest cap numbers at the position for a regular starter and 3rd-25th otherwise (real 2024 options set in 2023: QB playing time 10.3% of the cap, basic 9.0%; the game now 12.3% / 10.2%, WR 9.0% / 8.3%, CB 6.3% / 5.6%). (2) Cuts in **training camp and the preseason** are after June 1, so their dead money now splits between this season and next (it all hit this season before). (3) **In-season restructures** can only convert salary not yet paid (before: all of it, even weeks already paid). (4) **Carryover bug (big):** a player retiring in the spring took the salary he'd already been paid off last season's books, so it came back as unused room and carried over, **about $30M a team every year** (one test league: $4-66M a team, every team). Now his paid salary stays on that season; carryover equals each team's real leftover room exactly.
- **Bugs found while testing (`free_agency.py`):** (1) **An exploit: any roster move during the re-sign window** (a Release on the Roster tab, say) **signed all your expiring players to 1-year minimum deals** (the money safety net, `sync`, saw them on your roster without contracts; 23 players in the test). Fixed: they're left for you to decide. (2) An offer at exactly the ask a player had just named could get a counter repeating the same number: disloyal players wanted more than their own ask from their own team, and the screens round money to $10K. Fixed (loyalty only softens an ask now; $12K of rounding slack, `OFFER_ROUNDING`). This also affects re-signing and free agency offers.
- **Your staff only steps in when you're short of players (Blake's rule), checked everywhere:** fixed one place: your **tagged player** got a long-term deal from your staff about 45% of the time (`tagged_long_term`, now AI only; you can extend him yourself until camp). Checked and fine: start of the league year (your options, extensions, re-signings, cap cuts and getting under the cap are all skipped for your team; only practice squad reserve/future deals are automatic), free agency (only your own offers), getting under the cap (your staff asks, `request_cap_moves`), after the draft (AI only), `staff_minimum` (only when you can't field a legal team). Roster moves outside contracts that your staff still makes on its own (not changed, not in my files): at final cuts it cuts whoever you haven't (the 53 limit) and forms your practice squad unless you've started it; when camp opens it adds camp bodies and, **if you're over 90, releases your least valuable non-important players without asking** (`transactions.fill_camp_rosters`; for the main session).
- **AI money check** (`tools/free_agency_check.py`, 3 leagues x 4 offseasons). Finding: the tool still runs the offseason as "preseason" with the old cut-down, so it no longer matches the game (it reports 31 AI team-weeks over the cap at week 1 with or without today's changes; the game's own final cuts check every contract). A copy with the game's real stage order (scratchpad `make_flow_tool.py`) gives 0 over the cap before and after. Today's changes, before -> after: options exercised 21 -> 22 a season (real ~13-16), tags 8.8 -> 10.5 (real 6-14), big deals (5%+) 95 -> 85 (real 80), deals at about the minimum 77 -> 78% (real 76-87), week-1 payrolls 97.6 -> 97.8% of the cap (lowest team 84% -> 93%), pay ladder closer at the middle rungs (WR #32 5.8 -> 5.0%, real 4.8). Worse: market deals with a new team 41 -> 36% (real ~50-57) and players moving to a new team 21.7 -> 18.8% (real 23.8); the market had been leaning on the phantom carryover money. This belongs to Blake's open item 5 (tune the role-player market) and now starts from correct money. `tools/free_agency_report.txt` was rewritten by the plain tool run.
- **Tested in the app** (contracts-test save, New Orleans, end of 2026): extension at season's end (confirmation table, accepted at the ask, Payroll shows Extension through 2029); start of the offseason: option reminder, option rows in Re-sign, exercised one (reminder updates, contract runs through 2028); restructure of $3M with the live preview; post-June 1 release (dead money split 2027/2028, room up by exactly the amount shown); free agency waves 1-4, the draft (another session's new live draft), the untouched option lapsed right after the draft; camp: cut preview splits automatically, no June 1 button; regular season week 3: restructure limited to unpaid salary, an in-season extension; save and reload kept everything (no missing contracts, nobody over the cap). Engine script (scratchpad `contract_controls_test.py`): lowball/counter/patience (4th low offer: "done talking until next league year"), years too short refused, cap room refused when the new bonus doesn't fit, tagged player stays on the tag past camp.
- **PROVISIONAL for Blake (how it feels):** (1) you can extend a player with 1 or 2 contract years left (`EXTEND_YEARS_LEFT`); (2) a market-level player in his last year who'd rather test free agency won't talk about an extension that season, about 27% at average loyalty, decided once a season (`EXT_REFUSE_K`; loyal players and rookie-deal players almost always talk); (3) patience: 3 low offers per player per league year, no limit on how many players you talk to (`EXT_PATIENCE`); (4) the option deadline is the draft; (5) undrafted rookies can be extended after 2 seasons (judgment; I'm not sure of the real rule for undrafted deals); (6) his extension ask is his market price plus the usual re-signing premium (less if he's loyal).
- **Known gaps / for later:** Pro Bowl option levels (one Pro Bowl = transition tag, two = franchise tag) aren't used yet though the Pro Bowl exists now; a waived player claimed by another team still leaves all his guaranteed money as your dead money (real: the claiming team takes over the salary; the Roster tab's Release works the same way); AI teams exercise more options than real teams (22 vs ~13-16 a season); the contract kind still says "Rookie" for a player in his option year; `docs/free-agency-design.md` "Still open" still lists these features as not built.
- **AI cap economy in startup-draft leagues (Oct 24; main session's integration run: NSH $38M over at week 1 in 2030, down to 11 players, a game with no QB; "dead money" 2028 $103M; two QBs paid 38% of its cap).** Findings: (1) the collapse itself was `_comply` cutting and refilling minimum players round after round (fixed by the main session: no practice squad cuts, a cut only counts what it saves beyond a minimum replacement); replaying that save with the fix, NSH starts 2030 week 1 $4.9M under. (2) The "$103M dead money" for past seasons is mostly not cuts: since the carryover fix (above), a retiring player's already-paid salary is booked to the season just finished (about 9-11% of the cap a team a year, the league's ~150 rostered retirements), which counts for carryover only. Current-season dead money is what matters. (3) The two-QB bill: NSH's $82.7M QB was still on his startup contract, whose later years had been swollen by the new-league restructures, and in 2029 the AI extended its new starting QB for $92M without looking at either number: **AI extensions never checked the cap**. Now (`free_agency._extension_fits`) an extension's new bonus share has to fit this season with the room the team keeps (as re-signings do), and next season's commitments can't go past next season's cap (`EXTEND_NEXT_MAX` 1.0). (4) The biggest piece of real dead money is retirements: players retiring with big bonuses left (about 2.5-3.5% of the cap a team a year; a QB retiring at 30 a year into a $166M-guaranteed deal). That's the retirement model in `offseason.py` (it ignores money; real players rarely walk away from guaranteed money), for the main session. (5) Two measuring traps for tools: a league loaded from a pickle must bump `engine.player._next_id` (as `database.load_league` does), or new players reuse ids and steal old contracts (my first numbers were wrong this way: $86M "dead money" for a second-year player); and `tools/free_agency_check.py` (no games played) can't show in-season cuts.
  - **Played seasons, startup-draft league, 5 seasons in one process** (scratchpad `dead_sources.py`, every charge tagged with its cause), before -> after the extension check: league-average current-season dead money at week 2 by season 2.1/5.3/5.2/5.7/5.5% -> 1.7/4.8/5.2/4.5/4.5% (real 5-10%); worst team 8-25% -> 5-17%; AI teams over the cap at week 2: 0 every season, both; teams paying a backup QB over 8% of the cap: 0, both; next-season commitments median 59-68% of next season's cap. Where current-season dead money comes from (per team a season, after): in-season releases 2.5-3.8%, retirees' leftover bonus 2.3-2.9%, final cuts 0.3-1.1%, cap cuts 0.2-0.9%.
  - **`free_agency_check` (stage-corrected copy, 3 leagues x 4 offseasons), before -> after:** generated leagues: 0 over the cap both; dead money at week 1 4.6 -> 4.4%; extensions 58 -> 46 a season; tags 9.6 -> 12.6 (real 6-14); big deals 88 -> 88 (real 80); movement same team / new / gone 55.9/18.8/25.4 -> 55.5/19.4/25.1%. Startup-draft leagues: 0 over both; dead money 4.0 -> 4.4%; extensions 55 -> 48; big deals 85 -> 85; movement unchanged (55.1/19.0/25.9%).
- **Retirements now weigh money (Oct 24; Blake: "can you fix and work on that?").** `offseason.retirements` multiplies each player's chance to retire by `free_agency.retirement_money_factor`: the money he'd give up (this season's base salary plus guaranteed salary still to come, as a share of the cap) shrinks the odds by e for every 2% of the cap (`RETIRE_MONEY_K` 0.02: owed 3% -> 22% of his old odds, 10% -> under 1%), at full strength 3+ years before his position's usual retiring age, a quarter of it 3+ years past it (old stars still retire with money left, like Brady and Brees). Injury retirements are reduced the same way (paid players rehab). Judgment, not data: I found no real dataset of retirements by money owed. Players with little money (most retirees) are unchanged, so career lengths stay as calibrated.
  - **Results** (5 played seasons, startup-draft league, before -> after): retirements of players under contract ~190 -> ~175 a year (players with 0-4 seasons unchanged, ~55); retirees owed 3%+ of the cap 14-21 -> 3-6 a year; their leftover bonus as dead money 2.0-4.1% -> 0.9-1.6% of each team's cap a year; current-season dead money at week 2, league average 4.1-6.0% -> 3.4-4.5% (worst team 12-22% -> 8-21%, the 21% once in five seasons); 0 AI teams over the cap at week 2 in any season, both. `free_agency_check` (generated leagues): 0 over the cap; movement same / new / gone 55.5/19.4/25.1 -> 55.8/19.7/24.5% (real 65.7/23.8/10.5; slightly closer); week-1 dead money 4.4 -> 2.8%.
  - **Open, for Blake:** dead money now runs below the "roughly 5-10% of the cap" target in `tools/free_agency_check.py` (an estimate, not measured data). Real teams carry dead money mostly from cutting and trading overpaid veterans; the AI may do too little of that, or the target may be off. To measure against real team-by-team dead money before tuning.
- **Player ids after unpickling (Oct 24):** `League.__setstate__` moves `engine.player._next_id` past the highest id, as `database.load_league` already did for saves, so tools that keep a league in a .pkl file no longer give new players old players' ids (and contracts). The game itself wasn't affected (saves load through the database). The camp check failing at 95-101 players in my earlier runs came from this, not from the game.

### Draft screens: your own picks, interview scouting, the startup draft (Oct 23; draft screens session. Version 1 scope: "draft, interview-based scouting". Details and every PROVISIONAL choice: `docs/draft-design.md` section 13)
- **Blake's scouting decisions (Oct 23, asked before building):** scout in the offseason, before the draft (not all season); "fewer, harder choices": 20 interviews, 15 top-30 visits, 150 film hours (about half the real NFL's 45/30, for a class of ~440); an interview reads the person and his football mind (personality reads with some error, a character red flag 85% of the time, and it sharpens the grade a little); combine results in real units plus where each ranks at his position in this class ("4.52 sec (8th of 24 DEs)").
- **What's built:** `engine/draft_day.py` runs the draft one step at a time (the rules stay in `draft.py`); `engine/scouting.py` reworked to Blake's choices; `screens/draft_page.py` is the new **Draft tab** (Prospects / Draft room / Results; a startup draft room). The class is announced before the league year starts (the real combine is in late February) and stays in the save through free agency waves 1-4 (prospects aren't free agents). **Next: start the draft** ends scouting and the AI picks until you're on the clock (new offseason phase "drafting"). In the room: draft a player, let your staff pick, step one pick (so you can make an offer for the pick on the clock), sim to your pick, take one of up to 3 trade offers, make your own offer, or let your staff finish (= Next). **Save and load mid-draft** works (`league.draft`, a new league_info key). Tools still run the whole offseason in one call.
- **Your staff's grade** starts as good as an average NFL staff's (it shares the league's common mistake and has its own); film, cross-check, visit and interview each clear part of it, some always stays. Shown as ranges (truth inside about 2 times in 3): unscouted top prospects ~12-20 points wide, fully scouted ~4-6. Hidden ratings never appear (skills list skips them; durability shows only as the public medical flag).
- **Your trade offers (technical calls, PROVISIONAL):** picks in this draft only, at most 4 a side, at least full Jimmy Johnson chart value for the AI (as designed), and a team on the clock or within 5 picks of it that loves a player on the board won't move down (the same rule AI teams use with each other; without it you could buy any pick on the clock at chart value).
- **Startup draft** (New game, now the default, as Blake's main way to start): the players are generated, the round-1 order is drawn by lot (snake after), you pick your team seeing its slots, then you draft live (only the Draft tab exists until rosters do; you can't draft past a position's 53-man count; saves mid-draft). After it: depth charts, captains, practice squads, fresh contracts, 90-man camps, the schedule. `draft.startup_draft` is now a pick-by-pick `StartupRoom` with the same AI rules. Team strength spread after it: starter average SD 2.5-2.9 rating points vs 1.6-1.8 for random rosters.
- **Fixed on the way:** a prospect's college was re-drawn on draft day by the round he went in (you'd scout him at one school, draft him from another): now set once at the class's creation by where the media expects him to go. Prospects were counted in the free-agent pay ladders (`cap_market.active_players`).
- **Making the class earlier changed the draft's long-run numbers until corrected** (`tools/draft_check.py`, 6 leagues x 22 seasons, run before and after each step). With the class built at the league year's start, the roster openings that size each position's class differ from draft day (practice squads still on rosters, expiring deals not gone): K/P/LS/FB classes ~25% bigger, QB/TE/CB 7-14% smaller (measured over 18 offseasons), and K/P/LS starters crept up 3-4 points while QB starters slipped 2. Fix: `draft.CLASS_EARLY` size factors (only for the early class) and AI boards re-measured on draft day (`draft.regrade`, same scouting opinions). Final check, before -> after (real): picks 1-5 start 3+ seasons 91 -> 89% (93), R1/R2/R3 83/60/36 -> 80/61/37% (78/58/38), rookies 8 -> 9% of starters (8), 9+ year veterans 12 -> 10% (9), trades 19.4 -> 19.5 a draft (18.4 scaled), QB rule 0% with a young good starter (0), front-office skill odd/even 0.71 -> 0.63 (0.53), every position's starters within 1-2 points of the first season (QB 69->69, K 75->75, P 76->77, LS 76->76; before the fix QB 69->67, K/P/LS +3-4). Still low as before: WRs drafted 25.7 a draft (real 28.6), K/P 1.6 (3.9), R5 starters 14% (20).
- **Tested in the app** (port 8097, saves in this session's scratchpad): start of the offseason, the Draft tab during free agency, film/cross-check/visit/interview on a prospect (his range went from 70-89 to 66-71, budget counts down, table marks update), waves 1-4, start of the draft (stops at your pick, "You're on the clock"), accepting a trade-down offer, an offer refused by a team on the clock, Next pick, Sim to my pick, drafting a player yourself, Needs, Picks, save mid-draft + reload into the same spot, Staff finishes the draft (rookies signed, on to wave 5), Results. Startup draft: New game -> Startup draft -> team table with slots -> live picks (staff pick, your own pick, sim to your pick) -> save/reload mid-draft -> staff finishes -> camp (90-man rosters) -> preseason -> final cuts -> regular season week 1 played. No server errors. Engine scripts (scratchpad `test_draft_flow.py`, `test_startup_flow.py`, `test_trade.py`): no prospect ever in the free-agent pool, every pick under contract after the draft, 90-man camps, nobody over the cap after a startup draft.
- **Known gaps / for later:** once drafted, your rookies show true ratings on the Roster tab like every player (the staff-evaluation display is still to come); scouting is offseason-only (Blake chose it; in-season scouting could come with the staff system); the startup draft's veteran view is the screens' usual true ratings; no future picks in trades (D3); for other sessions: right after the draft AI teams occasionally release a just-drafted rookie to get under the cap (`free_agency._after_rookies`, 2 in one test draft); some AI teams chain-trade down many times in one draft (totals still ~19-20 a draft, real 18.4 scaled).
- **Startup draft QB fix (Oct 23, after the main session's integration pass found teams starting QBs rated 27-48 who took their first QB in rounds 16-23).** The startup grading compared each player only with his own position, so a team saw a good guard and a good QB alike and waited on QB (6 test leagues: 112 of 192 teams took their first QB after round 5, as late as round 25; 8 started a QB under 45). Now teams value a pick like real team-building: what he adds over the starter they'd be stuck with if they waited (the worst starter-level player left at his position, counting every team's open starting spots, never assumed better than the 8th best left), times how much the position matters (the depth chart's spot importance: QB 3.0, LT 1.3, most 1.0; K 0.3, P 0.25, LS 0.1 because a rating point there moves games far less); a backup is worth a quarter of a starter, and no team takes a backup good enough to start for a team that still needs a starter there; a team still without a QB in round 4 took the best one on its board (replaced the next day, below). Your staff's picks and its "would take" hint follow the same rules. Results (same 6 leagues): every team has its QB by round 4; starters under 45: 3-5 of 192; the 10th-percentile starter 52, about the pool's 32nd best QB (52-56), so the low ones are front offices whose scouting misjudged their QB; rounds 1-3 go mostly QB, T, WR, DE, CB, no K/P/LS (first K ~round 22, P 23, LS 38); team strength spread (starter average SD) 2.5 rating points vs 2.8 before and 1.6-1.8 for random rosters. Check script: scratchpad `startup_qb.py` of the draft screens session. Note: a generated league isn't fully set by its seed (it depends on what ran before in the process), so compare with the same set of seeds.
- **The round-4 QB rule softened (Oct 22; Blake: "if there are alot of really bad qbs available they shouldnt be reaching that hard for it... Thats fine if a team takes first qb in round 12 if it makes sense").** The data agreed: the hard rule changed when teams took their QB, not how good he was (with and without it: starters under 45 3-4 of 192, median 66, 10th percentile 52). Now a team without a QB gets 5% keener on one each round after round 3, only for a QB worth more than waiting (`STARTUP_QB_EAGER`, `STARTUP_QB_EAGER_FROM`). Same 6 leagues: latest first QB round 9-14 (hard rule: 4; no rule: 14-18), 39 of 192 teams take theirs after round 5, starters under 45 4 of 192, median 66, 10th percentile 52, starter-average SD 2.54.

### Scouting you can see: staff impressions, scout's notes, two scouts, cloud bars and a skill web (Oct 22; Blake, with FOF8 screenshots: after scouting "the blue bars would shrink (how much depends on your staff...)" and it was "revealed whether your coaching staff thinks the player is very underrated, underrated, as scouted, hard to read, overrated, very overrated... or brainstorm some really cool fun better ways")
- **Blake's choices:** all four ideas (staff impression, shrinking bars, scout's notes, two scouts + round projections); the impression shows after film study; it can be wrong, better staffs less; scout-slang wording; fuzzy cloud bars plus a skill web; "i dont want the number or range next to it, youre supposed to visually guess... it should have what the skill its representing but just dont spoil it"; a report card after the draft comes later with the staff system.
- **Staff impression** (`scouting.impression`; words in the new `engine/scouting_words.py`): Hidden gem / Sleeper / Fair value / Wild card / Overhyped / Fool's gold. It compares your staff's view with what a player at his spot on the big boards usually turns out to be (`scouting.media_worth`: big boards regress to the mean, so the media's top prospects are on average overrated; compared with the raw rank, 36-42% of the media's top 250 came out Fool's gold, correctly but uselessly). Wild card = raw (ceiling far above his ability now, more so for 21-year-olds) until a top-30 visit. Measured (one class, 6 staff/scouting combinations, `scratchpad impression_check.py`): whole class fully scouted, average staff: gem 7%, sleeper 20%, fair 45%, overhyped 15%, fool's gold 13%; Wild card 14% after film. How often it's right (he truly beats or trails his media spot): weak staff after film about a coin flip (44-58%), fully scouted 70-88%; average staff 59-76% / 85-100%; elite staff 95-100% / 94-100%. Weak staffs also see more Hidden gems (17% of the top 250) and are wrong about half.
- **Two scouts and round projections** (`scouting.projections`, `projected_pick`, `round_text`): "Big boards: Late 1st · Your staff: Mid 2nd"; after film and before a cross-check your area scout and national scout each give one (they sit apart by the cross-check's part of the mistake; the cross-check settles them).
- **Scout's notes** (`scouting.notes`): film notes (his two best skills and his worst, vs typical prospects at his position in this class, with a small misread that shrinks with the cross-check and visit; 51 skills each with two strength and two weakness lines, worded for receivers and backs where blocking is concerned; tape vs stopwatch: "Plays faster than he timed" / "Timed fast, plays slower"; a Wild card line), and after the interview notes on the person ("Natural leader: teammates follow him", "Runs hot and cold"... and the background check). Same lines every time you open him.
- **Screens** (`screens/draft_page.py`, built on the board redesign): a Staff take column on the board (sortable); the selected card shows the impression and projections; the report pop-up: the impression chip and projections in the header, the skill web (shaded band = his ability now, dashed line = ceiling) over **fuzzy cloud bars** (one per skill, named, no numbers, words or number tooltips: a darker cloud for now, a pale one for his ceiling, blurry when your staff is unsure, tightening into a sharp mark), both animating from the old width to the new after each scouting step; Scout's notes above the combine.
- **Tested in the app** (scouting test save, start of the 2027 league year): board column, report before and after film study (No read yet -> Wild card / Hidden gem, the two scouts' projections, notes), the clouds shrinking (e.g. Hands 14% -> 6% of the track) and the web, no server errors. The web's labels were clipped on the left; the drawing area was widened (checked by measurement; the pane was hidden for the final look).
- **No numbers on prospects' grades either (Blake, Oct 22: "take the numbers off the Now/Ceiling grades too... Show them as clouds or just the word, your call").** Claude chose clouds (a word gives the band away): the board's Now and Ceiling columns, the selected card and the report header show a small cloud on a track, no number or word (the columns still sort by your staff's best guess). Every cloud now uses one color (green for now, blue for the ceiling): coloring by rating tier would have told you the band. Tested in the app (board, selected card, report header, the web's labels no longer clipped; no server errors). Prospects you've drafted still show true ratings on the Roster tab, like every player.
- **One cloud per skill, and Developed % (Blake, Oct 22: "i dont think it needs two sections of the bar. I think what the bar is shrinking down to is closer to your staffs view of their true potential. the developed % will determine what they come into the league with their current rating at").** Each skill is now one cloud: your staff's view of his potential there (the web's band is the same thing for all skills). The board has a Potential cloud column and a Devl column; the selected card and the report header show Potential (cloud) and Developed %. Developed % is your staff's estimate (`scouting.developed`; the Oct 21 decision: the shown development % for rookies is the staff's estimate): his ability now as a share of his potential on the screen scale (so it matches what you'll see once he's yours; on the internal scale prospects read 80-97%, which looked like finished players), with a fixed mistake of about 6 points unscouted, 4 after film, 3 after a cross-check, 2 after a visit (times the staff's skill factor), never shown as 100. This class on the screen scale: 5th-95th percentile 63-96%, median 82%; the media's top 64 median 89%. Clouds are one color (blue). Tested in the app: board, selected card, report header, film study (the clouds tighten, Developed % updated 81 -> 82), no server errors.
- **Found, not mine (draft screens):** double-clicking a row opens two copies of the report, and only the newest one updates after scouting (the other goes stale).

### The Draft tab as a real draft board; Draft only in the offseason (Oct 22; side session. Blake, after a playtest: the draft should "look much better and much less like a google form"; his reference: FOF8's Amateur Draft Preview window. He chose to hide the Draft section until the offseason, since scouting only happens then; a college-season hub can come later)
- **Draft hidden outside the offseason (Blake):** the Draft section shows from the start of the offseason until training camp opens, and during a new league's startup draft. In the regular season, playoffs and preseason it's gone (`main_page.py`, one line: Draft is left out of the section list; the page still exists underneath, so links to it work whenever it shows). Side effect: past drafts' results can only be looked at in the offseason for now (player cards still show each player's draft round and year).
- **The look (`screens/draft_page.py`; screens only, `engine/` untouched):** one dense board table across the page with a green header row (hover any column name for what it means), position badges, your staff's Now and Ceiling grades as word-plus-number ranges, and the combine in real units: 40 (sec), bench (reps), vertical (inches), broad jump (feet-inches), 3-cone, shuttle, intelligence test (out of 50), position drill (out of 100). Each combine number is colored by where it ranks at his position in the class, with FOF8's colors: red = superior (best 10%), blue = great (best 25%), green = poor (worst 20%); hover for "4.35 sec (1st of 29 DEs)"; a legend line sits under the table. "Show position" dropdown and a "Marked only" box above it. On the right, FOF8's column of big buttons: the selected prospect (grade, the four scouting buttons, Full report, Mark), Begin draft, Continue draft / Pause draft, Next pick, Sim to my pick, Have staff finish draft, View draft order, Offer trade (or Trade offers (n) when you're on the clock), Team needs, and a Draft speed dropdown. In the draft room, under the table: the draft log (every pick, latest first, yours highlighted) and the draft-day trades.
- **Draft room:** a scoreboard strip for the pick on the clock (round, pick, team, "YOU ARE ON THE CLOCK" in yellow, whose pick it was, who your staff would take) and the next six picks, yours in yellow. New: **Continue draft** makes the picks one by one at the Draft speed (Slow 1.2 s, Normal 0.45 s, Fast 0.1 s between picks, or Instant) until you're on the clock, and **Pause draft** stops it (for example to offer the team on the clock a trade). Trade offers, your own offers, the draft order and your needs open as pop-ups (`popups.open_window`, added for this; needs now show ratings as "Bad (30)").
- **Players:** click a row to select him; click his name or double-click the row for his full report (a pop-up with the scouting buttons, bio, ratings as your staff sees them, combine with ranks, interview; in the startup draft, the player card). Right-click a row (or click its star) to mark him for later, like FOF8; marked rows are tinted blue. Marks are saved with the draft (`league.draft["marks"]`, a list of player ids; technical call: nothing in the engine reads it).
- **Startup draft:** the same board (overall, potential, and the combine ranked against every player at his position in the pool), the clock strip with "Your next pick: #39 (round 2), 12 picks away", the log, and a "Your roster" box with every position's count (red while short, faded when full); a My roster pop-up lists who you have.
- **Results:** the same table look with Round and Team filters and a new Value column (media rank minus pick: + means he went earlier than the media expected, a reach; - a steal); draft-day trades beside it.
- **Fits the window:** the boards fill the space under the green bar whatever the bar's height (it wraps to two rows on narrower windows). At 1366x768 everything fits without scrolling the page (smaller table text, narrower button column, shorter log).
- **PROVISIONAL (for Blake):** (1) the combine color cutoffs (10% / 25% / worst 20%) and FOF8's color meanings, where red is better than blue (the opposite of the rating colors, where blue = Elite); (2) no Fit column (the game has no schemes yet) and no Devl % (Now vs Ceiling shows the same idea; FOF8's single percentage could be added); (3) the Draft speed steps; (4) the Value column in Results; (5) the dark scoreboard strip; (6) past drafts only viewable in the offseason now that the section is hidden.
- **Tested in the app** (port 8105, `.claude/launch.json` "draft-board-test", copies of test saves in this session's scratchpad; 1600x900 and 1366x768): pre-draft offseason (board, selecting, film study narrowing a range and the budget counting down, the report pop-up by name click and by double-click, right-click mark, Begin draft -> draft room, on the clock at #12); mid-draft (trade offers pop-up and accepting one, the offer form's chart check, Continue draft at Normal to my pick, drafting a player, Slow + Pause, draft order and needs pop-ups, Ctrl+S, server restart, reload: same pick on the clock and the mark kept; Have staff finish draft -> Results); mid-season (no Draft section); startup draft (staff pick, Continue draft to my next pick with the up-next order matching the engine's snake, player card by name click, star mark, My roster, drafting a player myself, Have staff finish draft -> camp, no Draft section in the preseason). No server errors. Not tested: Begin draft greyed out during free agency waves 1-4 (no test save in that phase with a class). Before/after screenshots: the side sessions' shared notes folder, `draft-board-shots/`.
- **Found, not mine:** the green bar wraps to two rows at 1600 px wide whenever "Unsaved changes: Save" shows in the offseason (and always at 1366 px), which pushes every page down about 45 px.

### The rest of the screens spruced up (Oct 22; Blake, after seeing the new draft board: "can you upgrade and really spruce up the rest of the ui in any way you think could make it better", and "those boxes at the top right corner definitely need some work, they look like google form boxes")
- **The green bar's buttons (`main_page.py`, `nav.py`):** the main action in the bar (Sim week, Next..., Start the offseason, Go to the draft room) is a yellow button with an icon (play, skip ahead, football); Sim ahead, Scout the draft class and Menu are dark buttons that sit in the bar; Offseason report is text with an icon; "Saved" is a quiet check mark, and unsaved changes show a yellow-outlined Save button (hover says you have unsaved changes; Ctrl+S still works). The bar stays one row at 1600 px wide even when unsaved (the team button's second line is cut off at 210 px with the full text on hover; section buttons slightly tighter). Technical: Quasar's own button colors can't be overridden by page styles, so these buttons are made without a Quasar color (`color=None`) and styled by class (`bar_btn` in `main_page.py`).

- **Main menu as a title screen (`menu_page.py`):** a football field at night (stripes and yard numbers drawn with page styles, no image files), "FRONT OFFICE / Football", big menu buttons (Continue in yellow with the save's details), and up to 4 other saves as cards you click to load.
- **Schedule, league week (`schedule_page.py`):** a scoreboard grid instead of a list: one card per game (team abbreviation, name and record, score, winner bold with a marker, loser greyed, Final/OT, where it's played); your game first, with a yellow edge. Click a played card for its summary, a team name for its window, as before.
- **Home (`home_page.py`):** this week's game as a dark scoreboard (away left, home right, records or seeds, the score once played, your team underlined in yellow); every card's title is a green header band with its link in yellow.
- **Contracts (`contracts_page.py`):** the salary cap line is now a cap strip: big numbers (cap, used, room in green or red, standing offers, dead money, next year committed of the projected cap) and a bar of how full the cap is (contracts, dead money, standing offers, room), with the league money outlook underneath.
- **Watch a game, before kickoff (`game_page.py`):** a tale of the tape for your game: both teams with records, points scored and allowed per game, last 5 results, offense and defense starters' average rating, and the starting QB, the better side of each row in green.
- **PROVISIONAL (for Blake):** all the looks above, especially the title screen, the yellow main-action buttons, and green table headers everywhere (easy to switch back to the light grey headers in `theme.DESKTOP_CSS`).
- **Tested in the app** (port 8105, copies of test saves, 1600x900): title screen and loading from it; mid-season save (Home scoreboard, bar buttons, Roster, Contracts cap strip, Schedule scoreboard for an unplayed and a played week, a card opening its game summary, Standings, Watch a game preview); offseason save (bar with Offseason report / Scout the draft class / Next, unsaved Save button with the bar still one row, the draft window, Stats). No server errors. Not looked at: the playoffs and season-over versions of Home and the bar, the New game screen's table, 1366 px wide.
- **Fixed on the way:** my first schedule cards reused class names from the live game's scoreboard (`.scoreboard`, `.sb-team`) and broke both; renamed to `wk-*`.

### Scouting ranges made honest: the same everywhere, and right at the top of the board (Oct 22; the main session, after checking the new draft board in the app: ranges changed depending on what was opened first, and the top of the board was over-confident, e.g. Jacob Bennett, DE, really 79 now, shown "Elite (95-99)")
- **Same numbers everywhere (`engine/database.py`, `engine/scouting.py`):** a class's hidden notes (draft.py: the truth, the league's shared mistake, how deep in the class he is) were only put back on the prospects once something built the board, so anything that asked before that got default values (Bennett: 72-99 before the board, 95-99 after). Now loading a save puts them back, and scouting puts them back itself if they're ever missing.
- **What was wrong at the top (measured, 6 fresh classes from the main session's 2029 save, nothing scouted):** the old ranges had the truth inside 82% of the time for the whole class (design: about 2 in 3) but only 52% for the top 32 of your staff's board, whose range middles averaged 14.7 shown points too high (35% and +20.8 for prospects shown 70+). That's the winner's curse: the prospects any staff rates highest are mostly ones it overrates, and no range width fixes a 15-point lean without making ranges useless. The 99 cap wasn't the cause (none of the top-100 ranges were capped).
- **The fix (display only; your staff's board order, its picks and every AI board are unchanged):** your staff reads its own view the way a good scouting department does. (1) The middle of the range is pulled toward a typical prospect in this class by how unsure the staff still is (a lot unscouted, little once fully scouted). (2) The width is how unsure it is after that, sized by how deep in the class the STAFF thinks he is, never his true depth (before, a wide range quietly told you a prospect was truly worse). (3) The league-wide part of the width is read 60% of the way toward a typical prospect (`scouting.SHARED_TYPICAL` 0.6), because the prospects a staff rates highest partly come from deeper in the class, where the league's misses are bigger. Skills in the report move with the overall range. Code: `scouting.class_prior`, `_reading`, `remaining_miss(seen_grade, shared_grade)`.
- **Results (truth inside the "now" range / middle minus truth, shown points; old -> new):** nothing scouted: top 32 of your board 52% / +14.7 -> 65% / -3.8; picks 33-100 74% / +10.9 -> 68% / -1.3; the rest 86% -> 77%. Film study only: top 32 71%, 33-100 66%, rest 77%. Film + cross-check: 69%, 63%, 76%. Fully scouted (all four steps): top 32 71% / +2.0 -> 75% / -0.3; 33-100 71% / +2.8 -> 66% / 0.0; rest 76%. Ceiling ranges within 1-4 points of the same. A second league (the Charlotte test save, 4 classes) is a bit wider: top 32 77% unscouted, 80% fully scouted; the rest 81% and 74%. Bennett now: "Very Good (71-97)" now and 74-99 ceiling, with the truth (79/84) inside; staff board #10, media #3. Ranges for top prospects are wider than before when unscouted (median 29 shown points for the top 32 vs 26), and the middles are honest; scouting narrows them to about 8.
- **Left as is:** the rest of the class (past your board's top 100) runs a little wide (75-81% inside); a range whose top would pass 99 shows as "x-99"; a board rank and the range's middle can disagree a little (the board also weighs position value and age).
- **Check tool:** scratchpad `coverage.py <save> <classes> [steps]` and `misses.py` of this session (makes fresh classes from a save, groups by your staff's board rank). The app shows the new ranges (the main session's save, Draft window). The board's rows still build in about 0.07 s.

Main findings: add a home screen (overview layer), shrink the three top bands to one slim bar so tables get the screen (Roster shows ~9 rows at 1600x900 today), group the 12 tabs into ~6 hubs, keep pop-ups for quick details and full pages for ongoing work (FM26's lesson), one team picker, hover explanations, saved table views, sparklines.

### Pop-ups like FOF8 (Oct 21, night; side session; Blake: "can you add a fork for making windows be popups rather than separate screens you click on and it changes. Like how front office football does it")
- **Decision (Blake), reverses the old design rule** ("avoid stacks of floating pop-up windows"). Options shown: (1) in-window pop-ups, (2) real separate OS windows like an old Windows program (several sessions of work: NiceGUI's game window runs pywebview in its own process with no built-in way to open more windows, every window must re-sync after each sim, positions remembered, riskier for the .exe), (3) a mix. **Blake picked the mix:** tabs stay the home base; detail views open as pop-ups over the tab you're on, and closing one puts you right back (same tab, scroll position and filters). **Pop-ups stack** (Blake picked stacking over "one at a time with a Back button", as closer to FOF8): a player clicked inside a pop-up opens his card on top, cascaded down and right; closing it shows the one underneath. Real OS windows remain possible later on top of this. (Unsure: whether FOF8 itself lets several windows sit side by side or makes you close one first.)
- **What opens as a pop-up:** the player card (every player link and "Open player card" button on the game screen: Roster, Contracts, Draft, Stats (team summary links and player-stats rows), Bitter, Awards, box scores, the offseason report), a game's summary (season bar's Game summary, Playoffs, Bitter, the Game tab; before, it jumped to the Schedule tab), and the offseason report. Close with X, Esc, or a click outside; drag a pop-up by its green header (it can't be dragged off screen). Pop-ups block the screen behind them, so nothing sims while one is open, and each is drawn fresh when it opens (never stale). The Schedule tab keeps its own box score panel.
- **Code (screens only, `engine/` untouched):** new `screens/popups.py` (`install`, `open_player`, `open_game`, `open_offseason`, `show_player`; a page-wide click catcher turns any `<a href="/player/ID">` into a card pop-up). `player_card_page.py` split into `card_header`/`card_body`, `offseason_page.py` into `report_header`/`report_body`, `schedule_page.game_box(league, g)` moved to module level, so the pages and the pop-ups share them; `/player/{id}` and `/offseason` still work as pages. Box score names are now player links (`game_page.box_score_html`). `main_page.py`: `popups.install()`, the two Offseason report buttons, `open_summary`. One-line changes in `roster_page.py`, `stats_page.py`, `contracts_page.py`, `draft_page.py` (`popups.show_player`).
- **Tested in the app** (port 8099, `.claude/launch.json` "popups-test", copies of the test saves): player card from the Roster side panel; offseason report, then a player inside it (stacked, cascaded), Esc closes only the top one, X and clicking outside close; dragging and the on-screen limit; Next (the unsigned-players warning, then free agency wave 1) and the redrawn bar's Offseason report button; mid-season save: Game summary -> BOS box -> a receiver's card on top; Stats team summary link and Player stats row click (one card each, no duplicates, page stays on /game); Sim week with pop-ups used before: bar, Game summary and tabs refresh to week 6. No server or console errors.
- **Found, not fixed (engine, the awards session's file):** `engine/awards.py` `honors_summary` joins with a mis-encoded "Â·" instead of "·", so cards show "All-Pro Â· Pro Bowl".
- **For later:** the team summary or another team's roster could open as pop-ups too (Blake picked the mix, not "everything"); no limit on how deep pop-ups stack.

### Single-game extremes: tackles, long field goals, big rushing games (Oct 23; side session. Task from the main session: one-game spikes vs real weekly stats without breaking season realism)
- **What this covers:** `tools/game_extremes_check.py` counts one-game spikes per 17-week season (272 games) vs the real NFL's weekly stats 2021-25. Measured with leagues 101-103 x 2 seasons; each run moves a few counts by chance (12+ tackle games about +/-10). Season realism: `tools/realism_check.py --seasons 4` (16 seasons) after every change.
- **First, a measuring mistake in the tool (fixed):** real combined tackles are solo + "with assist" + assists (three columns in the nflverse files; Foye Oluokun 2021: 88 + 14 + 90 = 192, his official total). The tool left out the middle one, so real 12+ tackle games looked like 99 a season; the true number is **151 (range 140-160)**, and 15+ games **31 (26-35)**, not 17. The season benchmark already counted all three. So the tackle gap was about a fifth too many, not double. (`tools/big_games_real.py` and `tools/real_awards.py`, the news and awards sessions' files, still use two columns: noted for them in the shared notes.) New measures added: 10+ tackle games, 4+ field goal tries, 25+/30+ carry games, and season totals of 50+/55+/60+ yard field goal tries and makes.
- **Change 1, end-of-half field goals** (`game._kick_now`, new `tuning.FG_KICK_NOW_SECONDS` 8). Real (play-by-play 2023-25): on 1st-3rd down teams kick with 0-8 seconds left (~110 a season), almost never with 9-24 (~4). The game kicked as soon as 16-24 seconds were left, even with a timeout, so a team just past its kicker's range tried a 60+ yarder instead of running one more play. Now, with a timeout left, the offense runs another play (then uses the timeout) and kicks in the last 8 seconds. **60+ yard tries 25.5 -> about 18-19 a season** (real 13.6, range 9-22; 2023-25 average 15.3; same-seed tests: 27.7 -> 15.5 and 23.2 -> 18.7). 55+ tries 86 -> ~71-81 (real 83, range 52-114), points per game unchanged (22.8-22.9). Season check: 22 -> 20 of 282 out of range.
- **Change 2, who makes the tackle** (`tuning.TACKLE_POOL`; the line is now split into ends and tackles, `plays._pool_key`). Measured tackles per game at the same share of defensive snaps (real 2023-25 snap counts joined to weekly stats; scratchpad `fulltime.py`). One thing labels had hidden: real 3-4 edge rushers are listed as linebackers, so by role the real front line makes ~17.9 tackles a team game (the game's line 14.7) and off-ball linebackers ~18.9 (the game's 21.8). Before -> real: full-time corners 5.5 -> 4.6 a game, full-time safeties 5.2 -> 6.0, weak-side linebackers on 60-85% of snaps 7.5 (with 38 games of 12+ a season) -> 6.2, defensive tackles on 60-85% of snaps 2.7 -> 3.2.
