The startup draft of a new game starts at pick 1 the same way. The draft runs itself at the speed you choose and stops when you're on the clock; after your pick it carries on at that speed.
- **The draft speed control** (the draft room's right-hand column): Pause / Slow (a pick about every 4 s) / Normal (2 s) / Fast (0.5 s) / To my pick (every pick at once, stops at yours). The big yellow button is "Start draft · Normal" (or "Continue draft") / "Pause draft". The speed you last ran it at is remembered between games and sessions (`saves/settings.json`, `draft_speed`); the room always opens paused. While you're on the clock the control reads "Draft speed after your pick": choose Pause there and the draft stays put after you pick.
- **Each pick, live:** the clock strip (pick, team, "up next" teams, who your staff would take at your next pick, a red "LIVE · Fast" dot or "PAUSED"), the draft log (the newest line flashes yellow; players you marked get a gold star), the board (the player drops off; it stays sorted by your staff's board) and "Your next pick" at the top all update.
- **It pauses by itself:** when you're on the clock (the message says how many teams called with trade offers, e.g. "You're on the clock with pick 118. 3 teams called with trade offers"); when a player you marked is taken, if "Pause when a player I marked is taken" is ticked (on at first; remembered): "A player you marked is gone: Washington Sentinels take WR Kendrick Perry with pick 90. The draft is paused."; when you close the draft room; when you open Offer trade for the team on the clock; when the offseason's Next ("staff finishes the draft") is pressed, which still works mid-run.
- **Same results:** each timer pick is `draft_day.step`, one pass of the loop the straight run uses, with the same dice. Checked: a 2027 draft (from the GM session's integration save) made pick by pick vs straight through: identical, 224 picks and 37 trades; the startup draft: identical over 8 rounds (256 picks) from one league copied. Saving mid-draft and loading resumes at the same pick (as before: after a load the rest of that draft uses the resume dice, unchanged).
- **Can't hang:** an AI pick takes about 6 ms (worst 115 ms), so the timer (`ui.timer`, every 0.1 s) makes picks itself, with no background thread that could clash with saving. A tick never waits on anything slow: finishing the draft or opening camp runs on its own; an error, or a pick that doesn't go through, pauses the draft with a message instead of stopping the timer.
- **Code:** `engine/draft_day.py` (`start` and `startup_begin` no longer pick; without a team of yours, or with no picks of yours, the AI still runs it all at once, so the tools are unchanged; new `sim_until` / `startup_sim_until`: picks one at a time until your pick, a stop rule, or a count); `screens/draft_page.py` (the speed control, the timer, auto-pauses, live strip, log flash and stars; the old Continue loop, Sim to my pick and the speed drop-down are gone); `screens/main_page.py` (closing the room pauses it, Next waits for a pick being made, wording); wording in `engine/season.py`, `engine/offseason.py`, `screens/state.py`; `docs/draft-design.md` D1.
- **Tested in the app** (`.claude/launch.json` "live-draft-test", port 8131, a copy of the GM session's `integ_2027-resign` save taken to the draft): Next opened the room at #1, Miami on the clock, "No picks yet"; Fast through all of round 1 (one pick every ~0.55 s), stopped at #22 with "Trade offers (3)"; drafted T Ian Simpson myself and the draft went on at Fast; mid-round 2 switched to Normal (~2.05 s a pick) and later Slow (~4.1 s); Pause held for 6 s; staff pick at #54 with Pause chosen stayed paused; marked players: paused right after Atlanta took a marked G at #87 and Washington a marked WR at #90, message shown; To my pick: #91 to #118 (27 picks) in 0.24 s; closed the room while running at Normal (#121): reopened 10 s later still at #121, paused; saved, restarted the server, loaded: the room opened by itself at #121 with the same log, paused, "Continue draft · Normal" and the checkbox as left; ran it at Fast and pressed the bar's Next: the staff finished it (224 picks, each pick number and player once, all on their teams and under contract; on to wave 5). New game (startup draft): opens at pick 1 ("Start draft · Fast", remembered), ran by itself to my pick #7, the green bar showed "pick 7: you're on the clock". No server errors from the draft.
- **College beside every name in the draft log (Blake: "on the list that keeps going as the draft goes, should have the college of the player next to their name"):** both draft logs now show the school right after the name in dark text ("Lawrence Porter Florida State · media #10"; startup: "Shawn Moore Notre Dame (74)"). The yearly log had it before but in faint gray after the name; the startup log had none. Checked in the app in both rooms.
- **PROVISIONAL (for Blake):** (1) the speeds (4 / 2 / 0.5 s); (2) the room opens paused, even with a remembered speed; (3) after your pick the draft carries on at your speed (choose Pause while on the clock to stop it); (4) "pause when a marked player is taken" starts ticked; (5) there's no "pause when a team calls about a trade" before you're on the clock, because AI teams only offer when you're on the clock (it already waits then; the message counts the offers).
- **Found, not mine:** `screens/contracts_page.py` errors when the game page is built during free agency with the Contracts page on Free agency and nobody selected (`NameError: offers_box`: `panel()` runs before `offers_box` is defined below it). Seen after reloading the page in wave 5 right after the draft. Also: one 2027 test draft had 37 draft-day trades (the log's target is ~21 a draft); it may be the GM session's work in progress in `engine/draft.py`, just one draft.
### How snappy the screens are, measured; the window opens maximized (Oct 22; Blake: "is there any way to optimize the game as a whole and make it feel snappier and quicker and smoother? maybe higher res")
- **Measured in the app (a 2029 league, browser preview):** switching sections (Home, Team, Schedule, League, Bitter) 0.08-0.12 s; Team pages 0.09 s (Position counts and Contracts 0.4-0.5 s); Bitter right after a sim 0.36 s; Sim week 2.0 s from click to the new week on screen (was 3.2 s before today's engine speedups); Save 0.4 s and Load 0.45 s for a 6-season, 20 MB league. Pop-ups: building them in Python takes 0.004 s (game summary) to 0.06 s (player card); the rest of the 0.3-0.7 s measured was the hidden test browser drawing slowly, so in the real window they should feel close to instant. Sim week already runs in the background (`run.io_bound`), so the screen never freezes.
- **Blake's screen:** 2560x1440 at 144 Hz, Windows scaling 150%, plus a 1152x2048 portrait monitor. Text is drawn by WebView2 at the screen's full sharpness, so "higher res" adds nothing. **Changed (`app.py`):** the game window opens maximized (see the pop-out windows entry for how, and a correction about the screen size); "restore down" gives 1400x820. Remembering the last size and position was left out for now: pywebview's sizes under DPI scaling need checking in a real window first.
- **What's left that's slow is the engine:** a regular-season week about 2 s (the game sim itself about half of it); offseason Next steps 2-7 s (free agency waves about 2 s each; camp includes building next season's schedule, about 4-5 s, a search for a fair schedule; final cuts about 5 s).
### AI general managers (Oct 22; GM side session. Blake: "wouldnt a gm affect alot more than just the draft trade habits?"; he chose all four jobs (draft habits, free agency / re-signing style, cap style, hiring coaches) and visible GMs. Design, real data and every PROVISIONAL choice: `docs/staff-design.md` section 10)
- **Real data first** (`docs/research/gm_research.py` -> `real_gm.json`): who ran each team's draft 2002-2026 (Wikipedia's GM lists, fetched Oct 22), head coaches and records (nflverse games), draft trades (nflverse), contracts (OverTheCap via nflverse, 2011-21), dead money and cap left (Spotrac team cap tables 2013-25), restructure rates (OverTheCap, 2021). GM changes **4.54 a year** (spread 1.84, range 1-8; head coaches 6.9); both in the same offseason 2.6 a year (**57% of GM changes come with a new head coach**, 37% of coach changes come with a new GM); a new GM who keeps the coach replaces him within two seasons 55% of the time; GM tenure mean 4.7, median 4; 11% last one season; 14% of new GMs were GMs before; firing odds by wins x tenure (after his 1st season only 11%). Habits, team odd vs even years | same GM | same team across a GM change: draft trades 0.60 | 0.52 | 0.20; outside free-agent money 0.53 | 0.49 | 0.30; own players' share 0.49 | 0.43 | 0.10 (own money as such 0.08: teams don't differ lastingly in what they pay their own); dead money 0.45 | 0.48 | 0.20; cap room left at season's end 0.88 | 0.62 | 0.57 (an owner/organization trait).
- **Built:** `engine/gm.py` (every AI team's GM: name, age, record, reputation that follows his teams' results, hidden eye for coaches, four habits that are mostly his and partly the organization's (`ORG_SHARE`), firing by the real table, retirements, hiring from GMs out of work, other teams' scouting directors (their job opens for the carousel) and outside assistant GMs; older saves get GMs on load from the salt; your team never has one). Wired in: `offseason.offseason_start` (GM changes before the league year and the carousel), `staff.carousel_start` (a new GM brings his own head coach `NEW_GM_OWN_COACH` 0.55; an inherited coach's odds x1.4; `MARKET_LIFT` 1.18 -> 0.8 so coach changes stay real), `staff._ai_value` (the GM's eye for coaches), `draft.trade_style` (D14: the GM's habit; the head coach no longer counts), `free_agency.py` (knobs marked Oct 22: spenders bid more and chase more players; draft builders set a ceiling that grows with their room; own-player extensions and offers; cap pushers back-load, put more in bonuses, restructure to keep or sign starters, accept more dead money in cuts, with guard rails; clean-books GMs and frugal owners keep more room). News: `engine/news_front_office.py`, a new "Coaches and front offices" kind (GMs and head coaches fired, together when an owner cleans house, retiring, hired), with Bitter insiders' scoops, team welcomes and fans' reactions. Screens: team window **Front office** tab, Staff page **General managers** list and a GM panel for other teams, GM moves in the carousel list. Names from `names.elder` for GMs and coaches (as the name-pool session asked). New tool `tools/gm_check.py` (writes `tools/gm_report.txt`).
- **Results, 12 leagues x 16 seasons (`tools/gm_report.txt`) [real]:** GM changes 4.52 a year [4.54], spread 1.82 [1.84]; head coach changes 7.2 [6.9; 6.6 for 1999-2025]; both together 2.55 [2.58]; GM changes with a new coach 56% [57%], coach changes with a new GM 35% [37%]; kept coach gone within 2 seasons 46% [55%]; GM tenure mean 4.5 [4.7], median 4 [4], one season 14% [11%]; rehired GMs 15% [14%]; firing odds by wins 0-4 44% [35%], 5-6 21% [19%], 7-8 15% [16%], 9-10 9% [11%], 11+ 3% [4%]; playoff teams 5% [4%]. Draft check (22 seasons x 6 leagues): **trade habit lasts 0.60 [0.60]** (0.44 with head coaches only, 0.65 with the draft session's half-organization version), spread 0.66 [0.61], unrelated to drafting skill -0.00 [+0.01]; the GM's habit goes with him to his next team (0.48 in 91 moves [0.33 in 4]).
- **Honest gap (the main open call):** the money habits act (big spenders put 12.4% of the cap a year into other teams' players vs 9.3% for draft builders; money pushers restructure 3.5% vs 2.3%; dead money 11.5% vs 9.9%; GMs who pay their own early 24.5% vs 20.7%), but next to the year-to-year swings they don't make teams' money habits last measurably longer than with them switched off: outside money 0.20 with vs 0.25 without [0.53], own share 0.11 vs 0.11 [0.49], dead money 0.27 vs 0.22 [0.45], cap left 0.73 vs 0.77 [0.88]. Bigger versions got there (outside money 0.43-0.54 when draft builders held back room) but made the roomiest teams after free agency's first wave far roomier than real (34-41% vs 26%) or put AI teams over the cap. A real fix probably needs what makes real habits last: teams that plan cap room across years (and void years for pushers), not bigger knobs.
- **Checks on the final code:** `free_agency_check` 101-103 and 104-106 x 4: cap legality 0 and 0, missing contracts 0; room after the first wave 2nd/5th/8th/median/24th 25.4/18.2/14.7/9.4/5.6% and 25.5/18.9/15.1/9.9/6.3% [target 22.6/16.3/13.0/8.0/3.7] (GM money habits off on the same code: 23.2/18.0/14.5/10.0/6.2); tags 11.2-12.3, extensions 45-48, cap cuts 33-38. `gm_check`: cap legality 0 in 192 league-seasons (a softer guard rail had 1 and an earlier one 2: the most extreme money pushers restructured until half the cap was spread-out bonus and no move could get them under; fixed by `GM_CAP_HELL` / `GM_CAP_HELL_NOW` / `GM_CAP_PUSH_MAX` / `GM_CAP_RESTRUCTURE_MAX`). `news_check --full`: all automatic checks pass, 25 front-office stories in an offseason. `bitter_check` on that league: all targets met (0 repeats in a week; the first version had 5 same-shape repeats in For you from coach-hire stories, fixed with more wording). Integration test (6 seasons, startup draft, the real flow with save/load round trips): 0 problems, GMs identical after every save/load, your team never gets an AI GM, 17-26 front-office stories each offseason (now and then a fired GM is hired elsewhere the same offseason, as Tom Telesco was in January 2024). Realism check not run: no game-sim code changed.
- **In the app** (port 8124, scratchpad saves from the integration test): the Staff page's General managers list (all 32 teams, "You" for yours) and the team window's Front office tab (GM's reputation, record by season, what he's known for; the staff's reputations) work; no server errors.
- **PROVISIONAL for Blake:** showing GMs' habits on screens ("Known for", only clear ones); style sizes; the organization's share of each habit; the owner's link to unspent room; the guard rails; where new GMs come from; no in-season GM firings yet (AI head coaches aren't fired in-season either: `staff.ai_in_season` isn't hooked into the season). Not built: a best-player-vs-need draft lean (real teams show no lasting position preference to size it to).
- **Noticed, not mine:** own players' money runs in a two-year rhythm (odd vs even -0.38 before GMs too; real +0.08), for whoever owns free agency; a 30-year-old first-time head coach in one news check (the staff carousel's last-resort hires).
### Pop-out windows: player cards, teams and game summaries as real windows (Oct 22; Blake: "i want the window popups for stuff to be draggable outside the game window... thats how front office was", then "player cards, team windows and game summaries as real windows I can drag anywhere, even onto my second monitor, with several open at once")
- **How it works (`screens/windows.py`):** the game window is drawn by a second process (NiceGUI native mode, pywebview), and JavaScript can't open windows there (links just reload the same window). So `app.py` calls `windows.install_in_window_process()`, which runs in that process and gives the game window a command, `fof_open_window`, that opens another pywebview window; `windows.pop_out(kind, id)` sends it down the queue NiceGUI uses for its own window commands. Each pop-out is a page of its own (`/w/player/12`, `/w/team/3`, `/w/game/57`) drawn by the same code as the in-window pop-ups (`popups.open_player / open_team / open_game` try `pop_out` first; `popups._popup` draws into the window's page instead of a dialog when it's a pop-out's own page).
- **Behavior:** windows open a little down and right of the last one (FOF8-like compact sizes: player card 1240x860, team 1300x900, game 940x900, logical pixels); titles say who or what ("Lawrence Thompson (CB) · Player card", "Washington Sentinels · Team", "BUF 24, SDG 41 · Game summary"); each is owned by the game window (Windows' owner), so it stays in front of the game when you click the game, like FOF8's windows, can go anywhere on any monitor, and isn't on top of other programs. Links and roster rows inside one open more windows. Every open window is redrawn when the league changes (`state.mark_changed` -> `windows.refresh_all`); all close when the game closes, another save is loaded or you go back to the main menu. Settings has a switch ("Open player cards, teams and game summaries in their own windows", on by default); browser mode always uses the in-window pop-ups. The draft room and the offseason report stay in-window pop-ups.
- **Tested in the real game window** (Blake's 4K screen at 150% scaling): pop-outs opened for a player link on Home, a team link in the standings and a row in a team window's roster; three open at once; dragged like any window; stayed in front after clicking the game; after Sim week the open team window changed from 8-1 to 9-1 by itself; closing the league closed them all; no errors. Browser mode: in-window pop-ups as before.
- **Window size fix found on the way:** pywebview's `maximized=True` at creation scales the size twice under Windows scaling, so the game window is now maximized right after it's shown (`app.native.on("shown", ...)`). Correction to the earlier entry: Blake's main screen is a 4K monitor (PG32UCDM3) at 150%, which Windows reports as 2560x1440, so the old 1600x950 window did fit; maximized just uses the space.
- **Not done yet:** pop-out windows don't remember where you last put them (each kind could reopen where you left it, e.g. on the second monitor); worth adding once Blake has used them.
### Multi-year cap plans for AI teams; Blake's answers on the GMs (Oct 23; GM side session. Blake: "yes known for is a great idea, yes build multi year cap planning to close the money gap ... owners link to leaving cap room unspent (i think chris ballard used to get roasted for holding too much cap money and not spending) ... gm firing should by default be off, but can be enabled in settings. i always had it off in fof8")
- **Decided (Blake):** "Known for" stays on screens. Build multi-year cap planning. Unspent room: his Ballard example says a GM can be the one holding money; real data says the habit mostly outlasts GM changes (0.57 of 0.62), so it's mostly the owner's (70%) and partly the GM's (30%). **You getting fired** (asked to clarify; he meant FOF8's option, not AI GMs): off by default, a setting to turn it on; you can't be fired yet, so the "on" side is still to build. AI GMs keep being hired and fired.
- **Built (`engine/free_agency.py`, knobs marked Oct 22-23, `docs/staff-design.md` section 10):** each AI team's spending level (`spend_level`: 70% owner's budget factor, 30% its GM's free agency and cap styles) sets how full it runs its cap every year (`plan_keep`: frugal teams leave up to 8% unspent; free spenders keep less for in-season moves, never less than an all-in team) and how much of next season it commits now (`plan_next`: a multi-year deal for another team's player must leave next season's commitments under 85% +/-10% of next year's cap, else a one-year offer); the draft builders' ceiling got firmer (`GM_FA_BUDGET` k 0.45 -> 0.9); a spending floor (`PLAN_ROOM_MAX` 25%, after the real league's cash spending floor) after a frugal draft builder in a startup-draft league sat on 70-90% of its cap. Replaced the earlier `GM_CAP_KEEP_*` / `OWNER_KEEP_K` room factors. `tools/gm_check.py` takes experiment knobs (`FA_KNOBS`).
- **Results, 12 leagues x 16 seasons, before the plan -> after [real]:** outside free-agent money's habit 0.20 -> 0.33 [0.53] (same GM 0.11 -> 0.28 [0.49]); own players' share 0.11 -> 0.29 [0.49]; unspent room at season's end 0.73 -> 0.89 [0.88], average 5.1 -> 5.8% [4.8%]; dead money 0.27 -> 0.20 [0.45] (noise about +/-0.1); turnover unchanged (GM changes 4.3, coach 6.9, together 2.35 a year); cap legality 0 in 192 league-seasons. Twelve versions were tried (scratchpad `variants*.txt`); stronger ones reached 0.43-0.45 for outside money but left teams roomier still.
- **Checks on the final code:** `free_agency_check` 101-103 and 104-106 x 4: legality 0 and 0; room after the first wave most 31.4 / 33.0%, 5th 20.5, median 10.1 / 10.8, 24th 6.0 / 6.5 [2022-25 average 26.4 / 16.3 / 8.0 / 3.7; 2025 36.1 / 21.8 / 11.6 / 5.9]: about like 2025, roomier than the average; tags 11-12, extensions 43-46. `draft_check`: trade habit 0.64 [0.60], vs drafting value -0.07 [+0.01]. Integration test (6 seasons from a startup draft): 0 problems; the roomiest team at free agency's opening 68% in year 1 (startup rosters), then 41-54% [real most ~40-45%].
- **Still short:** money habits (about 0.3 vs 0.5), dead money's habit (may need void years, not in the game), teams a little roomier than real. Next candidates: void years for money-pushers; letting draft builders put unspent room into front-loaded extensions.

### FOF8's manual read and compared (Oct 23, overnight; side session. Blake wanted Claude to learn as much as possible about FOF8 and why he liked it; reading the manual from disk was chosen over clicking through the game)
- Notes: `docs/research/fof8-manual-notes.md`. Read from FOF8's own help files (`frfoot8.chm`, `DraftFile.chm`, `PlayerFile.chm`, the `datafile` lists); nothing launched, nothing copied word for word.
- Inside: a Top 10, then each FOF8 system with its rules and numbers (calendar, startup draft and the X-factor re-roll, roster limits, contracts and cap, free agency stages, franchise tag, draft and scouting, ratings, development and retirement, chemistry and cohesion, injuries, depth charts and game plans, coaching staff, owner and finances, trades, season, stats and history, how the sim thinks), each judged against Blake's priorities, then what FOF8 has that this game doesn't, where this game already goes further, and ranked ideas.
- Main findings: the manual explains Blake's exploits (AI drafts and trades by a fixed value per position, TEs among the lowest; trades judged by team strength before vs after with one league scouting view). Biggest missing system: player trades (build them on surplus value). Cheap wins: head coach game settings, playoff odds and point spreads, record books and a Hall of Fame, mentors and playing-time attitude, a free agency fair-price check and a TE draft check in the tools.
- Nothing in the game was changed. All suggestions are for Blake to decide.

### Standing checks against FOF8's two best-known exploits: cheap star free agents, falling tight ends (Oct 23; side session, from the FOF8 manual review's ideas 2-3)
- **Why:** FOF8's AI priced players by fixed position values with one league-wide view, so good free agents could be signed far below their worth and elite TEs lasted past pick 8. The game already prices by the real pay ladder and judges through each staff's eyes; these two checks make sure the exploits can't creep back as other systems change. Both checks now run every time the tools run.
- **Free agency "fair price" check** (new section in `tools/free_agency_check.py`; real data from the new `docs/research/fair_price.py` -> `real_fair_price.json`). Real yardstick, built the same way for the game: a good free agent = last season's starter (real: 65%+ of his team's snaps in the games he played, 8+ games; game: a base-package starter on his depth chart), 30 or younger, signing with a new team; his market value = what real good free agents at his position signed for (OverTheCap contracts 2014-22 linked to nflverse snap counts; 680 deals, about 76 a year). The game also gets its own view: good ratings too (rated a starter league-wide, priced 1%+ of the cap) against his game price the day the wave opened, by wave and by price tier, with how much room the AI teams that passed on him had.
- **Result (leagues 101-103 x 4 offseasons), game [real]:** good free agents signing under 60% of market value 30.1% [34.6%], under 40% 17.1% [22.1%], at about the minimum 15.4% [22.5%]; median deal 2.0% of the cap [1.9%]. **The game is a little firmer than the real NFL, not softer.** Game's own view: 13% of good-rated players sign under 60% of their price, all in the last two waves (after the draft and at camp, when asks drop to their floors); by price: 1-2% of the cap 19% sign cheap, 2-5% 12.5%, stars (5%+) 1.3% (about 3 cases in 12 offseasons). When one signed cheap, about 5 other AI teams could have afforded his full price, but the typical team had only 0.4% of the cap left to spend; the cheapest were fringe starters (overall 56-69, priced 1.1-1.8%) at 40% of their price in wave 7. Real teams also sign good veterans cheaply late in the summer, so this matches. **No engine change.** Cap legality 0, missing contracts 0, room after the first wave unchanged (most 31.4%, median 10.1%, 24th 6.0%).
- **Seen in passing (not the exploit, not changed):** fewer of last season's starters change teams in free agency than in the real NFL (50 a year vs 76), and the game's good movers are paid more than real ones at CB, DT, EDGE, C and TE and less at WR and LB (small samples by position).
- **Tight end draft check** (new section 14 in `tools/draft_check.py`; real data from the new `docs/research/te_draft.py` -> `real_te_draft.json`, nflverse draft picks 2000-2026 and PFR careers; 6 leagues x 22 seasons, sim | real): first TE taken median pick 21 | 21, in the top 10 27% | 22%, in round 1 74% | 78%; TEs per draft in round 1 1.19 | 1.11. By true grade (game only; real teams' grades aren't public): TEs graded in a class's top 8 go at a median pick of 13 (others 9), never after pick 64; TEs graded as first-rounders at 33 (others 24). In hindsight (career value: seasons started + 1.5 per Pro Bowl + 0.25 per season): the class's best TE was picked at a median of 33 | 42, in the top 10 19% | 5%, after round 3 5% | 10%; TEs among a class's top 8 careers were picked at 20 | 63 (other positions 12 | 19). First-round TEs: starter 3+ seasons 85% | 86%, Pro Bowl 50% | 64%. **Elite TEs don't fall; if anything the game finds its best TEs earlier than real teams do** (real TEs are harder to judge: Kelce, Kittle and Andrews went in rounds 3-5). **No engine change.**
- **Startup draft TEs** (new section 15, 10 new leagues, against real contract ranks, `real_startup_value.json`): the 1st / 3rd / 5th / 10th / 20th TE taken at picks 48 / 106 / 154 / 304 / 530 vs real ranks 100 / 130 / 162 / 239 / 318; the top TEs go earlier than real pay says and TE depth later (the known gap). Elite players (2+ spreads above their position's starters) go at a median pick of 66 for TEs vs 67 at other positions. No exploit; no change.
- **Found while building the check (not fixed, outside this task):** a new league isn't exactly reproducible. Somewhere in league creation or the startup draft the order of a set of strings matters (Python shuffles string hashes per process), and a process that already made a league makes the next one differently. The startup check gives each league a fresh process with a fixed hash seed, so its numbers repeat run to run. Across four runs with different hash seeds the conclusions held (1st TE at picks 38-59, elite TEs 62-76 vs others 67-72).

### Player trades (Oct 23; trades side session. Blake: AI teams call with offers at real NFL rates; future picks up to 3 drafts out, valued by how good that team should be, discounted for the wait; the AI answers in words like a phone call, no value meter, and a GM you keep insulting stops listening for a while; the real window, new league year to the deadline after week 9. Design, real data, results and every PROVISIONAL choice: `docs/trade-design.md`)
- **The FOF8 fix:** FOF8's AI traded by a fixed value per position and "team strength before vs after" through one scouting view (cheap elite TEs, quantity for quality). Here every team values **the player** through **its own staff's eyes**: what he adds to its position group over who'd play otherwise (real pay ladders plus a premium for the scarce top of a position), minus the base salary he brings above the minimum (his signing bonus stays behind, the real rule), season by season with his age curve, plus the rights to re-sign a young player; weighed by its timeline (all in / normal / rebuilding; in-season, its playoff chances). Picks on the same scale (the Jimmy Johnson chart the draft room uses); a future pick's value comes from where that team should pick, x 0.42-0.60 a year until that draft (real draft-time deals: a next-year pick counted at 0.36-0.48 of its round's middle). Teams act on half their read of another team's player (the full read made every call land on the player a staff overrated most).
- **Built:** `engine/trades.py` (pick ownership in `league.trades`, saved; values; legality: cap right after the trade by an exact dry run, roster limits, 5 a side, no practice squad or reserve-list players, the window; the money rules: bonus money accelerates onto the old team, the new team takes the base salaries left); AI trades at each step (each offseason phase from the league year on, each preseason week incl. cut-down day, each week up to the deadline): teams with a hole call about other teams' spare players and take the cheapest fix, rebuilding teams auction veterans, no flipping a player just traded for; your calls answered in words (accept, close + what they'd do, counter, not interested / not available, lowball; after 2-4 lowballs a GM stops taking calls for 10 steps; backing out of an agreed deal counts too; they won't sell you a player below 95% of what another AI team would pay); AI calls to you (an inbox, each offer good until the next step) and shopping a player. `engine/news_trades.py` (a "Trades" news kind: a story per trade, a deadline round-up, Bitter scoops, team welcomes and goodbyes, fans, players, rumors when you shop someone). `screens/trade_page.py` (Trades tab: Make a trade, Offers to you, Shop a player, Trade log); Home's "Needs your attention" lists AI calls. Hooks: `offseason.offseason_next`, `season.end_week`, `draft.DraftRoom` (traded picks go to their owners), `free_agency.set_pick_reserve` (the picks a team holds).
- **Real data first** (`docs/research/trade_research.py` -> `real_trades.json`, nflverse trades 2013-25): 59.8 trades with players a league year in 2021-25 (47.2 in 2013-25); by phase, league year to the draft 19.0 (incl. deals agreed after the deadline), draft 1.4, after the draft 2.8, camp and preseason 19.4, season 17.2 (deadline week 9.2); mostly one player for a late pick; median age 26; 46% of traded players under 1% of the cap; stars (8%+ of the cap) 4.5 a year; 82% of traded picks in the next draft.
- **Results** (`tools/trade_check.py`, 6 leagues x 3 league years, `tools/trade_report.txt`) [real]: 51.2 trades a league year [59.8, range 41-67]; by phase 16.1 / 0 / 1.9 / 17.7 / 15.5 [19.0 / 1.4 / 2.8 / 19.4 / 17.2]; deadline week 8.5 [9.2]; per team a year 3.2 [3.0], none 7% [14%]; median age 25 [26]; under 1% of the cap 56% [46%]; stars 4.7 [4.5]; recent first-rounders 5.9 [6.8]; returns for players under 2% / 2-5% / 5-10% of the cap, median chart points 21 / 76 / 269 [14 / 35 / 35]; picks by drafts away 80/15/5% [82/16/2%]; **0 of 921 trades left a team over the cap or its roster limit**; team strength spread 0.237 with trades vs 0.249 with AI trades off (no tilt toward strong teams). **Exploit checks:** asks for top players per 1% of market price, by position, 66-83 (TE 70: no cheap position); three average starters for a star accepted 1 time in ~290 (and good for the AI team by true ratings); dumping a bad contract for a 7th works only when the player fills the other team's hole (10 of 12 accepted were good for it by true ratings); buying low from one AI team to sell high to another profitable 3% of the time (90th percentile -25 points); lowballs: "lowball, lowball, gone".
- **Integration test** (scratchpad `integ_trades.py`: startup-draft league, 4 seasons of real game sims and phase-by-phase offseasons, your team taking AI calls, calling and shopping; save/load at the deadline, free agency and the draft): **0 problems**; 42-59 trades a league year (in-season ~18 a year), every traded pick used by its owner in the draft room, save/load keeps the trade state, 40-60 trade stories and 110-170 Bitter bits a season, 0 wording errors. A deadline step takes 1-2.5 s.
- **Checks before -> after** (AI trades off = the old game): `free_agency_check` 101-103 x 4: cap legality 0 -> 0, room at free agency's opening median 14.4 -> 14.5% [13.2], fair-price measures unchanged, dead money 5.5 -> 6.0% [real roughly 5-10%]. `draft_check` 6 x 22: within noise (R1 starter 3+ seasons 82 -> 83% [78], QB over a good young starter 0% -> 0%, best TE's median pick 32 -> 32, draft-day trade habit 0.64 -> 0.54 [0.60]). Run through a wrapper so the Guards session's report files weren't overwritten.
- **Fixed on the way:** undrafted signings no longer push a team past 90 (`draft.sign_undrafted` signs only what the roster has room for; teams with extra picks, from trades and from draft-day trades before, went over until camp); AI teams over 90 in the offseason cut their least valuable player.
- **Tested in the app** (`.claude/launch.json` "trades-test", port 8143, copies of test saves): the Trades tab in the Team section; a 2nd for a young RB accepted and made (rosters, picks, cap and the confirmation update); "Not allowed" shown before calling (roster limit, the other team's cap); a lowball ("I'm not even going to take that to the owner"; the mood line updates); deadline day ("Today is the trade deadline"), an AI call in the inbox and on Home; shopping a WR (offers with both cap effects); the trade log; trade stories and scoops on Bitter; no server errors.
- **PROVISIONAL for Blake:** the tab's layout; the GM's words and patience (2-4 lowballs, ~4 months); "close" = within 15%; AI teams want 15 points + 8% more from you than from each other; about 6-7 calls to you a season; shopping is unlimited and starts a rumor; a full roster must release before taking on a player; no trades of reserve-list players; the value model's knobs (star premium, backup share, pick discounts, half a staff's read of other teams' players, starters' premium).
- **Not built / follow-ups:** future picks in the draft room's trade builder and in AI draft-day deals; draft-day player trades; conditional picks; stars on 10%+ deals more often go as salary dumps for late picks than real ones (who often fetch a 1st).
- **Found, not caused by trades (for the main session):** AI teams drift over the cap in the later regular season (with AI trades off too: up to ~$10M; week 1 is clean); 1-3 teams over the cap in camp before final cuts.
- **Follow-ups (Oct 23; Blake: "work on the not done yet", and "no trades allowed in startup draft"):** (1) **Next year's picks in the draft room**, for your offers (the form lists them with what they're worth to the other team) and the AI's own draft-day deals (`draft.DraftRoom._package`, `FUTURE_PACKAGE_COST`, `FUTURE_MIN_PRICE`: only to move up into a mid-round-or-better pick; without it late-round trade-ups doubled): 14-21% of draft-day pick deals include one [real 2013-25: 23%]; `draft_check` on the final code: draft-day deals 21.0 a draft [same-year real 21.1], R1 starters 83% [78], QB over a good young starter 0%, best TE's median pick 32 (as before), trade habit 0.62 [0.60]. (2) **Draft-day player trades**: a trade step as the draft opens (`offseason.offseason_next`), 1.3 a year [real 1.4 in 2021-25]; during the draft the Trades tab trades players and later picks. (3) **Stars priced by an auction**: a player on a 10%+ deal goes for about what the second-keenest of several teams would pay; returns now 67% a 1st, median ~1,000 chart points [46%, 608], but such trades are rarer (0.5 a year [1.8]; all 8%+ deals 3.4 [4.5, range 1-10]). (4) **Release to make room**: on a full roster the deal box and each offer let you pick who's cut when the trade goes through. (5) **No trades in the startup draft** (already true: the window is closed and the startup room has no trade offers; the integration test now checks it). Results after (`tools/trade_report.txt`): 52.1 trades a league year [59.8], 0 of 937 trades illegal, exploit checks unchanged (quantity for quality 0 accepted in ~290, buy-low-sell-high profitable 4%, lowballs end in "gone"). Integration test on the final code (4 seasons): **0 problems**, 235 trades, 192 traded picks used by their owners in the draft room, 3 draft-day player trades. `free_agency_check`: cap legality 0; room at free agency's opening moved (median 17.7%) but also with AI trades off on the same code (18.4%): from another session's edits that landed meanwhile, not trades. The main session was told about the in-season cap drift (SendMessage, Oct 23).
- **Line endings:** my patch scripts had turned some LF files into CRLF (database.py, news.py, season.py, draft_day.py, free_agency.py, league.py, main_page.py, nav.py, draft_page.py, launch.json); converted back to LF, so the diff shows only real changes.
### The owner can fire you: off by default, on in settings (Oct 23; GM side session. Blake: "build the 'owner can fire you' setting here: off by default, on in settings, FOF8-style"; earlier: "gm firing should by default be off ... i always had it off in fof8")
- **Built:** `league.settings["owner_can_fire"]` (default off) with a switch on the New game screen and on your Staff page. When on, after each season your owner judges you by the same real odds as the AI GMs (`engine/gm.py` `owner_review`: record, playoffs, seasons in charge; FOF8 weighed finances, which this game doesn't have yet). Staff page shows your job security (Secure / Safe for now / Uneasy / Hot seat / On the way out, with the chance and why) and your own GM record. If you're fired (decided once a season when you press "Start the offseason", kept in the save), up to 3 teams opening their GM job that rank you among their top candidates make offers in a window over the game; you pick one (`take_job`) and move when the offseason starts (old team hires an AI GM; the new team's GM is let go). News stories and Bitter bits for both moves (`news_front_office._your_move`).
- **Tested:** engine script (a real season, a forced firing, an offer, the move, the whole offseason to camp, save/load identical, no AI GM on your new team, one on your old team); in the app (scratchpad save at season's end): the "You've been fired" window with the offer, "Take this job" moves you to the new team and the offseason runs (coaching carousel, your contracts), the Staff page readout and switch (on and off), the New game switch; no server errors. The setting is off by default, so every check and tool run is unaffected.
- **For Blake:** no "retire / leave the league" choice when fired yet; your firing only looks at results (no owner finances); one season out of about seven on average when on.
### AI teams drifting over the cap during the season: found and fixed (Oct 23; the trades session found it, Blake asked the main session to fix it)
- **What was wrong:** with trades off, AI teams went over the cap as the season went on (the trades session's run: 7 teams, 35 team-weeks, worst ~$9.5M over by week 18); the old integration test only checked week 1. Traced move by move (scratchpad `cap_drift.py`: every week, every team over the cap, every contract added or removed and the dead money):
  1. **The top-51 rule ran one week too long:** `contracts.top51_in_force` said yes for the AI moves made right after the week-1 games (the week counter still said 1), so teams signed players by the offseason count and were over once every contract counted. Now it ends when week 1's games are played.
  2. **Forced street free agents were priced like market free agents:** a team with nobody affordable to sign generated a street free agent, and the week's contract writer (`free_agency.sync`) gave him a market deal (up to $8.7M seen), unchecked. Street free agents now sign for the minimum at once (`transactions.fill_roster_spot`).
  3. **A week's signings were checked one at a time:** contracts are written at the end of the week, so a team's second signing was checked as if its first didn't exist. `free_agency.pending_hits` now counts the week's unwritten signings in every affordability check (`affordable`, `AffordCheck`).
  4. **The weekly clean-up couldn't fix small overages** (`free_agency._comply`): it ignored restructures under 0.2% of the cap and cuts saving under 0.1%, so a team $100K over had no move; and it charged a full year of minimum salary for a cut player's replacement, though in-season he's paid only the weeks left. Now the thresholds shrink to the need, and the replacement costs the weeks left.
  5. **Moves made at the cap anyway:** routine game-day elevations, required elevations (a game check), and injury-cover swaps (cutting a vested veteran saves nothing in-season: termination pay) now need room. A team with no room for even a minimum deal may leave up to 2 spots on the 53 open in-season (`transactions.SHORT_AT_CAP`, judgment: real teams often carry an open spot) and uses elevations; it still fills down to 51.
- **Result** (5 seasons, scratchpad `cap_drift.py`, 4 with trades off and 1 on): team-weeks over the cap per season 7-17 -> 1-5, worst overage $12.9M -> $0.8M. `tools/free_agency_check.py`: cap legality 0; room after the first wave most 28.1% / median 8.8% / 24th 6.2% (targets 26.4 / 8.0 / 3.7; unchanged). Integration (3 seasons): 0 problems.
- **Known gap:** a team whose whole payroll is vested veterans on 1-year deals has no way out once a run of injuries forces signings late in a season (nothing to restructure, no cut saves anything); it can end a season up to ~$0.8M over. Real teams avoid this by keeping a cushion; a planning cushion for such rosters is the next step if it matters.

### Combine results with real shapes and real limits (Oct 23; Blake: "i just saw a guy with a 4.14 speed which i dont think is currently physically possible")
- **Cause:** each combine result was the position average plus a bell-curve draw with no outer limit, so elite traits plus a lucky draw could pass anything real (the real 40 record is 4.21, Xavier Worthy 2024; nothing faster since 1999).
- **Fix:** `tools/make_combine.py` builds `engine/combine_data.py` from every real combine 2000-2026 (nflverse, 8,968 players): for each position and drill, the real result at 40 points of the spread, with the ends one step past the real best and worst (a new record can happen, by a hair). `player._combine_value` maps each player's standing (the same z as before: his athletic trait plus luck) through that real curve. Rare position-drill pairs (under 25 real results, e.g. a long snapper's bench) keep the old formula held inside the all-time records.
- **Check** (8,000 generated players per position vs real): every position's minimum, 1%, median, 99% and maximum match within a hair for the 40, vertical and bench (WR 40: game 4.20 / 4.28 / 4.50 / 4.76 / 4.86, real 4.21 / 4.28 / 4.50 / 4.76 / 4.85). Players already in a save keep their numbers; new players and draft classes use the real shapes.
### Pop-out windows open at fixed sizes where their content fits; the player card in three columns (Oct 23; Blake: "player card windows when opening them i think should open at fixed sizes so that everything fits in them organized and neat ... all pop out windows actually", "i just had to drag the window bigger on a player card to be able to read everything")
- **Measured** (content sizes in the browser at candidate window sizes): at the old 1240x860 the player card's width fit but its content was 1,631 px tall (a 4-season QB): the left column held bio, ratings, combine and personality. **Player card now in three columns** (`player_card_page.card_body`, `roster_page.report_html(part=...)`): bio and ratings by season | ratings, combine and personality | awards and career stats; columns wrap in a narrow window (e.g. on the portrait monitor). At 1440 wide: columns 833 / 938 / 1,116 px tall, no sideways overflow.
- **Sizes** (`windows.SIZES`, logical pixels): player card 1440x1280, team window 1440x1240 (its roster and schedule are long lists that scroll), game summary 940x1100 (a box score scrolls). Each opens at its size, never bigger than the main screen and placed on it (`webview.screens`); still resizable. Real-window check (4K at 150%): 2160x1920, 2160x1860 and 1410x1650 physical = the planned sizes.

### Every league its own dice; free agency measured on the real offseason and tuned (Oct 23-24; side session. Blake: "can you fix the engine like you mention being a separate job, also tuning things to be more accurate and real feeling when playing"; then "wait dont we want new leagues differing between runs? ... if not, everything would feel scripted")
- **New games still differ every time** (New game calls `create_league` without a seed). What changed: a given seed now always makes the same league (`league.create_league` restarts player ids at 1; a counter left from an earlier league in the same run changed every id-keyed draw), so the check tools compare like with like.
- **Dice that ignored which league they belonged to, now salted with the league's own dice** (Blake's "scripted" point): every game's seed was only the season and game id, so every save drew the same dice and the same weather for the same game slot (`season.new_game`); and since ids restart at 1 in every league, player #57 got the same hidden aging profile and durability draw in every save (`development.aging_profile`, `player.fill_new_ratings`, now keyed by id, name and birth date). Also: playoff tiebreak coin, Glory Bowl site, draft-order ties, captain votes, practice-squad elevations, coaching-carousel taste and views, GM candidates from scouting, in-season contract terms, the fallback coordinator styles. Within one save everything still plays out the same if replayed. Side effect: players in an existing save get their hidden aging profile redrawn once (durability is stored, unchanged). Not changed: news and Bitter accounts keyed by team (every save has the same beat writer per team; cosmetic, the news session's call).
- **`tools/free_agency_check.py` now runs the game's own offseason** (`offseason.offseason_start/next` phase by phase, as the Next button plays it, then camp and `transactions.final_cuts` after the preseason); its old copy still cut to 53 in the spring, so its "out of the league" numbers measured a schedule the game no longer plays. A new league starts with the cut to 53. Section C added to the fair-price check: good players still unsigned when camp opens (the late-bargain window for you).
- **Real data added:** `docs/research/deal_kinds.py` -> `real_deal_kinds.json`: real market deals (1%+ of the cap) a season, OverTheCap 2016-21: 148 with a new team, 102 re-signings, 50 extensions.
- **What the real-path measurement showed** (3 leagues x 4 offseasons): room after free agency's first wave too high (median 12.0% vs 8.0), new-team market deals 85 a year vs 148, about 120 market-level free agents still unsigned at camp, and good players sitting out. Causes found, each fixed where it starts (engine/free_agency.py unless noted):
  1. Each team sent its few offers to the same top players; the losers sat out the rest of the wave. Now `WAVE_ROUNDS` 3: a team that lost a target bids on its next one in the same wave, as real teams do within hours.
  2. Bids were checked at 90% of the player's ask, but the winner paid up to the next bidder's figure; when that didn't fit, the deal fell through (about 97 a year). Now no team bids more than it can fit (`_keep_k`, `fits`).
  3. Camp invitations went only to free agents priced at the minimum, so a veteran priced a little above it who went unsigned was never invited (made-up street free agents were) and often left the league. Now by what he'd ask once camp opens (`transactions.camp_pool`).
  4. **Teams make room (Blake's choice):** a team that can't afford a free agent who'd start for it releases a veteran at his position he's clearly better than (`MAKE_ROOM_GAIN` 5 rating points, as its staff sees them) when that saves money under the cap-cut rule (`_room_cut`, `_cut_to_fit`).
  5. **Prices drop too (Blake's second choice):** in the summer and camp waves (`SUMMER_WAVES` 6, 7) an unsigned starter-caliber player lowers his ask to the best offer a team that would start him can actually fit, counting a make-room release and its GM's budget (`summer_asks`). Worked out as the wave becomes current, so your screens show the same ask the AI sees; saved in `league.fa`, carried by `cap_market.market_table`.
- **Results, 3 leagues x 4 offseasons, today's real-path baseline -> now [real]:** room after the first wave: most 38.4 -> 31.3% [target 26.4], 5th 23.3 -> 15.6 [16.3], median 12.0 -> 8.0 [8.0], 24th 7.2 -> 5.5 [3.7], teams under 2% 0.8 -> 1.1 a year [5.8]. Fair price (good free agents changing teams): under 60% of market value 28.5 -> 34.5% [34.6], under 40% 18.3 -> 22.2 [22.1], about the minimum 16.7 -> 20.2 [22.5], 62 -> 78 a year [76]. Good-rated players 30 or younger still unsigned at camp 69 -> 50 a year, star-priced ones 9.3 -> 5.9. Out of the league at week 1: all 16.8 -> 15.3% [10.5], 3-4 seasons 20.2 -> 16.5 [8.9], 5-7 19.9 -> 17.8 [14.0], 8+ 30.4 [28.3]. Market deals 264 -> 279 a year [~300 deduplicated]. Cap legality 0, missing contracts 0. Your own offers still work (tested).
- **Speed:** all seven free agency Next clicks in an offseason 13.6 s -> 16.1 s (one-league test; each staff's view of each player is remembered for the wave, `team_score(memo=)`).
- **Tried, not kept:** letting more market-level players test free agency (`TEST_MARKET` 0.55 -> 0.70 / 0.85): re-signings fell to the real 100 a year and new-team deals rose to 115, but cap room when free agency opens rose further above real; more rounds or more first-wave offers (no change: money, not chances, was the limit); lower ask floors alone (no change).
- **Checks after:** realism check (16 seasons) league averages within about 2% except rushing TDs +6% (as before) and interceptions -6%; the leaderboard flags moved around, partly because another session's quarterback sim tuning (`engine/sim/tuning.py`, QB knack and season form) was in progress during the run. Draft check: tight end checks still pass (first TE pick 20 | 21; startup elite TEs at pick 63 vs 72 for other elite players); on a 53 again after a 3rd season 64 -> 70% [74]; players with 9+ seasons 12 -> 14% [9] in the draft harness (which runs without contracts, so its free agency isn't the real one).
- **Known gaps:** new-team market deals still short (39% of market deals vs 49%; re-signings ~127 a year vs 102); the low end of cap room (near-broke teams 1 a year vs 5.8); players with 3-7 seasons still leave the league more than real (early retirements ~5% a year and camp cuts); about 6 star-priced free agents a year still unsigned at camp, because the only teams with room are frugal rebuilders whose GMs won't spend (Blake chose not to loosen them).
### QBs: the top end brought to real, and a hidden career knack (Oct 23; Blake: "make sure quarter backs are not over performing, on the league leaders page im seeing the top 5 with 113 or better rating ... I remember in FOF8 a really highly rated quarterback would just suck sometimes ... they just played bad their whole career despite being high rated"; he chose a hidden knack, "QBs mostly")
- **Measured first.** Real NFL 2015-24 (nflverse, qualifiers 14 att/game): passer rating #1 113.7, #5 103.1, #10 98.0 on average. Game (9 full seasons from integration saves): 119.1 / 106.8 / 100.2. The top QBs completed too many passes (best 72.6% vs real 69.1%) and threw too few interceptions (1.09% vs 1.41%); yards per attempt and TD rate were real. The realism check had no rate leaderboards, so this slipped through: **new measures** (`tools/measures.py`): passer rating #1/#3/#5/#10/#20, completion % #1/#3/#5/#10, yards per attempt, TD % and lowest INT % among qualifiers; real benchmarks rebuilt (existing measures unchanged). `tools/consistency_test.py` also reports a QB's passer rating change season to season (real 8.1 points) and the correlation of a QB's overall with his passer rating.
- **Hidden knack and season form** (`engine/sim/gameteam.hidden_form`, `tuning.KNACK_SD`, `SEASON_FORM_SD`): rating points added to everything a QB does, a career-long knack (never shown, from his own dice: league salt and player id) plus a good or bad year (new each season); other positions 0 for now. Test tools give each replay its own season form (`simlab.play_season` sets `gameteam.SEASON_KEY`).
- **Fitted** (7 variants, each a 16-season realism run plus a consistency test; scratchpad `qb_variant.py`): `KNACK_SD` QB 3.5, `SEASON_FORM_SD` QB 4.5, `COMP_ACCURACY_K` 0.28 -> 0.15, `INT_SKILL_K` 0.27 -> 0.15, then `tools/recenter.py --write`, then `INT_SCALE` 1.00 -> 1.035 (league INTs had dropped to -6%; now -1.8%).
- **Results:** realism (16 seasons): passer rating #1/#3/#5/#10 115.5 / 106.2 / 102.5 / 98.1 (real 112.7 / 105.0 / 102.2 / 98.4; before 117.4 / 109.5 / 106.1 / 99.5); completion #1 71.9% (71.1; before 73.1). Living leagues (2 x 5 seasons): a QB's passer rating carries over season to season at correlation 0.34 (real 0.40; before 0.47), and his overall predicts his passer rating at 0.46 (before 0.65): a highly rated QB can be ordinary for years, a modest one can overachieve. Team-sport test: the best QB (91) with an average cast 11.6 wins, a below-average one (64) 9.5. Integration (3 seasons): 0 problems. Out of range 29 of 303 (before 24; the added ones are kicker/data-label caveats and measures at a range edge).
- **Side effects / known gaps:** 4,500-yard passing seasons 2.2 a year (real 3.8; before 2.6); the very safest QB still throws slightly too few INTs (0.7% vs 0.9%). Staffs and AI teams can't see the knack (it's hidden); benching a QB who plays badly comes with the planned coaching work.
- **Also fixed:** saving over a file that isn't a readable save (empty or damaged) crashed; it's now simply replaced (`database.save_league`).
